# Facts: extend arm

Harvested 2026-09-15 from docs/extend/*, tightened the same day. Agent-facing; never shipped; not register-graded. Every fact carries a source. Format: one bullet per fact, then `Source:` then a status tag.

## docs/extend/add-a-custom-admin-screen.md

- `f:03zj56` A custom route under `src/routes/admin/` wins over the `[...path]` catch-all automatically because SvelteKit resolves the more specific route first. Source: SvelteKit routing precedence. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:2sd4if` `createAuthGuard()` gates the whole `/admin` subtree before any load runs, but does not decide per-route authorization; that is `requireAccess`/`createSectionAction`'s job. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/` guard plus `requireAccess`/`section-action.ts` split. [verified]
- `f:3j02dk` `createSectionAction<Env, Db>({ resolveDb })` builds a reusable per-section action wrapper; `resolveDb` reads the site's own binding off the Worker env the engine hands it, which the engine takes from `cloudflare:workers`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts`. [verified]
- `f:10ojk8` `requireAccess` in `load` and `createSectionAction` in every action share one fail-closed predicate: a session the access map does not admit is refused on both the read and the write. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts` (rate-limit and access-map-not-attached branches reference the same guard). [verified]
- `f:3lbdl6` A role or session with no rule declared for a route's path gets a 403 from the access map, owner included, since `hasAccessRule` carries no owner exemption. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#requireAccess` (`if (!hasAccessRule(access, resolvedTarget) || !canReach(...))`). [verified]
- `f:lblh3u` `createSectionAction` also runs the audit and authentication work `createAdminAction` does underneath it (editor identity, CSRF, one form read); a section built on `createSectionAction` never calls `createAdminAction` directly. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts`. [verified]
- `f:7ik6ng` `@glw907/cairn-cms/admin-toolkit` exports `PageHeader`, `AdminTable`, `ListToolbar`, `ToolbarDisclosure`, `Pagination`, `StatusChip`, `EmptyState`, `FieldLabel`, `ExpandableRow`, `MediaPicker`, and `Tooltip` (plus the `format.js` helpers) as general-purpose scaffolding primitives. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/index.ts` (full export list). [verified]
- `f:8x0qpa` `AdminTable` sets its own horizontal scroll; a wrapping `<div>` should carry `overflow-hidden card-shell card-shadow` but not `overflow-x-auto`. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/AdminTable.svelte:189` (`.toolkit-admin-table-wrap { overflow-x: auto; }`), `card-shell`/`card-shadow` per `docs/internal/admin-design-system.md:354`. [verified]
- `f:mfa5vi` The admin-toolkit's charter is general-purpose primitives only; a component rendering one of cairn's own content concepts (a `ConceptList` row, an `EditPage` field) does not belong there. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/index.ts` module comment ("each export's contract stays general-purpose, never a domain assumption from its first consumer"). [verified]
- `f:68h31z` `ctx.audit()` needs an `AuditSink` wired via `event.locals.cairnAuditSink`, set in `hooks.server.ts` from a bound D1 database (typically via `createD1AuditSink`). Source: `src/lib/ambient.ts:47` (`cairnAuditSink?: AdminActionAuditSink`), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:266` (`event.locals.cairnAuditSink?.(full)`), `src/lib/sveltekit/audit-sink.ts:93` (`createD1AuditSink`). [verified]
- `f:rurhey` `hooks.server.ts` holds exactly one `handle` export; several `Handle` functions compose through `sequence()`, which runs them in order. Source: SvelteKit hooks documentation, `sequence` from `@sveltejs/kit/hooks`. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:ff3l1u` The `createD1AuditSink` sink is fail-open: it returns before the insert settles, so a persist failure never fails the action being audited; a rejected write logs rather than disappearing. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/audit-sink.ts:66` (doc comment: "The sink is fail-open end to end: it returns synchronously, before the insert settles..."). [verified]
- `f:da6d2z` `createSectionAction` audits every refusal its own checks make, not only a successful action, so an unrated action fills an audit table cheaply without a `rateLimit` option; the one exception among its own checks is the rate-limit-exceeded refusal, which returns `fail(429)` with no `ctx.audit` call since back-pressure is not a domain-state change. The session and CSRF refusals `createAdminAction` makes underneath it redirect or throw before any audit call, so they leave no audit record. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:16-17` (module comment) and its `ctx.audit(...)` calls at every other refusal branch (access-map-not-attached, no-access-rule, ownerOnly/canReach, db-not-bound); `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:208-210,233-239` (`admin.action.session_absent` redirect and `admin.action.csrf_refused` throw, both before `ctx` exists). [verified]
- `f:6vy0ka` The `ctx.audit` a `createSectionAction` handler receives seeds the record's `action` and `entity` from the wrapper's options unless the handler's own `ctx.audit` call passes either, so an audit row names the wrapper's `action` and `entity` by default. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:208-209` (`action: record.action ?? opts.action, entity: record.entity ?? opts.entity`), `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:310` (`ctx: { ...ctx, audit: sectionAudit, db: resolvedDb }`). [verified]
- `f:9fwsz5` In the dialog-form recipe, `enhance`'s `'error'` result must skip `update()`, because `update()` calls `applyAction(result)`, which renders the nearest `+error` page for an `'error'` result and would destroy the dialog. Source: SvelteKit `enhance`/`applyAction` behavior. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:cdllbv` `reset` (an `update()` option) is consulted only on a `'success'` result; SvelteKit never resets a `<form>`'s values after `'failure'`. Source: SvelteKit `enhance` behavior. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:lmtfkt` A route nested under an already-gated `/admin` page inherits the auth guard (covers the whole subtree) but never inherits a page's own `requireAccess` rule, which is scoped to that page's route id only; a detail endpoint needs its own `requireAccess` call and its own access-map entry. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:457-486` (`requireAccess`: `target` defaults to `event.route.id`, resolved per route; each route must call it explicitly, so a nested route's own rule is never inherited from a parent's call), consistent with `f:2sd4if`'s already-verified guard/requireAccess split. [verified]
- `f:g7zuji` If the root layout renders visible chrome around `/admin` (header, footer, width cap), the admin shell cannot fill the viewport; a dev-only ancestor-chain check (`detectChromeWrap`) catches the width-cap case only, an ancestor between the admin root and `<body>` whose computed `max-width` is not `none`, `100%`, or `100vw`, and logs one console error pointing at the route-structure doc (a header or footer beside the admin appears only as context inside that message), but only in dev and it never throws or changes rendering. Source: `src/lib/admin/chrome-guard.ts:17,31-64` (`detectChromeWrap`, `warnIfChromeWrapped`). [verified]
- `f:9oa6sk` A custom admin screen's plain daisyUI classes render cairn's corner ladder, since both admin themes set daisyUI's radius tokens: `--radius-selector` 0.25rem for chips and small markers, `--radius-field` 0.375rem for controls, `--radius-box` 0.5rem for cards and dialogs. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:122-124,383-385`. [verified]
- `f:9rxw92` Every daisyUI component and utility class except calendar compiles into the admin sheet, generated at build time from the installed daisyUI package, so a custom admin screen can use any daisyUI class without a safelist entry. Source: `scripts/build/admin-css.input.css:13,93`, `scripts/build/daisyui-classes.mjs`. [verified]
- `f:gc0hx3` A bare `btn` with no color, style, selected, or disabled form renders as a hairline button (a quiet `base-content` edge on the base fill), set in the `cairn-idiom` sublayer. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:954-994`. [verified]
- `f:5stbq2` A `btn` in any of five selected forms, `.btn-active`, `aria-pressed="true"`, `aria-checked="true"`, a non-empty non-false `aria-current`, or `:checked`, renders as the selected segment (a neutral wash, a hairline, weight 600); `btn-link`, color variants, `btn-outline`, and `btn-dash` keep their own look. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:1189-1304`. [verified]
- `f:2babfl` A fixed Tailwind radius (`rounded-lg`, `rounded-xl`) still compiles in the admin sheet but does not follow the corner ladder; markup that should follow it writes `rounded-selector`, `rounded-field`, or `rounded-box`. Source: `scripts/build/admin-css.input.css:73-81`, `docs/internal/admin-design-system.md:317-329`. [verified]
- `f:39sn8c` `cairn-audit`'s ratified norms moved with the corner ladder and the size step: the `button-ghost`, `button-primary`, `input-text`, and `select` radius bands are 6px (were 10px), `card` is 8px (was 16px), `status-chip` is 4px (was 8px), and the control height bands grew with `--size-field` 0.28125rem. Source: `src/lib/audit/norms.ts#RATIFIED_NORMS`, `src/lib/audit/norms-manifest.json`, `docs/superpowers/research/2026-09-26-theme-identity-pass-a-proof.md:127-152`. [verified]
- `f:bwn0uo` `StatusChip` renders a chip on `--radius-selector` (0.25rem in the admin themes), not a fully rounded pill, because daisyUI's `badge` reads that token. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/StatusChip.svelte:12`, `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:122`. [verified]
- `f:x8rhdh` `ListToolbar`'s controls share one height derived from `--size-field`, and a segmented group that does not fit scrolls horizontally inside its own box instead of wrapping an option onto a second row. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ListToolbar.svelte:490-509`. [verified]
- `f:54sgmg` `cairn-audit`'s `chip-ground-collision` rule counts an element as a chip when it carries `.badge`, or when every corner equals the resolved `--radius-selector` (it used to test for a pill shape); a `rounded-full` chip without `.badge` is not detected, a page that declares no `--radius-selector` detects `.badge` chips only, and `button`, `input`, `select`, `textarea`, and `a` elements, the daisyUI control classes, and `role="button"` are skipped. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/rendered/chip-ground-collision.ts:207-243,307`. [verified]
- `f:b8rkq9` The single-mount admin is two pairs of files: `src/routes/admin/+layout.server.ts` (exports the shell load) with `+layout.svelte` (renders `CairnAdminShell`), and the `src/routes/admin/[...path]/` catch-all with its `+page.server.ts` and `+page.svelte`. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.server.ts:6`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.svelte:23`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/[...path]/+page.svelte:1-29`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/[...path]/+page.server.ts:1-10`. [verified]
- `f:brfitv` Every `/admin/**` route, the catch-all view page and any custom route a site adds under `src/routes/admin/`, renders as `CairnAdminShell`'s children through the shared layout, so a custom screen gets the shell chrome (nav, user, theme) with no wrapper code and no registration. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.svelte:3-5,23`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.server.ts:1-3`. [verified]
- `f:esp93u` `createSectionAction<Env, Db>`'s `Env` is a type parameter the site supplies for its own platform bindings; the engine ships no `Env` type for them, and `resolveDb` receives `Env | undefined`. The showcase's signups screen passes the generated `Env` from `worker-configuration.d.ts`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:37-39`, `examples/showcase/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.server.ts:60-62`. [verified]
- `f:hroqbu` `createSectionAction` requires a `resolveDb` binding resolver; a site that wants only the CSRF-plus-audit contract with no database binding stays on `createAdminAction` itself. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:195-200`, `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:37-39`. [verified]
- `f:clyg9r` Each admin-toolkit primitive has one job: `PageHeader` the eyebrow, title, and meta band; `AdminTable` the table chrome around caller-written header and row markup; `ListToolbar` the search and filter band; `ToolbarDisclosure` a trigger that opens a floating panel; `Pagination` page navigation; `StatusChip` a status chip; `EmptyState` the centered empty state; `FieldLabel` a control's label wrapper; `ExpandableRow` a summary row with an expand-in-place panel; `MediaPicker` a picker over the committed media library. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/index.ts:1-20`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/PageHeader.svelte:3-5`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/AdminTable.svelte:9-12`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ListToolbar.svelte:3-6`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ToolbarDisclosure.svelte:3-7`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/Pagination.svelte:3-4`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/EmptyState.svelte:3-5`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/FieldLabel.svelte:3-5`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ExpandableRow.svelte:2-7`. [verified]
- `f:zpi2ux` The compiled admin stylesheet scans only the engine's own admin components and `src/lib/admin-toolkit/` (automatic source detection is off), so every toolkit primitive's classes ship pre-compiled in it and a site's own route markup never feeds that build. Source: `scripts/build/admin-css.input.css:5-9,14,22`. [verified]
- `f:asujoi` A primitive graduates into the admin toolkit from a consumer site's own screens once it has a second real consumer; `ExpandableRow` graduated on that trigger. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ExpandableRow.svelte:2-4`. [verified]
- `f:xgy3iu` SvelteKit's `enhance` hands its callback the server's action result as one of four types (`'success'`, `'failure'`, `'redirect'`, `'error'`); the default `update()` resets the form and runs `refreshAll()` on `'success'` only, then calls `applyAction` for a same-page result, or always for `'redirect'` and `'error'`. Source: SvelteKit `enhance` default callback (kit 3.0.0, `fallback_callback` in `runtime/app/forms/client.js`). [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:ho6dxa` A native `<dialog>` opened with `showModal()` closes on Escape without script; a `<form method="dialog">` closes it on submit but is its own form element, and nested `<form>` elements are invalid HTML. Source: https://html.spec.whatwg.org/multipage/interactive-elements.html#the-dialog-element. [external: HTML]
- `f:tkqoia` A live region (`role="alert"`, `role="status"`) announces content changes made after it is in the DOM, so a region inserted already holding its text is announced unreliably and should be mounted empty first; `role="status"` is implicitly polite and `role="alert"` implicitly assertive. Source: https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#alert. [external: WAI-ARIA]
- `f:vao0dd` `ExpandableRow` renders the caller's summary cells plus one trailing trigger cell of its own, and the panel row's single `<td>` spans the `colspan` the caller passes, which must count that trigger cell; the panel snippet receives the row's `datum`. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ExpandableRow.svelte:88-93,133-135`. [verified]
- `f:b5mcea` A SvelteKit `load` that returns an unawaited promise streams it, but the promise starts running when `load` creates it, so streaming defers when the result reaches the page and not whether the work runs. Source: SvelteKit `load` documentation, streaming with promises. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:s5pdrz` No shipped example wires `createSectionAction` yet. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.server.ts:13,60-62` (the showcase's signups screen imports it and builds its section action from it). [rejected: the showcase's custom admin screen already wires it]
- `f:4xrx5f` `createAuthGuard(config)` takes an optional `roles` (the declared role vocabulary) and `access` (the declared access map); called with no argument it resolves every session against the implicit owner/editor pair. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:33-49,169`. [verified]
- `f:2tqkv9` cairn's own admin screens are audited the way a custom `createSectionAction` route is. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:279`, `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:4-5`. [rejected: only `createAdminAction`, and `createSectionAction` built on it, forwards records to `event.locals.cairnAuditSink`; no engine content, roster, or settings route calls either, so cairn's own screens write no audit records]
- `f:5t1i7o` The admin-toolkit is the general-purpose primitive set a site's own `/admin` screen composes instead of hand-rolling a bespoke list, table, or field, and cairn's own admin screens compose it too: `ManageEditors` builds from its `PageHeader` and `AdminTable`. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/index.ts:1-3`, `src/lib/admin/ManageEditors.svelte:18`. [verified]
- `f:7m5o61` The engine's packaged admin sheet is compiled from cairn's own admin sources and never scans a site's markup, and each toolkit primitive ships its classes, compiled into that sheet, and its own scoped styles with the package, so a later release still renders the primitive under a site's route with no change on the site's side. Source: `docs/reference/admin-toolkit.md:21-27`, `scripts/build/admin-css.input.css:5-9`. [verified]
- `f:chsstm` `createSectionAction` is the guarded action factory a site's admin section would otherwise hand-roll: SvelteKit dispatches a matched form action directly and never re-runs an ancestor layout's `load`, so a section's POST cannot rely on its page's authorization check, and a hand-rolled action that skips the access-map check and the audit call admits any signed-in session to the write and records nothing. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:1-11`. [verified]
- `f:pyt58u` The showcase's signups screen is the worked custom admin screen: its `load` calls `requireAccess`, and each form action is a `createSectionAction` wrapper whose handler receives `{ form, ctx }`, writes through `ctx.db`, calls `ctx.audit` with a `detail` or an `entityId`, and passes `{ action, entity }` as its second argument, with `ownerOnly: true` added on the destructive `remove`. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.server.ts:40-41,60-62,64-89`. [verified]
- `f:6quvqm` Handles composed with `sequence` run their code before `resolve` in order, ahead of the route, so a handle that only sets a `locals` field the route reads, such as the audit-sink wiring, needs nothing an earlier handle sets and works placed after `createAuthGuard`. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/@sveltejs-kit-hooks#sequence. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:jra92k` A form inside a native `<dialog>` submitted with `enhance` survives every result when its callback shows a `'failure'` message in the dialog and then calls `update()`, which for a same-page failure only updates `form` and the page status; reports an `'error'` in the dialog without calling `update()`; closes the dialog before awaiting `update()` on `'success'`, so it never sits open through `refreshAll()`; and hands `'redirect'` to `update()`. Such a callback can leave `reset` unset, since `reset` applies only on `'success'`, when the dialog is closing anyway. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/form-actions#Progressive-enhancement-Customizing-use:enhance. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:tskfz1` An element with the `dialog` role must have an accessible name; `aria-labelledby` pointing at the dialog's own heading supplies one that does not depend on its body text. Source: https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#dialog. [external: WAI-ARIA]
- `f:5vn194` In a dialog form's failure wiring, Tailwind's `sr-only` hides the empty, always-mounted `role="alert"` paragraph visually while keeping it in the accessibility tree; the input's `aria-describedby` names that paragraph as its description and `aria-invalid="true"` marks the failed value, so the error is read with its field when focus moves to the input; and a submit button disabled while the request is pending cannot fire the action a second time. Source: https://tailwindcss.com/docs/display#screen-reader-only, https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#aria-describedby, https://www.w3.org/TR/wai-aria-1.2/#aria-invalid. [external: WAI-ARIA and Tailwind CSS, reading the description on focus is common screen-reader behavior rather than spec text]
- `f:n2bhjw` `ExpandableRow`'s trailing trigger cell has no visible column heading, so the caller's `header` snippet matches it with a `<th scope="col">` whose text sits in an `sr-only` span, the pattern the showcase's signups table uses for its actions column. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/ExpandableRow.svelte:88-93`, `examples/showcase/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.svelte:106`. [verified]
- `f:uy7vyc` A page's `load` runs each time a reader navigates to the page, so a list load that also fetches every row's detail pays for all of them on every visit; fetching a row's detail when its panel opens, and caching it per row, costs only the rows a reader opens. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/load#Rerunning-load-functions. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:od9mww` `fetch` resolves on an HTTP error status and rejects only on a network failure, and `Response.json()` rejects on a body that is not JSON, such as an HTML page, so a lazy-detail fetch checks `response.ok` and parses inside one `try`, caching a row's detail only after both succeed and leaving a failed row retryable. Source: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/fetch, https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Response/json. [external: Fetch]
- `f:hafpqf` `SectionActionConfig.rateLimit` takes three members: `resolve`, which receives the platform `Env | undefined` and returns the limiter binding (any object whose `limit({ key })` resolves to `{ success }`, the `RateLimitLike` shape); `key`, which receives the handler's `AdminActionContext` and returns the limiter key string; and an optional `message`, the 429 copy, which defaults to `Too many requests. Wait a moment and try again.`. The limit runs before the access-map checks; an unresolved binding, or a `key()` or `limit()` call that throws, degrades to open and logs, except that a thrown SvelteKit `redirect()` or `error()` propagates. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:41-45,229-283`, `src/lib/cloudflare/rate-limit.ts:13-15`. [verified]
- `f:bcybve` `AdminActionOptions.access` takes a required `target`, the access-map key the check matches (never a request pathname), and an optional `ownerOnly`; `access.target` has no default, where `SectionActionOptions.target` defaults to the route id with route-group segments dropped. A refused session is audited with `action` `deny` and `entity` `admin-action`, logged as `auth.access.refused` with that target, and thrown as `error(403, ...)`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:76-83,306-311`, `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:54-64,203`. [verified]
- `f:vkfd7b` The scaffold's site admin sheet, `src/admin.css`, imports Tailwind's theme and its utilities with `source(none)` (automatic source detection off), scans `./routes/admin` and `./lib/admin` through `@source`, and then imports `@glw907/cairn-cms/admin-sources.css`; the `build:admin-css` script compiles it with the Tailwind CLI to `.cairn/admin.css`, which `src/routes/admin/+layout.svelte` imports, so a custom screen's own utility classes compile only when its file sits under one of the scanned roots. Source: `templates/waymark/src/admin.css:1-11`, `templates/waymark/package.json:13`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.svelte:11`. [verified]
- `f:fers77` Each form that posts to a custom `/admin/` screen's action mounts `CsrfField` from `@glw907/cairn-cms/admin`, since the guard refuses every unsafe admin form POST whose `csrf` double-submit field is missing or wrong with a 403, logged as `guard.refused` with `reason: csrf`; inside the authed shell the field takes no `token` prop, because it falls back to the token `CairnAdminShell` provides through context. The signups screen, in the scaffold and the example site alike, mounts a bare `CsrfField` in both its `create` form and its `remove` form. Source: `src/lib/admin/CsrfField.svelte:16-24`, `src/lib/admin/index.ts:17`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:257-283`, `examples/showcase/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.svelte:8,63-64,144-145`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.svelte:8,63-64,144-145`, `docs/reference/admin.md` (the `CsrfField` entry). [verified]

## docs/extend/add-a-second-audience.md

- `f:4673n6` A role declared `{ capability: 'none', home: '/admin/classes' }` still authenticates through the same magic-link flow every editor uses; `none` capability means the engine's content and roster screens refuse this role. Source: `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:12` (`Capability = 'owner' | 'editor' | 'none'`), `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#requireEditor` (`if (editor.capability === 'none') throw error(403, ...)`), `src/lib/auth/access.ts:157`. [verified]
- `f:kqcyv1` A `none`-capability role with no declared `home` lands on a minimal signed-in screen (`view: 'welcome'`, "no content access here") instead of `/admin`'s first concept. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-shell.ts:304-313` (`indexLoad`: `roleHome` absent and `capability === 'none'` returns `{ view: 'welcome', page: {...} }`). [verified]
- `f:hcjb3o` An instructor/staff-shaped second audience is still added and removed through the same invite screen as an editor once the role vocabulary is larger than the default owner/editor pair. Source: `src/lib/admin/ManageEditors.svelte:38-55` (renders a labeled `<select>` over every entry in the declared vocabulary, not a hardcoded owner/editor pair). [verified]
- `f:we2lmp` `createAuthChannel` builds request, confirm, and logout actions over a numeric code, 8 digits by default, configurable from 8 to 10 digits. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:442` (`codeLength: resolveLimit('code.length', code.length, 8, { min: 8, max: 10 })`). [verified]
- `f:rcpe0n` `createAuthChannel`'s backing D1 binding must never be `AUTH_DB`; it needs its own binding and its own `migrations_dir` distinct from the one the site's `AUTH_DB` uses. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:267` (`AuthChannelConfig.resolveDb` doc comment: "The channel's own D1 binding, never `AUTH_DB` (spec, decision 1: physical separation)"), `migrations-channel/0000_channel.sql` (the channel's own migration path, distinct from `migrations/`). [verified]
- `f:41kl6q` `createAuthChannel` config requires `resolveDb`, `deliver`, `lookup`, `normalize`, `challenge`, and `cookie.name`. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:266-298` (`AuthChannelConfig` interface). [verified]
- `f:ffskeq` `lookup` and the optional `verify` each take a `{ env }` context alongside their subject so a roster read can reach its own binding without the channel holding one; neither may read request-shaped data. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts#AuthChannelConfig.lookup` (`lookup: (contact, ctx: { env: Env | undefined })`) and `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts#AuthChannelConfig.verify` (`verify?: (subject, ctx: { env: Env | undefined })`). [verified]
- `f:pa2hqh` `normalize`, `lookup`, and `challenge` each carry a correctness obligation the factory cannot verify: a lossy `normalize` maps two people onto one identity's rate budget, and `challenge` is the whole economic bound on guessing a code. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:285-298` (`AuthChannelConfig`'s `lookup`/`normalize`/`challenge` doc comments: "must be idempotent and canonical per identity, and injective across distinct people"; "the most load-bearing of the three correctness obligations this factory cannot itself verify"). [verified]
- `f:6ab2z6` The packaged channel migration ships at `node_modules/@glw907/cairn-cms/migrations-channel/0000_channel.sql`; every statement is idempotent (`CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`), so re-running it against an already-provisioned database changes nothing and records the `d1_migrations` marker. Source: `migrations-channel/0000_channel.sql` (module comment: "Every statement is idempotent"; `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS cairn_channel_meta`). [verified]
- `f:fcqs22` Testing a channel against a real D1-shaped double uses `@glw907/cairn-cms-dev`'s `createChannelDb`, which needs `node:sqlite`; the package declares `engines.node` `>=24` but adds no runtime floor guard, and npm only warns when the running Node misses an `engines` floor. Source: `packages/cairn-cms-dev/package.json:12-14`, `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/channel-db.ts:36-42`. [verified]
- `f:l2xruc` `createChannelDb` is Node only: it needs `node:sqlite`, and under workerd `await import('node:sqlite')` resolves but `new DatabaseSync(':memory:')` throws `Illegal constructor`, so it serves a vitest run or `vite dev` on Node and never a Worker, and `wrangler dev` needs local D1 instead. Source: `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/channel-db.ts:44-46`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-sveltekit-3-upgrade-design.md` (the workerd probe). [verified]
- `f:7n28wc` The `/admin` index redirects a session whose role declares a `home` to that path (303) before it tries the first reachable concept; a `none`-capability role with no `home` gets the welcome view instead. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-shell.ts:302-317`. [verified]
- `f:5t6hz6` A session for a `none`-capability role is an ordinary row in `AUTH_DB`'s `editor` table (its `role` names a declared role) and populates `locals.cairnEditor` the same way an editor's does, with `capability` resolved from the declared vocabulary. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:351-356,372`, `migrations/0001_roles.sql:6-10`. [verified]
- `f:16paho` A `navLayout` entry or section takes an optional `roles` list of declared role names; on a section it gates every child at once, composing with each child's own capability gate, and a name the site's vocabulary does not declare fails validation. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:61,126-133,166-170`. [verified]
- `f:dtz8oa` The channel's `lookup` returns the stable subject for a normalized contact or null, which decides membership; a `false` from the optional `verify` destroys the session row on that resolution, while a throw from it only refuses that resolution and keeps the row. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:239-253,267-276,1092-1124`. [verified]
- `f:mfmmof` No admin-toolkit component reads an editor session or `locals.cairnEditor`; `MediaPicker` alone reads the admin shell's media-base context and falls back to a default base outside the shell. Source: `src/lib/admin-toolkit/index.ts:46-56`, `src/lib/admin/MediaPicker.svelte:65`. [verified]
- `f:5rkxfp` A shared `migrations_dir` applies each database's schema to the other the first time either is migrated, so the channel migration lives in a sibling directory; hand-inserting the `d1_migrations` marker stays the fallback only for an operator who must not run the migration runner. Source: `migrations-channel/0000_channel.sql:3-6,8-12`. [verified]
- `f:tnvu0a` `createChannelDb` carries no runtime guard for `node:sqlite`, which has been unflagged since Node.js 22.13, below the dev package's `engines.node` of `>=24`. Source: `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/channel-db.ts:33-38`, `packages/cairn-cms-dev/package.json:12`. [verified]
- `f:xlej3f` The dev package's `engines.node` field enforces the Node floor at install time. Source: `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/channel-db.ts:36-38` (npm only warns when the running version misses an `engines` floor). [rejected: an engines mismatch warns and does not block an install unless engine-strict is set]
- `f:q7fj6p` `createAuthChannel` returns `actions` (request, confirm, logout), `resolveSubject`, and `revokeSessions`, and ships no route or UI; its sessions never populate `locals.cairnEditor`, so a site builds the member area as its own routes outside `/admin`, gated by `resolveSubject(event)`. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:2-5,347,1129-1137`. [verified]
- `f:vo4m61` A wrong code on confirm returns `bad-code` and mints no session; construction throws when `challenge` or any other required function is missing, and a false or thrown challenge answers `challenge-required` without minting. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:394-398,596-597,703-704,1017`. [verified]
- `f:b3l3t0` A population beyond the editors gets one of two mechanisms: a declared role, whose members sign in by the same magic link and live as rows in `AUTH_DB`'s `editor` table, or `createAuthChannel`, a separate login with its own subject, D1 binding, and session that never populates `locals.cairnEditor`. Source: `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:12`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:359-360`, `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:1,343,1127-1135` (returns actions and `resolveSubject`, never touching `locals`). [verified]
- `f:lncjdr` The admin-toolkit primitives render the same list-and-form scaffolding in a member area outside `/admin` as they do in the admin, since they carry no dependency on an editor session. Source: `src/lib/admin/CairnAdminShell.svelte:43,689-693`, `scripts/build/admin-css.input.css:1-3`, `docs/reference/admin-toolkit.md:21-24`. [rejected: the primitives assemble daisyUI classes that `cairn-admin.css` compiles and scopes under the admin `data-theme` root, and `CairnAdminShell` is what imports that sheet and sets the root; outside the shell each primitive's own scoped `<style>` still applies, but its daisyUI classes go unstyled unless the site's own stylesheet supplies them]

## docs/extend/add-an-island.md

- `f:nm3bbo` An island is declared with `hydrate: true` (mount eagerly, on first load and after every client navigation) or `hydrate: 'visible'` (defer to first intersection). Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:74` (`hydrate?: boolean | 'visible'`) and `src/lib/islands/index.ts` (`observeIsland`/`mountIsland` dispatch on `data-cairn-hydrate === 'visible'`). [verified]
- `f:1uk9sw` `build()` still runs for a hydrating component; its output is the fallback shown before JS runs and the only content a no-JS reader ever sees. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts` `ComponentDef.build` contract (component context doc). [verified]
- `f:jz4qka` Every island prop is untrusted: the pipeline HTML-attribute-escapes on emit, and the client `JSON.parse`s inside a `try`/`catch`, safe against breakout only because the value never enters a script context. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts` `mountIsland` (`JSON.parse` wrapped in `try`/`catch`, returns on parse failure). [verified]
- `f:thjrvl` Islands are registered per directive name on the adapter's `rendering.islands`, alongside `rendering.render`. Source: `src/lib/islands/types.ts:11` (`IslandRegistry`, keyed by directive name), `src/lib/content/types.ts:266` (`islands?: IslandRegistry` on the `rendering` group). [verified]
- `f:mv684d` `defineAdapter` fails closed at declaration if a `hydrate` component has no matching `islands` entry, or vice versa, naming the mismatched directive. Source: `src/lib/content/adapter.ts:10-25` (`assertIslandsConsistent`, called from `defineAdapter`). [verified]
- `f:rmlwca` `hydrateIslands(islands, root = document)` tears down the previous pass before mounting again, so re-navigating to a page with an island mounts exactly one instance, never a stack of duplicates. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts` (`teardown()` called at top of `hydrateIslands`). [verified]
- `f:urw3z7` One bad island (unregistered directive name, a prop that fails to parse, a component that throws on mount) is isolated: the static fallback stays in place for that boundary, and every other island still mounts. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts` `mountIsland` (per-node try/catch restoring `fallback` on mount failure; unknown directive name skipped via `if (!Comp) continue`). [verified]
- `f:w8oe1v` `unmount` runs with `outro: false` so teardown is synchronous and deterministic on navigation. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts` `teardown()` (`unmount(instance, { outro: false })`). [verified]
- `f:59u3b9` An empty islands registry keeps the `@glw907/cairn-cms/islands` dynamic import out of a static site's bundle entirely: a site imports the runtime dynamically, gated on a non-empty registry, so a static site never ships it. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:3-4` ("A site imports this dynamically, gated on a non-empty registry, so a static site never ships it"). [verified]
- `f:8o2gbl` The edit page's preview frame is sandboxed and never runs scripts, so it always shows the static fallback, never the mounted island component; verify a live island on the deployed page or `npm run preview`. Source: `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:2130` (`<iframe sandbox="" ... srcdoc={previewDoc}>`). [verified]
- `f:4hd2vg` An island is a component declared with `hydrate`: the pipeline wraps `build()`'s static output in an island boundary carrying the directive name and a JSON prop payload, and a live Svelte component registered under the same name mounts over it in the browser; a component with no `hydrate` stays static, server-only markup with no client JavaScript. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:67-73`, `src/lib/render/rehype-dispatch.ts:126-141`. [verified]
- `f:bwaj5c` The islands runtime mounts with Svelte's own `mount()` and `unmount()` directly, with no framework abstraction, because cairn is Svelte-only by design. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:2-4,8,49`. [verified]
- `f:hje4zb` An island's props are the directive's declared attributes: a `number` field arrives as a JSON number, a `boolean` field as a JSON boolean, and every other field as the literal string the author wrote. Source: `src/lib/render/rehype-dispatch.ts:107-123`. [verified]
- `f:drsabs` `hydrateIslands(islands, root)` is the one client call: a site makes it after each client-side navigation, from an `afterNavigate` callback in its root layout, once the new DOM is in place. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:69-75`. [verified]
- `f:9zxml1` Mounting an island clears the boundary's static fallback and mounts the live component into the same element, restoring the fallback only if the mount throws, so any size or shape difference between the fallback and the component's first render shifts the layout; the showcase's banner fallback states the same message its live component shows so the swap never shifts it. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:35-53`, `examples/showcase/src/theme/markdown-components.ts:325-331`. [verified]
- `f:mynt0l` A `hydrate: 'visible'` island registers an `IntersectionObserver` and mounts once, on the boundary's first intersection with the viewport, so an island a reader never scrolls to never mounts. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:55-67,84`. [verified]
- `f:8356mn` The islands runtime passes a boundary's parsed `data-cairn-props` values to the component unchanged, with no sanitizing or URL filtering, so an island that routes a prop into `{@html}`, an `href` or `src` (a `javascript:` URL), or an inline `style` owns that injection risk itself. Source: `src/lib/islands/index.ts:37-49`. [verified: the pass-through traces to `mountIsland`; that Svelte injects `{@html}` content and URL attributes without sanitizing is Svelte's documented behavior]
- `f:lv29ox` The showcase's root layout gates island hydration on a `hasIslands` flag its server load computes from the registry, then dynamically imports only `@glw907/cairn-cms/islands` and the lean `#theme/islands/registry.js`, never the site adapter, since importing `#theme/cairn.config.js` client-side would ship the adapter's renderer, icon set, and media manifest to every public page. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/+layout.svelte:11-26`, `examples/showcase/src/routes/+layout.server.ts:23`. [verified]

## docs/extend/add-cairn-to-a-sveltekit-app.md

- `f:5f4kmk` cairn commits through a GitHub App's installation token, never a personal access token; the commit author is always the signed-in editor, and the engine omits the committer, so GitHub records the App's bot identity as the committer (the engine's own comments say so), and the App's registered name (`cairn-<site slug>` by default for a scaffolded site) is not a field the engine sets. Source: `src/lib/github/types.ts:20` ("The committer is left to the App"), `src/lib/github/repo.ts:260-263` (the committer is omitted; "GitHub attributes the commit to the App"), `packages/create-cairn-site/src/github/chapter.mjs:161`. [verified: the engine omits the committer and says GitHub then names the App; observed on `glw907/907-life` commit `18644a55` (2026-05-30, an "Update posts" publish): author Geoff Wright, committer `cairn-cms[bot]`]
- `f:vgw8x9` The GitHub App needs exactly one repository permission, Contents: Read and write, with Webhook cleared (Active unchecked) since cairn never receives a webhook. Source: GitHub App setup UI. [external: GitHub]
- `f:gglwt4` The Contents: Read and write permission is repository-wide; GitHub's installation token can write to any path in the installed repository, and cairn's confinement to its declared content directories is enforced by engine code, not by GitHub. Source: `src/lib/github/repo.ts:265-268` (comment: "Caller preconditions this layer cannot enforce (the save and lifecycle paths must): every `path` is confined to the site's content directories (the App token can write anywhere in the repo)"). [verified]
- `f:vpieos` The engine documents the Worker secret `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` as the PEM contents base64-encoded onto a single line, and it decodes the secret with `atob()` before signing; `atob()` ignores ASCII whitespace, so the one-line form is the documented form, not a parse requirement (see the rejected `f:w78j1b`). Source: `src/lib/env.ts:31` ("The GitHub App's private key, base64 of the PEM on one line, decoded with `atob()` before signing"), `src/lib/github/signing.ts:44,67,132` (`atob(creds.privateKeyB64)`). [verified]
- `f:if45on` The self-owned auth D1 database is created with `npx wrangler d1 create <name>` and bound as `AUTH_DB`, with `migrations_dir` pointing at a copied-in `migrations/` directory. Source: `src/lib/env.ts:75-79` (`requireDb`, throws `config.bindings-missing` unless the site wires the `AUTH_DB` binding); the `npx wrangler d1 create`/`migrations_dir` setup steps are Cloudflare Wrangler CLI mechanics, external to engine code. [verified]
- `f:gffvfd` The migration count, settled against `migrations/`: five migration files (`0000_auth.sql`, `0001_roles.sql`, `0002_audit.sql`, `0003_preview.sql`, `0004_login_nonce.sql`) producing five tables. `0000_auth.sql` creates `editor`, `magic_token`, `session`; `0002_audit.sql` creates `audit_log`; `0003_preview.sql` creates `preview_tokens`. `0001_roles.sql` and `0004_login_nonce.sql` are ALTER-shaped migrations (rebuild `editor` to drop its role `CHECK`; add a `nonce_hash` column to `magic_token`) and create no table of their own, so only three of the five files run a `CREATE TABLE`. Source: `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `migrations/0001_roles.sql`, `migrations/0002_audit.sql`, `migrations/0003_preview.sql`, `migrations/0004_login_nonce.sql`. [verified]
- `f:rn62i1` `0001_roles.sql` is opt-in, needed only if a site declares a role vocabulary beyond the default owner/editor pair; `0003_preview.sql` is opt-in, needed only for draft-preview links. Source: `migrations/0000_auth.sql` (original `role TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('owner', 'editor'))`, already cited by `f:xkkt1o`), `migrations/0001_roles.sql` (lifts that CHECK, needed only for a role vocabulary beyond the default pair), `migrations/0003_preview.sql:1-2` ("Opt-in: only a site that mints preview links... ever touches this table"). [verified]
- `f:pkrwom` `0002_audit.sql` is a standalone `audit_log` table recommended on its own dedicated D1 binding (commonly `AUDIT_DB`) with its own `migrations_dir`, distinct from the auth database's, so heavy audit writes never contend with session and token lookups. Source: `docs/reference/sveltekit.md` (the `createD1AuditSink` section, lines 639-663: a separate database keeps audit writes from contending with session and token lookups, a dedicated `AUDIT_DB` binding, its own `migrations_dir` `migrations/audit`), `src/lib/sveltekit/audit-sink.ts:81` (`db` is "the D1 binding the packaged `audit_log` table lives in"), `migrations/0002_audit.sql:1-3`. [verified]
- `f:xhwl32` The Email Sending binding (`send_email: [{ name: 'EMAIL' }]`) needs the domain zone in `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` onboarded via `npx wrangler email sending enable <domain>`; a `workers.dev` subdomain has no zone to onboard. Source: Cloudflare Email Sending / wrangler CLI. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:t4pwpw` Sending to a second person requires Cloudflare's Workers Paid plan ($5/month); sending to yourself is free on any plan. Source: Cloudflare Workers pricing. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:zcwf5i` The App ID and Installation ID are not secrets; the engine treats them as plain identity, never as credentials to sign with, and they pass directly into `createGithubApp(...)`. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:87,104` (`GithubAppProvider`: "the GitHub App's non-secret identity facts"; `GithubAppConfig`: "The non-secret GitHub App identity an adapter carries in source; the private key stays a Worker secret"). [verified]
- `f:pg2smj` The retired JS doctor's `github.app` check read its GitHub credentials from the local shell environment (`GITHUB_APP_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_INSTALLATION_ID`, `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64`), not from the pushed Worker secret, and reported a skip when not all three were set. The check is dropped in the doctor retirement, with no doctor-time substitute (`cairn doctor`'s eleven checks carry no `github.app` entry); the runtime equivalent is the `github.app-unreachable` condition, raised when the Worker's own `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` secret is unset. Source: `src/lib/github/credentials.ts:9-22` (`appCredentials`, throws `CairnError('github.app-unreachable', ...)` when `env.GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` is unset) and `docs/reference/cli-cairn-doctor.md` (the checks table). [verified: the runtime `github.app-unreachable` half traces to `credentials.ts`; the retired doctor's credential-reading and skip behavior is historical, its source removed with the rest of `src/lib/doctor`]
- `f:m0ouh8` After wiring bindings by hand, `cairn doctor` (no flags, no credential) run from the site's directory has a `config.bindings` check that confirms `AUTH_DB` and `EMAIL` are both wired, replacing the retired npm bin's `--from`/`--repo` invocation. No command checks the GitHub App itself: that still needs a manual publish and a check that the resulting commit lands on `main`. Source: `tool/internal/doctor/check_bindings.go:17-28` (`config.bindings` check confirms `EMAIL` and `AUTH_DB` are declared); no `github.app`, D1, or auth-store check exists among the eleven `tool/internal/doctor/check_*.go` files. [verified]
- `f:26kuvx` `@cloudflare/workers-types` (`^5`) is a required peer dependency of `@glw907/cairn-cms`, because the shipped sources import `D1Database` and related types from it; `@anthropic-ai/sdk`, `daisyui`, and `tailwindcss` are optional peers. Source: `package.json:217-235`, `src/lib/env.ts:5`. [verified]
- `f:xyizai` The scaffolded site builds with `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` and deploys to Cloudflare Workers. Source: `templates/waymark/vite.config.ts:1`, `templates/waymark/package.json:38`. [verified]
- `f:gyu7jc` cairn ships no GitHub App and no shared credential: each site registers its own App, carries the non-secret App ID and Installation ID in its adapter source, and keeps the private key as its own Worker secret. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:104-112`, `src/lib/env.ts:31`. [verified]
- `f:nbnq9c` After a GitHub App is installed on an account, GitHub redirects to an installation settings URL of the form `https://github.com/settings/installations/<installation_id>`, and the trailing number is the Installation ID. Source: GitHub installation settings page for a personal account. [external: GitHub]
- `f:bbbfqb` GitHub never lets an App's permissions be reduced after installation. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/maintaining-github-apps/modifying-a-github-app-registration ("Changing the permissions of a GitHub App"). [rejected: GitHub applies a permission the owner removes from an App immediately; only added permissions need each installation owner's approval]
- `f:ntdafg` The engine reads `AUTH_DB` (the D1 binding), `EMAIL` (the Email Sending binding, or an injected sender of the same shape), `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` (the canonical origin for confirmation links), and `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` from the platform env; a missing `AUTH_DB` throws `config.bindings-missing`, and an unset or invalid `PUBLIC_ORIGIN` throws `config.public-origin-invalid`. Source: `src/lib/env.ts:17-31,43-66,69-79`. [verified]
- `f:hft8s8` `0000_auth.sql` and `0004_login_nonce.sql` are the two migrations every site applies: the `magic_token.nonce_hash` column from `0004` binds a sign-in token to the browser that requested it, and a store call against an `AUTH_DB` without it fails with an error naming the missing column and `0004_login_nonce.sql`. Source: `src/lib/auth/store.ts:20-31,63-68`. [verified]
- `f:fekvhi` `createD1AuditSink(db, waitUntil)` persists into the `audit_log` table of whichever D1 binding the site passes as `db`; the reference's worked hook reads that binding as `AUDIT_DB`, and `0002_audit.sql` is applied to that database. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/audit-sink.ts:81`, `migrations/0002_audit.sql:1-3`. [verified]
- `f:7y7act` A real publish produces a commit authored by `cairn-cms[bot]`. Source: `src/lib/github/types.ts:20` (the commit author is the signed-in editor), `src/lib/github/repo.ts:260-263`, https://docs.github.com/en/rest/git/commits#create-a-commit. [rejected: the author is the signed-in editor; the bot identity is the committer, since no committer is set and GitHub records the App's `<app name>[bot]`]
- `f:xxtooz` The package ships its five migrations under `migrations/`; a site copies the ones it needs into its own `migrations_dir` and applies them with `wrangler d1 migrations apply <database> --local` or `--remote`. Source: `package.json:196-198`, `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `migrations/0004_login_nonce.sql`. [verified: the shipped-files half traces to the package `files` list; the apply command is Wrangler CLI mechanics, external to engine code]
- `f:02g94x` GitHub generates a GitHub App's private key from the App's settings page and downloads it as a `.pem` file, and the App ID is shown on that same settings page. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps. [external: GitHub]
- `f:9yi7fu` The GitHub App's name and homepage URL are cosmetic: the engine never reads them (a grep of `src/lib` finds no App name or homepage read), and it sets no commit field from them: a publish's author is the editor and its committer is omitted, so GitHub attributes the commit to the App. Source: `src/lib/github/types.ts:20`, `src/lib/github/repo.ts:260-263`, `packages/create-cairn-site/src/github/chapter.mjs:161`. [verified]
- `f:e5vm42` Wiring cairn into an existing app creates three things before any adapter config: the GitHub App, which yields the App ID, Installation ID, and private key; the D1 auth database, whose `wrangler d1 create` output carries the database id the `AUTH_DB` entry needs; and the Worker bindings and secret, which consume both and so come last. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:105-112`, `src/lib/env.ts:31,75-79`. [verified: the identity, key, and binding needs trace to the engine; the database id in `wrangler d1 create` output is Wrangler CLI behavior]
- `f:7dtrwy` The `.pem` GitHub generates is the App's private key, the credential that signs the App's requests for installation tokens with Contents write on the repository, so it stays out of the repository: the site keeps it only as the base64 Worker secret `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64`, never in a tracked file. Source: `src/lib/env.ts:31`, `src/lib/github/signing.ts:44,67`. [verified]
- `f:l5gx1t` The installation token can write any path in the repository the App is installed on, and only engine code confines writes to the declared content directories, so installing the App on a repository that also holds code or other teams' content puts that content inside the token's write reach. Source: `src/lib/github/repo.ts:265-268`. [verified]
- `f:0w7jar` `createGithubApp` takes `owner`, `repo`, `branch`, `appId`, and `installationId`, all required strings, with `branch` the backend's default branch, and the scaffolded site's adapter passes all five in one call. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:122,162-168`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:149`. [verified]
- `f:rp65d2` GitHub's App registration form asks whether the App can be installed only on the registering account or on any account, and installing it lets the installer grant access to all repositories or only selected ones; a cairn site needs one installation, on the account that owns its content repository and covering that repository, since each site registers its own App and the adapter names that `owner` and `repo`. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/registering-a-github-app/registering-a-github-app, https://docs.github.com/en/apps/using-github-apps/installing-your-own-github-app, `src/lib/github/backend.ts:105-112`. [external: GitHub]
- `f:97fxdx` `base64 -w0 your-key.pem` encodes the PEM onto one line on both macOS and Linux. Source: `docs/internal/record/2026-08-unagented-setup-baseline.md:75-78`. [rejected: `-w` is a GNU coreutils flag that the BSD `base64` shipped on a Mac rejects, reproduced in the 2026-08 unagented setup baseline; the PowerShell form, `[Convert]::ToBase64String`, does emit one line]
- `f:skeche` The engine's `@sveltejs/kit` peer range is `^2.70`, which admits no SvelteKit 3 release, so `npm install @glw907/cairn-cms` in a project whose `package.json` requires `@sveltejs/kit` `^3.0.0` stops with `ERESOLVE`. Source: `package.json:221`. [verified: the range traces to package.json:221; the ERESOLVE was reproduced on 2026-10-03 against 0.98.0 in a fresh `sv create` project]
- `f:ghzx9c` Since 2026-10-01 (October 1, 2026), `@sveltejs/kit` 3.0.0 and `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` 8.0.0 carry npm's `latest` dist-tag, so `npx sv create --template minimal --types ts --no-add-ons` scaffolds `@sveltejs/kit` `^3.0.0` and `@sveltejs/adapter-auto` `^8.0.0`, and the scaffold's `tsconfig.json` extends `$app/tsconfig`. Source: the npm registry's dist-tags and publish times for `@sveltejs/kit` (3.0.0, 2026-10-01T17:22Z) and `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` (8.0.0, 2026-10-01T17:24Z), https://www.npmjs.com/package/@sveltejs/kit, and the `npx sv create` run of 2026-10-03. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:jzm5ef` `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` 7.x declares `@sveltejs/kit` `^2.0.0` as its peer and 8.0.0 declares `^3.0.0-next.0`, so a SvelteKit 2 project takes the adapter at `^7`; after `npm uninstall @sveltejs/adapter-auto` and `npm install -D @sveltejs/kit@^2.70 @sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare@^7` in a fresh `sv create` project, `npm install @glw907/cairn-cms` resolves and `npm run build` exits 0. Source: the npm registry's `peerDependencies` for `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` 7.2.9 and 8.0.0, and the 2026-10-03 run against `@glw907/cairn-cms` 0.98.0 (`@sveltejs/kit` 2.70.3, `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` 7.2.9). [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:g48ytv` On SvelteKit 2, a project's `tsconfig.json` extends `./.svelte-kit/tsconfig.json`, which `svelte-kit sync` writes, and `$app/tsconfig` does not resolve, so `svelte-check` reports `File '$app/tsconfig' not found` until the file takes the SvelteKit 2 form. Source: the 2026-10-03 run in a fresh `sv create` project pinned to `@sveltejs/kit` 2.70.3, where `npm run check` failed on the scaffold's `tsconfig.json` and passed with 0 errors on the SvelteKit 2 form, which matches the reader read's working run of the same date. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:thgmpz` A Worker's Wrangler configuration declares a Custom Domain as a `routes` entry whose `pattern` is the hostname and whose `custom_domain` is `true`; `npx wrangler deploy` creates the Custom Domain, and Cloudflare creates its DNS record and certificate, on a hostname inside an active Cloudflare zone. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/routing/custom-domains/ (last updated 2026-09-29). [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:ibis7z` The minimal `sv create` scaffold's home page is `src/routes/+page.svelte`, a "Welcome to SvelteKit" heading and one paragraph, beside the root layout `src/routes/+layout.svelte`. Source: the `npx sv create --template minimal --types ts --no-add-ons field-notes` run of 2026-10-03. [external: SvelteKit sv]
- `f:9slxqf` The setup command scaffolds a new site only into a target directory that is missing or empty: a directory that already holds files fails the scaffold step with "already exists and is not empty", so `create-cairn-site` never adds cairn to an existing app. Source: `packages/create-cairn-site/src/scaffold.mjs:80-104` (`assertTargetDirEmpty`, the refusal at line 99), called at `packages/create-cairn-site/src/scaffold.mjs:118`. [verified]

## docs/extend/animate-a-custom-screen.md

- `f:t767qb` The audit's `static.adminScope` defaults to `src/routes/admin`, `src/lib/admin`, and `src/lib/admin-toolkit`; a site whose admin screens live elsewhere names their roots under that key so the three static motion rules resolve over them. Source: `src/lib/audit/config.ts#DEFAULT_ADMIN_SCOPE`. [verified]
- `f:017qss` Both admin theme roots, `[data-theme='cairn-admin']` and `[data-theme='cairn-admin-dark']`, declare the same eight motion custom properties: durations `--cairn-dur-instant` 70ms, `--cairn-dur-quick` 110ms, `--cairn-dur-base` 150ms, `--cairn-dur-shift` 240ms, `--cairn-dur-settle` 400ms, and curves `--cairn-ease-standard` `cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 0.38, 0.9)`, `--cairn-ease-entrance` `cubic-bezier(0, 0, 0.38, 0.9)`, `--cairn-ease-exit` `cubic-bezier(0.2, 0, 1, 0.9)`. The design system treats the set as closed (five durations, three curves), and `motion-vocabulary` accepts only these eight names in `var()` form. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:140-147,398-405`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-vocabulary.ts:83-92`, `docs/internal/admin-design-system.md:1544-1545`. [verified]
- `f:018sgk` Both theme roots repoint Tailwind's `--default-transition-duration` at `var(--cairn-dur-base)` and `--default-transition-timing-function` at `var(--cairn-ease-standard)` in an unlayered rule, so a `transition-*` utility with no `duration-*` or `ease-*` sibling animates on the base duration and the standard curve. `motion-vocabulary` treats such a declaration as riding those defaults and reports, once per run, when the built sheet's two roots do not carry both repoints. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:154-155,406-407`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-vocabulary.ts:194-222,272`. [verified]
- `f:06bmkq` A bare `npx cairn-audit` runs four motion rules over a site's admin screens, three at error tier (`motion-property`, `motion-vocabulary`, `motion-hover-gate`) and one advisory (`motion-reduced-delay`). Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/index.ts:28-52`, `src/lib/audit/bin.ts:69-71`. [rejected: a bare run executes only the static registry, whose motion rules are five, all error tier: `motion-band`, `motion-property`, `motion-vocabulary`, `motion-hover-gate`, and `reduced-motion`; `motion-reduced-delay` is a rendered advisory rule that runs only under `cairn-audit --rendered` against a running server]
- `f:09g8ev` `motion-property` (error tier, `adminOnly`) allows a `transition` or `transition-property` to name only thirteen properties: `opacity`, `color`, `background-color`, `border-color`, `box-shadow`, `outline-color`, `outline-width`, `outline-offset`, `rotate`, `translate`, `scale`, `transform`, and `grid-template-rows`. It fails a named layout property (`width`, `height`, `top`, `left`, `right`, `bottom`, `margin`, `padding`, `font-size`, and every `margin-*` and `padding-*` longhand) with a layout-hazard message, fails any other unlisted name as outside the language, and fails one declaration that names more than three properties. `display` and `overlay` pass only in an entry that also carries `allow-discrete`. `transition: all` and `none` are not reported here, since `motion-band` owns `all`. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:37-87,145-151,256-266,428-439`. [verified]
- `f:09vl09` The `motion-property` fix message tells you to drop the property or move it onto the one frame-offset allowance. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:153-169`. [rejected: the layout-hazard message says to move the change onto an allowlisted paint property, or to claim the one documented frame-offset exception if this is the persistent-frame case, and the outside-the-allowlist message says to express the change through a property the motion language already covers; neither says to drop the property]
- `f:0aa9tp` `motion-vocabulary` (error tier, `adminOnly`) fails a literal duration (any `ms` or `s` value in `transition`, `transition-duration`, `animation`, or `animation-duration` not written as a `--cairn-dur-*` `var()`) and a literal easing keyword or `cubic-bezier(` (not a `--cairn-ease-*` `var()`), in hand-authored CSS and in the compiled class join. Its duration message names the nearest of the five duration tokens by millisecond distance and the authoring form (`var(...)` in CSS, `duration-(...)` in a class); its easing message always points at `--cairn-ease-standard`. An `infinite` animation skips the duration check and must use the literal `linear`. It records a note and no finding for the reduced-motion floor (`0.01ms` inside `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`), a shorthand carrying `allow-discrete`, a `var()` in the shorthand's property slot, and a `calc()` over a foreign variable. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-vocabulary.ts:72-99,157-192,224-246,259-292,409-426`. [verified]
- `f:0mbj5n` `motion-hover-gate` (error tier, `adminOnly`) fails a hand-authored `:hover` selector outside `@media (hover: hover)` when that rule, or the base rule the selector matches in the same file with `:hover` removed, declares a transition or animation; it also fails a `:focus-visible` alternative that declares motion from inside the hover guard. A pair split into a guarded `:hover` rule and an unguarded `:focus-visible` rule passes, and a Tailwind `hover:` utility is out of scope because it compiles to the guard already. The fix message says to wrap the block in the hover media query and to split a `:hover` and `:focus-visible` or `:focus-within` pair first, because a keyboard user on a touch device still needs the focus motion. Unit tests pin the split pair, the focus alternative left inside the guard, and the utility exemption. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-hover-gate.ts:52-104`, `src/tests/unit/audit/rules/motion-hover-gate.test.ts:35-88`, `docs/internal/admin-design-system.md:1601-1611`. [verified]
- `f:0w432q` `motion-reduced-delay` is a rendered advisory rule that opts into a `reducedMotion: 'reduce'` context: it reads the computed `transition-delay` and `animation-delay` of every element and its `::before` and `::after`, and flags one whose list has an entry above zero, since the delay still holds motion back for a reader who asked for less. It is advisory because every offender found was DaisyUI's own CSS. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/rendered/motion-reduced-delay.ts:1-21,51-97`. [verified]
- `f:1md5oj` `cairn-audit` exits 1 only when a report holds an unsuppressed error-tier finding, so an advisory finding prints without changing the exit code; a bare run reads the static registry, and `--rendered` drives a browser against an already-running server instead. Source: `src/lib/audit/report.ts:55-57`, `src/lib/audit/bin.ts:69-73`. [verified]
- `f:1x8r1x` `motion-property`, `motion-vocabulary`, and `motion-hover-gate` declare `adminOnly`, so the runner hands them only the components under `static.adminScope` and only the `static.cssFiles` entries that lie inside those roots; a marketing page outside the admin roots is never read by them. `motion-band` and `reduced-motion` declare no `adminOnly` and read `static.scope` and every named CSS file. Source: `src/lib/audit/run.ts:209-210,226-228`, `src/lib/audit/types.ts:78-86`, `src/tests/unit/audit/rules/motion-hover-gate.test.ts:90`. [verified]
- `f:22odbz` `static.cssFiles` names standalone CSS files, none by default, that the CSS-family static rules read beside every component's scoped `<style>` block. `token-colors`, `motion-band`, and `reduced-motion` read every named file, while the three `adminOnly` motion rules read a named file only when it lies under an admin root, so a theme file outside `src/routes/admin`, `src/lib/admin`, `src/lib/admin-toolkit`, and any configured `static.adminScope` is not checked by them even when named. Source: `src/lib/audit/config.ts:23-29,314`, `src/lib/audit/run.ts:209-210`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/css-scope.ts:33-50`. [verified]
- `f:24f8gn` The audit has two motion surfaces: the CSS family reads authored rules from a component's scoped `<style>` block and from `static.cssFiles`, and the class join resolves every class token in a component's markup through the built admin sheet's compiled declarations and applies the same checks to what the class compiles to. `motion-property` also resolves an `animate-*` utility through its `--animate-*` custom property's `@keyframes`. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:10-24,268-334`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-vocabulary.ts:6-7,369-407`. [verified]
- `f:2c19kf` In the class join, `motion-property` skips a class token whose variant-stripped base is one of twelve DaisyUI names (`btn`, `drawer-side`, `filter`, `collapse`, `modal`, `modal-box`, `dropdown`, `dropdown-content`, `menu`, `checkbox`, `card`, `toggle`) or one of six Tailwind transition utilities (`transition`, `transition-all`, `transition-colors`, `transition-opacity`, `transition-shadow`, `transition-transform`), keyed on the utility name alone, so an arbitrary form such as `transition-[width]` is convicted normally. `motion-vocabulary` skips fourteen DaisyUI names (those twelve plus `select` and `radio`) and treats the compiled Tailwind transition utilities as riding the theme root defaults. The lists are explicit class names, not attribution from the compiled sheet. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:89-125,283-298`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-vocabulary.ts:102-132`. [verified]
- `f:2gdaks` The frame-offset attribute counts only as the literal static string `data-cairn-motion="frame-offset"`: the rule compares each parsed attribute's value to that string, so a bound or interpolated value reads as absent, and when no carrying node exists anywhere in the run every property the attribute-keyed CSS rule declares, `margin-left` included, is convicted at that rule. A unit test pins the bound-value case. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:246-250,354-393`, `src/tests/unit/audit/rules/motion-property.test.ts:301-315`. [verified]
- `f:2gmjvn` The frame offset is the one allowance keyed on the attribute plus the property: a CSS rule whose selector contains `[data-cairn-motion="frame-offset"]` may transition `margin-left` and nothing else, the first carrying element passes, a second layout property in that rule is convicted on the carrying element, and a second carrying element is convicted for each property the rule declares, `margin-left` included. There is no file key and no selector key. The CSS-family pass skips such a rule, and a second carrier with no attribute-keyed rule in the run draws no finding. The shell carries the attribute on its content column and cairn-admin.css declares the rule, entering on `--cairn-dur-shift` and `--cairn-ease-entrance` and leaving on `--cairn-dur-base` and `--cairn-ease-exit`, gated behind a frame-ready marker so hydration never animates. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:26-29,126-130,272,354-414`, `src/tests/unit/audit/rules/motion-property.test.ts:266-300`, `src/lib/admin/CairnAdminShell.svelte:759`, `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:179-184`. [verified]
- `f:2khr2m` A screen is one component file, so the frame-offset allowance is counted per file: the first carrying element in a file passes and a second in the same file is convicted. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:354-374,395-398`. [rejected: `checkFrameOffset` collects the carrying nodes across every file the run parses and only the first, at index 0, is exempt, so the count runs across the whole admin scope in file then document order, and the rule and its carrier normally sit in different files, as the shell's carrier in a `.svelte` file and its rule in `cairn-admin.css` do]
- `f:2mhd9l` A carrying element declares its margin transition with a Tailwind arbitrary class, as in `<div data-cairn-motion="frame-offset" class="transition-[margin-left]">`. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/motion-property.ts:283-298,376-377`, `src/lib/admin/CairnAdminShell.svelte:747-753,759`. [rejected: the exemption belongs to a CSS rule whose selector contains the attribute selector, and the class join has no frame-offset branch, so a compiled `transition-[margin-left]` class is convicted as a named layout property; the shell writes the attribute alone and declares the transition in cairn-admin.css, and its own comment notes that an unclassed attribute stays outside the class-join scan]
- `f:2p5otw` The admin's blanket `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` rule over both theme roots and every descendant sets `animation-duration` and `transition-duration` to `0.01ms !important`, zeroes both delays, and sets `animation-iteration-count: 1`. The design system permits a paint transition (`opacity`, color properties, `box-shadow`, `outline-*`) to opt back in by restating the transition inside the same guard with `!important`, and keeps transform, `grid-template-rows`, and frame-offset motion at the floor with no opt-back-in; cairn's own admin restates none. Source: `src/lib/admin/cairn-admin.css:1791-1806`, `docs/internal/admin-design-system.md:1613-1636`. [verified]
- `f:2v0doe` Each reduced-motion opt-back-in costs one allowlist entry in a site's own `custom-surface-budget.json`, which `check-custom-surface.mjs` compares by length. Source: `scripts/checks/custom-surface-budget.json:2-3,28`, `package.json:64,203-216`. [rejected: the check and its budget are this repository's own gate over the engine admin tree and the showcase, wired as the engine's `check:custom-surface` script, and neither ships in the package `files` list or the scaffold, so a consumer site has no such budget and a restatement adds no allowlist entry there]
- `f:326755` A custom admin screen and the engine's own admin screens are audited by the same static rule objects: the default scan scope names `src/routes/admin` beside the engine's `src/lib/admin` and `src/lib/admin-toolkit`, the packaged `cairn-audit` bin runs one registry with one motion ruleset, and the scaffold's `check:cairn` script runs it. Source: `src/lib/audit/config.ts:20-29`, `src/lib/audit/rules/static/index.ts:28-52`, `package.json:200`, `templates/waymark/package.json:16`. [verified]

## docs/extend/announce-on-publish.md

- `f:ix641z` `buildNewlyPublished(before, after)` returns entries where `draft` is false, `publishedAt` is set, and no prior entry at the same `concept`/`id` key carried a stamp. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:56-65` (`buildNewlyPublished`: `if (e.draft) return false; if (!e.publishedAt) return false; return !priorStamps.get(keyOf(e));`). [verified]
- `f:pm1osl` The draft check runs before the stamp check, so a currently-unpublished entry never matches even if it carries a stamp forward from an earlier publish. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:61-63` (order of the two `if` guards). [verified]
- `f:6cas58` `before: null` means no prior manifest, and `buildNewlyPublished` treats every stamped, non-draft entry in `after` as newly published (a full fan-out), matching the code comment: "A null `before` indexes to nothing... every currently-live stamped entry in `after` reads as newly published." Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:57-59` (comment plus `priorStamps` built from `before?.entries ?? []`). [verified]
- `f:pc3iy5` `buildNewlyPublished` is pure: no I/O, no clock read, deterministic given both manifests. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:56-65` (function body reads only its two arguments, no side effects). [verified]
- `f:df3hqw` The entry identity key is `concept`/`id` together (`keyOf(e) = \`${e.concept}/${e.id}\``); renaming an entry changes that key, so a rename reads as a new publish since the old key's row disappears and the new key has no stamped counterpart. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:36` (`keyOf`). [verified]
- `f:w3m0ac` Deleting a published entry and recreating one under the same id also reads as a new publish, since the deleted row (stamp included) drops out of the prior manifest with no successor to carry the stamp forward. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:56-65` (`buildNewlyPublished`: `priorStamps` is keyed only by `keyOf(e)` from `before`; a key absent from `before`, whether never-published or deleted-then-recreated, reads identically as newly published, with no special case for either), consistent with `f:pm1osl`/`f:ix641z`. [verified]
- `f:xlj297` The engine sends nothing over the network on its own for publish announcements; persisting the prior manifest across deploys (KV, R2, or other storage) is the site's own responsibility. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:47-53` ("A consumer wiring announce-on-publish persists the prior manifest across deploys..."; "The engine performs no network sends; a consumer diffs and then acts."). [verified]
- `f:tdbki1` `@glw907/cairn-cms/delivery/data` exports `buildNewlyPublished`, `parseManifest`, and the `Manifest` type, so a site parses a manifest string and diffs two of them without importing the engine's server code. Source: `src/lib/delivery/data.ts:71-75`, `package.json:134-136`. [verified]
- `f:u1mies` `buildNewlyPublished` only walks the `after` manifest's entries, so an entry present only in `before` (deleted in `after`) is never returned and a removal is silent through this seam. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:44-45,60-65`. [verified]
- `f:4wnm6o` Passing `before: null` on an ordinary deploy re-announces every live stamped entry, so `null` fits only a first backfill run and every later run passes the prior manifest the site persisted. Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:49-51`. [verified]

## docs/extend/architecture.md

- `f:a7qx4m` The engine's export map splits into six functional groups: core/adapter (root barrel), the SvelteKit layer (`/sveltekit`), admin UI (`/admin`, `/public`, `/admin-toolkit`, `/islands`), rendering (`/render`), delivery (`/delivery`, `/media`), and auth/platform (`/auth-store`, `/auth-channel`, `/auth-crypto`, `/cloudflare`, `/vite`, `/ambient`). Source: `package.json:98-197` (`exports` map: root `.`, `/sveltekit`, `/admin`, `/public`, `/admin-toolkit`, `/islands`, `/render`, `/delivery`, `/media`, `/auth-store`, `/auth-channel`, `/auth-crypto`, `/cloudflare`, `/vite`, `/ambient`). [verified]
- `f:0duu5p` Nothing on the root barrel imports SvelteKit, and nothing on `/sveltekit` is a `.svelte` file; a Svelte admin component lives on `/admin`, and a built-in public component on `/public`. Source: `src/lib/*.ts` (no `@sveltejs/kit` or `svelte` imports; `grep` finds none), `src/lib/sveltekit/*.svelte` (no matches). [verified]
- `f:4esdoz` A site declares a single `CairnAdapter` object (typically `src/theme/cairn.config.ts`); every route factory, admin screen, and delivery helper reads its behavior from that one object, and the engine never hard-codes a concept, directory, or field. Source: `src/lib/content/adapter.ts:26-29` (`defineAdapter`, one `CairnAdapter` object), `src/lib/content/types.ts:226-274` (`CairnAdapter`, the single seam the engine consumes). [verified: structurally against the single-adapter-object shape every module reads from; "never hard-codes" is the adapter pattern's design consequence, not one asserted line]
- `f:cjonmm` The write path: an editor's save commits to a per-entry holding branch named `cairn/<concept>/<id>` through the site's GitHub App installation token, with the signed-in editor as commit author and no committer set, so GitHub records the App's bot identity as the committer (see `f:5f4kmk`); a separate Publish action copies that branch's content onto the default branch, which triggers the site's existing deploy. Source: `src/lib/content/pending.ts:1-11`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:1-5`, `src/lib/github/types.ts:20`, `src/lib/github/repo.ts:260-263`. [verified]
- `f:cng7dr` The admin reads content two ways: an entry's edit load and history load assemble one entry's view from a concurrent batch of GitHub reads of the file itself, its pending branch head, the committed manifest, and the media manifest, all through the Backend; corpus-wide admin facts, the concept list, inbound links, reference and media usage, and the link check on save, read the committed content manifest from the default branch through the Backend instead of crawling entry files. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-read.ts:1-4,299-306`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-list.ts:141-146`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:233-236`. [verified]
- `f:e69d0l` The content manifest is rebuilt at build time, and the `cairnManifest` plugin verifies it against the markdown on disk in `buildStart`; it is patched only in a commit to the default branch that changes an entry (a publish, a publish-all, a delete, or a rename), each landing the manifest change together with the entry files, while a save to the pending branch commits no manifest change. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:233-236,341-352,476-479` (`publishAction`'s and `publishAllAction`'s `changes` arrays carry the entry files and `formatManifest(...)`), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:171-178,388` (delete removes the row and rename upserts it, in the same default-branch commit as the file change), `src/lib/vite/internal.ts:1-10,57-85`. [verified]
- `f:n3cvf9` The directive-stamping and dispatch machinery inside the render pipeline, the exact commit tree-operation shape against the GitHub API, and the guard's internal CSRF/session resolution are engine-internal, not seams a site reaches into; each is instead covered by a stability-tiered public surface (`/render`, `/sveltekit`, `/auth-crypto`). Source: `package.json` `exports` map (the `/render` subpath resolves to `dist/render/authoring.js`, a curated re-export, not the full `src/lib/render/*` file set which includes `registry.ts`, `rehype-dispatch.ts`, etc.). [verified]
- `f:i87sd3` Six named seams a site extends through: the adapter's `content` map (`ConceptConfig`/`fieldset`), `render` (built with `createRenderer`), the access map and role vocabulary (`defineAccess`, `defineRoles`), `navLayout`, the Backend interface (`createGithubApp` the only shipped implementation), and `AssetConfig` (media). Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:226-274` (`CairnAdapter`: `content`, `rendering`, `access`/`roles`, `media` fields), `src/lib/content/types.ts:300` (`navLayout` under `editor`), `src/lib/github/backend.ts:78,162` (`BackendProvider`, `createGithubApp` the one shipped implementation). [verified]
