- `f:bmxw7w` The engine has no `/components` export subpath: the admin UI is exported on `/admin` (`CairnAdmin`, `CairnAdminShell`, and the rest of the admin screens) and `/admin-toolkit`, a built-in public component such as `PreviewBanner` is exported on `/public`, and `/islands` is the separate client runtime that mounts a site's live components. Source: `package.json:109-127`, `src/lib/islands/index.ts:1-4`. [verified]
- `f:3rb362` A site touches the engine in three places, and each reaches a different subpath. The adapter is declared with the root barrel's `defineAdapter` and folded by the root barrel's `composeRuntime` into the runtime that `createCairnAdmin` from `/sveltekit` closes over; the admin mount renders `CairnAdmin` and `CairnAdminShell` from `/admin` over that factory's `load`, `shellLoad`, and `actions`; and the public routes call `createPublicRoutes` and the feed, sitemap, and robots responders from `/delivery`. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:63`, `templates/waymark/src/chassis/cairn.server.ts:4-10,28`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/[...path]/+page.svelte:8-9`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/+layout.svelte:8-9`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/[...path]/+page.server.ts:2`, `src/lib/index.ts:51,54`, `src/lib/sveltekit/index.ts:94`. [verified]
- `f:j0ut9n` The engine ships as the npm package `@glw907/cairn-cms`, which a site lists as an ordinary dependency; the site's admin and public pages are route files in the site's SvelteKit app whose load functions and form actions come from the engine's route factories, and the app builds with `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` into one Worker, so the engine runs inside the site's Worker and ships no CMS server of its own. Source: `package.json:2`, `templates/waymark/package.json:23,33`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/[...path]/+page.server.ts:3,9-10`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/[...path]/+page.server.ts:2,8`, `templates/waymark/svelte.config.js:1,9`, `templates/waymark/wrangler.jsonc:6`. [verified: traced against the Waymark template; "no CMS server" rests on the package's bins being build and audit tools only, `package.json:198-202`, with no server entry]
- `f:3x332v` The site's delivery routes call the SvelteKit layer. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/[...path]/+page.server.ts:2`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/feed.xml/+server.ts:2`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/sitemap.xml/+server.ts:2`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/robots.txt/+server.ts:2`, `src/lib/delivery/index.ts:14`. [rejected: the public delivery routes, the entry catch-all, its markdown twin, the feeds, the sitemap, and robots.txt, import only from `/delivery`; a template route reaches `/sveltekit` only for the admin mount, the preview loader, the media route, and the health route]
- `f:40pxcq` The `/sveltekit` source reads and writes the content repository through the GitHub Backend (`../github/`), renders through the render pipeline (`../render/`), reads and writes media through the media store (`../media/`), composes entry data through the delivery module for the preview route (`../delivery/public-routes.js`), reads and writes the auth store and crypto helpers (`../auth/`), and imports two helper modules from `../admin/`; the admin Svelte components sit on `/admin`, which receives the loaded data as props. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-context.ts:10`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:23-24`, `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:35-42`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-media-ingest.ts:7-12`, `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:16-26`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-shell.ts:8-9`. [verified]
- `f:4b3rhm` The D1 side of the auth and platform group is the auth store: `src/lib/auth/store.ts` takes a `D1Database` for every editor, token, and session query, and the `/auth-store` barrel re-exports the roster provisioning functions from it. Source: `src/lib/auth/store.ts:11,52`, `src/lib/auth-store/index.ts:1-17`. [verified]
- `f:4dqyi5` The delivery group, the `/delivery` and `/media` subpaths, reads and writes R2. Source: `src/lib/media/index.ts:1-11`, `src/lib/sveltekit/media-route.ts:133`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-media-ingest.ts:10`. [rejected: neither public barrel touches R2, since `/delivery` holds no R2 code and `/media` is node-safe by design, with its R2-touching `store.ts` and `delivery-bucket.ts` and the delivery-route factory kept on `/sveltekit`; R2 is read by the `/sveltekit` media route and read and written by its media ingest and delete actions]
- `f:533flu` The export map holds subpaths that sit in none of the diagram's six boxes: `./delivery/head` and `./delivery/data`, `./reproductions` and `./reproductions/manifest`, `./log`, and the asset exports `./admin-sources.css` and `./cairn-public.css`; the `/log`, `/reproductions`, and `/delivery/data` subpaths each have their own reference page, and `check:reference` fails an undocumented export. Source: `package.json:138-161,190-195`. [verified]
- `f:5fwrpf` The committer of every cairn commit is the GitHub App's bot identity, `<app name>[bot]`, which is `cairn-cms[bot]` for an App named cairn-cms: the engine sets no committer, so GitHub records the App. A scaffolded site's App is `cairn-<site slug>` by default, so its bot is `cairn-<site slug>[bot]`. Source: `src/lib/github/repo.ts:260-263`, `src/lib/github/types.ts:20`, `packages/create-cairn-site/src/github/chapter.mjs:161`. [verified: the engine omits the committer; observed on `glw907/907-life` commit `18644a55` (2026-05-30, an "Update posts" publish): author Geoff Wright, committer `cairn-cms[bot]`]
- `f:5kerxw` A pending entry is the existence of its `cairn/<concept>/<id>` branch and nothing else; each save commits onto that same branch, so an editor iterates across saves, and the concept list finds the pending entries by listing branches under `cairn/<concept>/`, so the admin shows what is pending without it being live. Source: `src/lib/content/pending.ts:1-11`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-list.ts:141-146`. [verified]
- `f:5kijg0` A public page render reads the entry's file directly through the Backend's content API. Source: `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:1-6`, `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:35-42,491-492`. [rejected: a public page renders from the build-time site resolver that site code builds from `import.meta.glob` raw markdown, so no request-time Backend read occurs; Backend reads belong to the admin edit and history loads and to the preview loader, which reads the pending branch]
- `f:5m3725` A request needing corpus-wide facts on the public side, an index page, a tag list, or the sitemap, reads the committed content manifest. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/sitemap.xml/+server.ts:5-14`, `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:1-6`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-list.ts:141-158`. [rejected: the public sitemap, index, and tag pages read the build-time site index, `site.all()`; the committed manifest is read by admin routes, the preview loader, and the build's own verify step, and the link-integrity check on save reads it through the Backend]
- `f:5uhx5o` The content model runs from a build script with no request in scope: the `cairnManifest` plugin's virtual module imports `formatManifest`, `verifyManifest`, and `verifyReferences` from the root barrel and `buildSiteManifest` from `/delivery/data`, loads the site's adapter from its `configModule`, and builds the manifest inside the app's own Vite graph. The root barrel states its scope as no server route, no Svelte component, and no per-request framework binding. Source: `src/lib/vite/internal.ts:1-10,57-85`, `src/lib/index.ts:1-11`. [verified]
- `f:5vjwlc` `CairnAdapter` requires `content` (the concepts, keyed by id), `backend` (a `BackendProvider` from `createGithubApp`), `email` (the magic-link sender), and `rendering.render`; it optionally takes `roles`, `access`, `media`, `aiPosture`, `rendering.components`, `rendering.icons`, `rendering.islands`, and an `editor` group holding `preview`, `nav`, `supportContact`, and `navLayout`. The scaffold declares its adapter at `src/theme/cairn.config.ts`, and left at those defaults with no roles declared it yields the built-in owner and editor pair. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:222-300`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:63`. [verified]
- `f:69rw88` A site's `AssetConfig` is resolved by `normalizeAssets` into a `ResolvedAssetConfig` holding the R2 bucket binding name, the public base, the URL form, `maxUploadBytes`, the allowed types, and the transformations flag; the media route reads its bucket and delivery settings from the composed runtime's `resolvedAssets`, and the upload action checks `maxUploadBytes` from the same value. Source: `src/lib/media/config.ts:14-33`, `src/lib/sveltekit/media-route.ts:87,133`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-media-ingest.ts:93,103,130`. [verified]
- `f:6a32oy` Adding a concept is an edit to the adapter's `content` map and adding a role is an edit to its `roles` declaration, while a custom admin screen is a route file under `src/routes/admin/` that SvelteKit resolves ahead of the catch-all, shown in the sidebar through an `editor.navLayout` site entry; none of these forks the engine. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:226-300`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/signups/+page.server.ts:13`, and the SvelteKit route-precedence behavior in `f:03zj56`. [verified]

## docs/extend/auth-channel-security-model.md

- `f:pjjo64` The governing rule: no control keyed on the victim's identity may deny, delay, or destroy anything; denial keys only on the requester, and an identity-keyed control either escalates through a site-actionable channel or only logs. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:10` (module comment: "identity-keyed control either escalates through `challenge-required` or only logs"), instantiated by the eviction, ceiling, and escalation facts below. [verified]
- `f:7idpoq` Escalation carries the wire result `challenge-required`, returned without a hard failure; a failed challenge on an already-escalated action returns `challenge-required` again, never a hard error. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:62` ("`challenge-required` is a retry invitation, never a hard failure"), `:668`, `:936` (both `return { outcome: 'challenge-required' }`). [verified]
- `f:k6u4g2` Anything that evicts stored rows keys eviction on the requester's own bucket, so a caller can only crowd out its own pending rows, never another person's. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/store.ts:232-239` (comment: "Filtered on `requester_bucket`, never `identity`, so an evictor..."; `WHERE requester_bucket = ?1`). [verified]
- `f:e0imm6` The anti-abuse spend ceiling does not deny; crossing it logs at error to alert the site operator rather than refusing a real member's login. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:339` ("logs `auth.channel.ceiling_exceeded` only, never denies"), `:781` (`log.error('auth.channel.ceiling_exceeded', ...)`). [verified]
- `f:wu8x70` Every action (`request`, `confirm`, `logout`) asserts the request's `Origin` matches the site's own origin and that the connection is `https`, except on a local development host, before any code, budget, or session logic runs; both checks throw a plain 403 with no wire result. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:70-78` (`assertOriginAndScheme`: `@throws HttpError 403 on an origin mismatch, or on plain http anywhere but a local host`), called at `:691` ahead of the rest of `request`. [verified]
- `f:fslodf` Identity correlates via a salted, prefixed hash: `hashToken(salt + 's:' + subject)` when a roster lookup resolved a stable subject, or `hashToken(salt + 'c:' + contact)` otherwise; logs correlate on the first 16 hex characters of this hash (`correlationId`), never on the raw contact. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/identity.ts:6-14` (`deriveIdentity`), `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts#createAuthChannel.requestAction` (`correlationId = identity.slice(0, 16)`). [verified]
- `f:plcf5v` A numeric confirmation code is drawn through rejection sampling over a Web Crypto random byte range, not naive modulo, to avoid the low-end bias a non-power-of-range naive modulo introduces. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/identity.ts:16-34` (`drawBelow`: draws bytes via `crypto.getRandomValues`, rejects draws at or above the largest multiple of the range that fits). [verified]
- `f:1rstld` A confirmed code mints a channel session resolved the same hashed-lookup way the built-in admin session is; `revokeSessions` lets site code end every session for one identity at once (roster removal, reported compromise), the one deliberate identity-keyed destructive capability since it is invoked by the site's own trusted code, not a public caller. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:357,1127` (`revokeSessions(db, subject)`). [verified]
- `f:wuwk2q` A dev transport that prints a channel's code to console instead of sending it creates a roster oracle by construction: an unauthenticated caller can learn whether an arbitrary contact is on the roster with no code-guessing, since delivery only ever runs for a known subject; the same transport also lands plaintext one-time codes in Workers Logs if run in a deployed Worker with observability on. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:877-878` (comment: "Step 8: deliver, only for a known subject"), `:865-874` (the `subject === null` branch returns without calling `config.deliver`). No such dev transport ships in engine code; this is a hazard warning about a pattern a site's own `deliver` implementation could introduce. [verified]
- `f:ez788q` `normalize` must be idempotent, canonical per identity, and injective across distinct people; `lookup`'s subject must be stable and canonical per person; `challenge` must actually verify a human (the factory cannot distinguish a real Turnstile call from `async () => true`). Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:285-298` (same doc comments already cited for `f:pa2hqh`). [verified]
- `f:cf1avu` The site supplies `createAuthChannel`'s delivery function, roster lookup, identifier normalizer, and bot challenge (plus a D1 binding resolver and a cookie name); the factory itself owns code minting, code consumption, rate budgets, sessions, and revocation. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:2-5,266-298`. [verified]
- `f:2w1yrc` The channel's governing rule came from three review rounds that failed on the same move: keying a failed-attempt count on the victim's contact enabled a permanent lockout, keying the send budget on the victim's identity reintroduced it, and a written-down rule with two exceptions still left an escalation path with no result code and a live-row cap that pruned by identity. Source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-03-auth-channel-factory-design.md:40-48`. [verified]
- `f:j254i8` A failed challenge returns `challenge-required` before any code mint, attempt increment, or code consumption, so it charges nothing and destroys nothing: `request` runs the challenge on every call, as its step 2, ahead of normalize, the budgets, and any mint, and `confirm` runs it only once the identity's failure gate is at its threshold (the escalated attempt), where `charge()` adds no unit. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:258-259` ("Awaited before any mint on `request`, and on an escalated `confirm`"), `:663-668` (request's step 2), `:932-936` (confirm's step 5). [verified]
- `f:dipwmx` The `'s:'` and `'c:'` prefixes keep a subject-derived identity from colliding with a contact-derived one even when a subject looks like an email address, and the per-deployment salt, provisioned on first use, keeps the hash from reversing against a small contact space. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/identity.ts:5-12`, `src/lib/auth-channel/store.ts:60-75`. [verified]
- `f:so26hi` When a `createAuthChannel` `deliver` callback throws, the cleanup that deletes the pending code row and refunds the requester's send charge runs inside its own `try`/`catch`; a cleanup that throws logs `auth.channel.send_cleanup_failed` (error, `correlationId` plus a hex-redacted, length-capped `error`) instead of rejecting the promise `waitUntil` holds, which nothing observes. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:840-857`, `docs/reference/log-events.md` (`auth.channel.send_cleanup_failed` row). [verified]
- `f:gubeex` Channel logout deletes the session cookie and destroys that one session's row; it never touches another session of the same identity. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:1084-1106`. [verified]
- `f:s047l1` NIST SP 800-63B requires the secrets behind its authenticators, such as a PIN, a look-up secret list, and a salt, to be generated with an approved random bit generator. Source: https://pages.nist.gov/800-63-3/sp800-63b.html. [external: NIST SP 800-63B; the reading that guessing-resistance math needs a uniform draw is an inference, the text states the generator requirement]
- `f:8u4iiv` Every channel action opens with an unconditional origin and scheme check that mirrors the admin guard's own rule, because the admin guard's stricter admin-path handling never covers the site's own member routes, where the guard only restores kit's origin check for unsafe form posts. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:70-73`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:206-213`. [verified]
- `f:nz890r` `createAuthChannel` is the second-audience login factory; a site that declares no roles gets the built-in owner/editor pair, whose sign-in is the email magic link. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:1`, `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:24`. [verified]
- `f:d2j9cj` A capture transport that records each delivered code for readback, as the showcase's does for its `/test/last-otp` route, answers the same roster oracle a console dev transport does, since delivery runs only for a known subject; the showcase keeps its roster to six demo contacts with no real contact data. Source: `examples/showcase/src/members/capture-transport.ts:1-7,21-39`, `src/lib/auth-channel/factory.ts:865-878`, `examples/showcase/src/members/channel.ts:16-28`. [verified]
- `f:rv9gdc` The showcase keeps its capture transport off a deployed Worker the way cairn's dev backend is kept off: the transport's `deliver` refuses unless `CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND` is `'1'`, checked inside the function, and the dev backend loads only by dynamic import from the `__CAIRN_DEV_BUILD__` branch of `hooks.server.ts`. The members fixture reads the real `MEMBER_DB` binding, which is a local D1 database under `wrangler dev`, so no handle builds a database double or stamps the flag onto the platform. Source: `examples/showcase/src/members/capture-transport.ts:5-7,27-36`, `examples/showcase/src/hooks.server.ts:11-23`, `examples/showcase/src/members/channel.ts:56-58`. [verified]
- `f:7l4bdf` The showcase e2e serves a `VITE_CAIRN_E2E=1` build through `wrangler dev` after applying `migrations-members` to the local `MEMBER_DB`, and delivers the dev-backend opt-in as a command-scoped `--var CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND:1` alongside `--var PUBLIC_ORIGIN:http://localhost:$E2E_PORT`; the flag never goes in `wrangler.jsonc` `vars` or a `.dev.vars` file, so a later unflagged serve cannot inherit it, and an OS environment variable would reach a Node preview server but not workerd. Source: `examples/showcase/playwright.config.ts:44-52`. [verified]

## docs/extend/build-a-site-by-hand.md

- `f:75hawi` Node 24 or later, a GitHub account, and a Cloudflare account are required. A bare SvelteKit deploy runs on Cloudflare's free tier, while a cairn site runs on Cloudflare's Workers Paid plan from the first deploy that carries cairn's own admin, the same plan a second person's sign-in needs, and real sign-in mail also needs a domain zone. Source: `package.json:7` (`"engines": { "node": ">=24" }`) for the Node floor; `packages/create-cairn-site/src/cloudflare/chapter.mjs` (the "Deploy your site to Cloudflare" step's consent, stating plainly that a cairn site needs Workers Paid from its first deploy) for the Workers Paid requirement, the same requirement CHANGELOG.md's Unreleased entry records for the setup command. [verified]
- `f:yegr67` `typescript` must stay on major version 6 for now: `svelte-check` cannot run on TypeScript 7 until the 7.1 compiler API lands, so three tools in cairn's build still pin `typescript` to 6; `npx sv create` already pins the correct major, and cairn's own code and shipped types are 7-clean, which a weekly advisory workflow compares against a TypeScript 7 run. Source: `package.json:304` (`"typescript": "^6.0.3"`), `docs/reference/supported-toolchain.md:52-64`, `.github/workflows/tsgo.yml:1-10`, and a run of `npx sv create --template minimal --types ts --no-add-ons` on 2026-09-29, whose `package.json` listed `"typescript": "^6.0.3"`. [verified]
- `f:ifuvcl` A current `sv create` scaffold has no `svelte.config.js` file; SvelteKit's config, including the adapter, lives inline in the `sveltekit()` plugin call inside `vite.config.ts`. Source: the output of `npx sv create --template minimal --types ts --no-add-ons` run on 2026-09-29 (it listed `package.json`, `vite.config.ts`, `tsconfig.json`, `src`, and `static`, and no `svelte.config.js` or `wrangler.jsonc`), https://svelte.dev/docs/cli/sv-create. [external: SvelteKit sv]
- `f:mhsere` `adapter-auto` picks an adapter at build time by guessing the deploy target; a self-deployed site should name `@sveltejs/adapter-cloudflare` explicitly instead. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/adapter-auto. [external: SvelteKit]
- `f:q13lck` `wrangler.jsonc`'s minimal Cloudflare adapter config needs `main: ".svelte-kit/cloudflare/_worker.js"` and an `assets` block pointing at `.svelte-kit/cloudflare` bound as `ASSETS`, beside a Worker `name` and a `compatibility_date`. Source: `templates/waymark/wrangler.jsonc:4-11`. [verified]
- `f:9mx680` `@glw907/cairn-cms-dev` stands in for the GitHub commit pipeline and the magic-link sign-in loop with in-memory fakes, gated behind a three-layer fence: a build-time flag that strips it from a production bundle, a `devDependency` boundary, and a runtime tripwire the guard enforces; it must never reach a deployed site. Source: `src/lib/dev-flag.ts:1-30` (`isDevBackendFlagSet`, the runtime tripwire `guard.ts` and `auth-channel/factory.ts` both read), `templates/waymark/package.json` (`@glw907/cairn-cms-dev` listed only under `devDependencies`), the already-external `__CAIRN_DEV_BUILD__` Vite `define` fold (`f:72mctx`). [verified]
- `f:vvgpr5` `src/app.d.ts` must `import '@glw907/cairn-cms/ambient'` to augment `App.Locals` with the five fields the engine reads and writes on every admin request: `cairnEditor`, `cairnBackend`, `cairnAuditSink`, `cairnAccess`, and `cairnIdentity`; Worker bindings are typed by the `Env` that `wrangler types` generates, which `src/app.d.ts` references, and a custom route reads them from `cloudflare:workers`, since adapter-cloudflare 8 declares no `App.Platform`. Source: `src/lib/ambient.ts:41-49` (`App.Locals` augmentation, five members), `templates/waymark/src/app.d.ts:1-10`. [verified]
- `f:pdgkex` `createSiteIndexes` throws at build time for any declared concept with no matching `import.meta.glob` passed in, because Vite needs the literal glob pattern at the call site and cannot have one added programmatically. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-indexes.ts:54-58` (throws `cairn: concept "${descriptor.id}" has no glob passed...` for any declared concept missing from the globs record). [verified]
- `f:n0laoh` The `cairnManifest` Vite plugin has two distinct failure modes, confirmed by reading the plugin directly. (1) It fails the build if the committed manifest has drifted from the markdown files on disk: in verify mode the plugin's virtual module runs `verifyManifest(built, committed)` inside `buildStart`, and `verifyManifest` throws when the built and committed manifests differ (after a `mediaRefs`/`references` migration-tolerant normalization). This throw fails the build as a hard error. (2) A concept declared in the adapter but missing from the plugin's own `content` option produces zero rows for that concept, with no throw: `buildSiteManifest` reads `globRecord[descriptor.id] ?? {}`, so a missing key falls back to an empty glob record and the concept's loop simply pushes no entries; nothing in the call path throws or warns. Both build-a-site-by-hand.md's drift-fails-the-build claim and declare-your-own-concept.md's missing-concept-produces-zero-rows claim are accurate as stated. Source: `src/lib/vite/internal.ts:53-80` (`virtualSource`, verify-mode `resultExpr`), `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:308-333` (`verifyManifest`), `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:18-33` (`buildSiteManifest`: `const record = globRecord[descriptor.id] ?? {};`). [verified]

Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:65-68` (`defaultPermalink`: `id === 'pages' ? '/:slug' : '/${id}/:slug'`), `:189` (`datePrefix: policy.datePrefix ?? 'day'`). [verified]

Source: `package.json:98-102` (`exports["."]`: `"svelte": "./dist/index.js"`, alongside the durable gotcha that shipped dist `.svelte` files still carry `lang="ts"` and need the consumer's own Svelte plugin). Source: Vite `define` substitution mechanics (string-literal replacement, not cross-module constant propagation). [external: Vite]

- `f:e8dr5r` A one-off environment variable is set on Windows with `set "NAME=1" &&` in `cmd.exe` and `$env:NAME=1;` in PowerShell, since the inline `NAME=1 command` form belongs to POSIX shells. The quotes around the whole assignment matter: `set` assigns everything after the equal sign, so the unquoted `set NAME=1 &&` stores `1` with a trailing space, while the quoted form stores the bare value without the quotes. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/set_1 ("assigns everything that follows the equal sign"), https://ss64.com/nt/set.html (the `SET "_variable=value"` form keeps the quotes out of the value, and extra spaces are not ignored), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_environment_variables. [external: Windows shells]

## docs/extend/choose-an-ai-posture.md

- `f:rh0xv5` Setting `aiPosture` on the adapter alone changes no output bytes; `robotsResponse` reads its posture from its own `posture` option, which the site's `robots.txt` route must pass through explicitly from `cairn.aiPosture`. Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts` (posture is a function parameter `opts.posture`, not read from any adapter object internally) plus page's own worked route example. [verified: against robots.ts's function signature reading `opts.posture`]
- `f:2edxez` Leaving `aiPosture` unset emits a `robots.txt` byte-identical to a site with no opinion (every cairn site's behavior before the field existed). Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts:36-37` (both `Content-Signal` lines are conditional on `posture === 'decline'` or `'invite'`; no posture emits neither). [verified]
- `f:vmphgq` `'decline'` emits a `Content-Signal: ai-train=no` line plus a `User-agent`/`Disallow: /` pair for each token in the engine's own training-crawler table (`AI_CRAWLERS`). Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts:22,36` (`CONTENT_SIGNAL.decline = 'ai-train=no'`; comment "`User-agent`/`Disallow` group per {@link AI_CRAWLERS} token plus a `Content-Signal` line declining training"). [verified]
- `f:qgtfu6` `'invite'` emits `Content-Signal: search=yes, ai-train=yes` and adds no crawler-specific `Disallow` lines, since no `robots.txt` directive grants access. Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts#CONTENT_SIGNAL` (`CONTENT_SIGNAL.invite = 'search=yes, ai-train=yes'`) and `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts#buildRobots`. [verified]
- `f:ibespi` The `Content-Signal` directive syntax follows Cloudflare's published Content Signals Policy: directive `Content-Signal`, keys `search`/`ai-input`/`ai-train`, values `yes`/`no`, pairs separated by comma-space; an absent key expresses no preference. Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts:4-7` (module comment citing the Cloudflare blog post). [verified]
- `f:duev87` The general-purpose `disallow` path list (e.g. `/admin`) is a separate mechanism from `aiPosture`; it always emits under the blanket `User-agent: *` group ahead of any posture-specific group, and has no way to target one named crawler. Source: page text; consistent with `robots.ts`'s separate `disallow` option existing alongside `posture`. [verified: structurally, exact group ordering not line-verified]
- `f:po5wx7` There is no per-crawler override or side door to add a training-crawler token beyond the fixed table `posture: 'decline'` iterates; cairn ships no crawler token it lacks first-party documentation for. Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts:34` (`buildRobots(opts: { sitemapUrl, disallow?, posture? })`, no per-crawler parameter), `:41` (`for (const crawler of AI_CRAWLERS) ...`). [verified]
- `f:6g7mpp` `buildRobots` emits one fixed line order: `User-agent: *`, then the posture's `Content-Signal` line (inside that same `*` group, only when a posture is set), then `Allow: /`, then one `Disallow: <path>` per `disallow` entry, then under `'decline'` one blank-line-separated `User-agent: <token>` / `Disallow: /` group per crawler, then a blank line and `Sitemap: <sitemapUrl>`. Run with `sitemapUrl: 'https://example.com/sitemap.xml'` and `disallow: ['/admin']`, no posture yields exactly the four lines `User-agent: *`, `Allow: /`, `Disallow: /admin`, blank, `Sitemap: https://example.com/sitemap.xml`; `'invite'` inserts `Content-Signal: search=yes, ai-train=yes` after `User-agent: *` and nothing else; `'decline'` inserts `Content-Signal: ai-train=no` there and appends seven groups in table order (Amazonbot, Applebot-Extended, CCBot, ClaudeBot, Google-Extended, GPTBot, meta-externalagent) before the `Sitemap` line. Source: `src/lib/delivery/robots.ts#buildRobots` (`const lines = ['User-agent: *'];`), output produced 2026-09-27 by running `buildRobots` under `npx tsx` for all three postures. [verified]
- `f:s62pyb` `AI_CRAWLERS` holds exactly seven training-crawler tokens, each with a first-party operator citation: Amazonbot (Amazon), Applebot-Extended (Apple), CCBot (Common Crawl), ClaudeBot (Anthropic), Google-Extended (Google), GPTBot (OpenAI), meta-externalagent (Meta). A token with no first-party documentation does not ship (Bytespider is absent for that reason), and search crawlers (Googlebot, OAI-SearchBot, Claude-SearchBot) are excluded, since disallowing one costs search presence for no training benefit. Four of the seven operators (Amazon, Anthropic, Google, Common Crawl) state outright that they honor robots.txt; the other three document it as the control without that promise. Source: `src/lib/delivery/ai-crawlers.ts:1-17` (module comment) and `src/lib/delivery/ai-crawlers.ts#AI_CRAWLERS` (`export const AI_CRAWLERS: readonly AiCrawler[] = [`). [verified]
- `f:fd7rln` `robotsResponse`, `buildRobots`, and the `AiPosture` type all import from `@glw907/cairn-cms/delivery` (which re-exports the whole of `/delivery/data`); `AiPosture` also exports from the root. `robotsResponse` passes `posture` to `buildRobots` unchanged and always sets `Content-Type: text/plain; charset=utf-8`. Source: `src/lib/delivery/index.ts:13` (`export * from './data.js';`), `src/lib/delivery/data.ts:27,64,69`, `src/lib/index.ts:44-48`, `src/lib/delivery/responses.ts#robotsResponse` (`headers: { 'Content-Type': 'text/plain; charset=utf-8' },`). [verified]
- `f:gnlib7` The scaffolded site's robots route (`src/routes/robots.txt/+server.ts`) is prerendered and calls `robotsResponse({ sitemapUrl: siteMeta.origin + '/sitemap.xml', disallow: ['/admin'], posture: (cairn as CairnAdapter).aiPosture })`, `siteMeta` imported from `#chassis/content.js`; the scaffold's adapter is exported as `cairn` from `src/theme/cairn.config.ts` (reachable as `#theme/cairn.config.js` through the `#theme` subpath import in `package.json`) and leaves `aiPosture` unset on purpose, with a comment saying to set it once decided. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/robots.txt/+server.ts:1-18`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:63,158-162`, `templates/waymark/package.json:5-9`. [verified]
- `f:1ij5h5` The scaffold's own `robots.txt` route already fulfills the pass-through `f:rh0xv5` names: it passes `cairn.aiPosture` to `robotsResponse`, cast to `CairnAdapter` since `defineAdapter`'s exact-literal return type drops an omitted key entirely rather than typing it `undefined`, so a scaffolded site needs no route edit to serve the posture the setup command wrote. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/robots.txt/+server.ts:16` (`posture: (cairn as CairnAdapter).aiPosture,`), `packages/create-cairn-site/template/src/routes/robots.txt/+server.ts` (the same route, baked into the packed tarball). [verified]
- `f:65atya` `cairn doctor`'s `ai.posture-effective` check compares the adapter's declared `aiPosture` (read from `src/content/.cairn/site-facts.json`) against the `/robots.txt` the public origin actually serves, and fails with `ai.posture-not-effective` (severity warning) only when a posture is declared and the served file carries no directives consistent with it, or directives consistent with the other posture. An unset posture always passes, and the report notes an outside layer (such as a managed robots.txt ahead of the origin) alongside the comparison. The condition's remediation: check that the robots route passes `aiPosture` to `robotsResponse` and that the build carrying it deployed. Source: `tool/internal/doctor/check_posture.go:211-239` (`evaluatePosture`), `src/lib/diagnostics/conditions.ts:134-140`, and that entry's `docsAnchor` field (the condition's own docs pointer). [verified]
- `f:87pdbt` Declining cannot stop a user-triggered fetch: OpenAI says of `ChatGPT-User`, "Because these actions are initiated by a user, robots.txt rules may not apply," and Perplexity says of `Perplexity-User`, "Since a user requested the fetch, this fetcher generally ignores robots.txt rules." Both re-read 2026-09-27. Source: https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/bots and https://docs.perplexity.ai/docs/resources/perplexity-crawlers, quoted in `src/lib/content/types.ts#AiPosture`. [external: OpenAI and Perplexity crawler documentation]
- `f:tno8hh` Cloudflare's managed robots.txt, when enabled on a zone, prepends its own content (including its own `Content-Signal` line) ahead of the origin's robots.txt in one combined response, so the served file can carry a posture the site did not write. Cloudflare's page: "Cloudflare will prepend our managed `robots.txt` before your existing `robots.txt`, combining both into a single response." Re-read 2026-09-27. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/bots/additional-configurations/managed-robots-txt/, and `src/lib/diagnostics/conditions.ts:139` (remediation names the prepending). [external: Cloudflare managed robots.txt]
- `f:k4p9ws` When `src/content/.cairn/site-facts.json` is absent, `cairn doctor`'s `ai.posture-effective` check reports unchecked, not failed, with the exact detail `needs engine 0.97.0 or later, and one build`. Source: `tool/internal/doctor/facts.go:19` (`const factsAbsentDetail = "needs engine 0.97.0 or later, and one build"`), `tool/internal/doctor/check_posture.go:297-298` (`if !found { return uncheckedResult(factsAbsentDetail) }`). [verified]
- `f:b970xo` `cairn doctor`'s `ai.posture-effective` check reports `UNCHECKED`, not failed, when it cannot read one of two inputs: `src/content/.cairn/site-facts.json` is absent, with the detail `needs engine 0.97.0 or later, and one build`, or the origin's `/robots.txt` could not be fetched, with the fetch's own reason (no origin resolves, the origin does not parse, the GET fails, or the response is non-200) as the detail instead. Source: `tool/internal/doctor/check_posture.go:289-306` (`AIPostureEffective.Run`: `uncheckedResult(factsAbsentDetail)` when site facts are not found, `uncheckedResult(postureRobotsAbsentDetail(s.Robots.Reason))` when `!s.Robots.Present`, `uncheckedResult(detailPostureBadOrigin)` when the resolved origin does not parse), `tool/internal/doctor/check_posture.go:240-249` (the four `detailPosture*` reason strings). [verified]
- `f:m2v7qd` `AiPosture` is the two-value union `'invite' | 'decline'`, and `CairnAdapter.aiPosture` is optional; absent (the default) the engine emits no posture the site did not declare. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:121` (`export type AiPosture = 'invite' | 'decline';`), `src/lib/content/types.ts:270-275` (`aiPosture?: AiPosture;` with its doc comment). [verified]

## docs/extend/configure-rendering.md

- `f:3l7f56` Every adapter declares exactly one `rendering.render` function (`SiteRender`); the edit page's live preview and every public page call the identical function, so there is no second render path to keep in sync. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:255,391` (`render: SiteRender`, one required field), `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:88-89,114` (`render?: SiteRender` prop: "The site's design-accurate render pipeline; the preview pane renders its output"), `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:184` (public routes call the same `render(...)`). [verified]
- `f:dmn2uu` `createRenderer(registry)` returns `renderMarkdown` (HTML string) and `renderDocument` (HTML plus collected headings in document order, `{ id, text, depth }`); an empty registry still renders plain markdown, GFM tables, `cairn:` links, and `media:` references correctly. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:86-105` (`createRenderer`, returns `renderMarkdown`/`renderDocument`; `registry: ComponentRegistry = defineRegistry({ components: [] })` defaults to empty). [verified]
- `f:s6n9zw` `defineComponent`'s `build` function returns a hast `Element` synchronously, runs inline inside the pipeline's tree transform once per occurrence, and cannot `await` anything; data a component needs must be fetched outside the render pipeline and passed through `attributes`. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts` `ComponentDef.build` (typed as returning an `Element`, not a `Promise`). [verified: against registry.ts type shape]
- `f:cxhx8w` `attributes` accepts exactly ten `fields.*` descriptor types that serialize to a single directive-attribute string: `text`, `textarea`, `number`, `select`, `url`, `email`, `date`, `datetime`, `boolean`, `icon`; everything else (`multiselect`, `object`, `array`, `reference`, `image`) throws at declaration. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:245` (error message: "a directive attribute must be a single-value scalar (text, textarea, number, select, url, email, date, datetime, boolean, or icon)"). [verified]
- `f:34wt4f` A component's inner CSS classes should be named apart from any DaisyUI component class name (`card-body`, `card-title`, etc.), because DaisyUI's plugin generates CSS for any matching class name found anywhere in scanned source, not only markup a component-typed element wraps; a directive reusing a DaisyUI class name gets silently restyled if the site enables that component elsewhere. Source: DaisyUI's Tailwind content-scan mechanism. [external: DaisyUI]
- `f:wvcw71` `RendererConfig` (`createRenderer`'s second argument) exposes `sanitizeSchema` (extends the sanitize allowlist), `tableScroll` (controls the scrollable table wrapper), and `remarkPlugins`/`rehypePlugins` (composed after cairn's own pipeline steps, before stringification). Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:48,68,74,81` (`sanitizeSchema?`, `tableScroll?`, `remarkPlugins?`, `rehypePlugins?`). [verified]
- `f:fqdknx` The directive vocabulary a component registers is container-only (`:::name[Title]{attrs} ... :::`); a leaf (`::name{attrs}`) or text (`:name{attrs}`) directive is always restored to its literal source text, since the pipeline treats any such form as an accidental colon in prose (a time, a ratio), never as a component invocation; `::include` is the one leaf form the pipeline reads, resolved in an earlier step before the restore. Source: `src/lib/render/remark-directives.ts:39-52` (`restoreLiteral`, called from the `['textDirective', 'leafDirective']` visitor at `:113-124`), `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:98-101`, `examples/showcase/src/theme/markdown-components.ts:114-117` (registry comment: "The directive vocabulary is container-only... a bare colon or double-colon directive always restores to literal prose, never dispatches"). [verified]
- `f:ps54gh` `SiteRender` takes `{ body, concept?, frontmatter?, resolve?, resolveMedia?, resolveFragment? }` and resolves to an HTML string only, with no headings; `renderMarkdown(body, { resolve, resolveMedia, resolveFragment })` fits it, and a route that needs the headings calls `renderDocument` directly instead. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:212-219`, `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:32-38,141-160`. [verified]
- `f:my6ci3` The edit page runs the site's render from an effect debounced on the live body and discards a stale result when a newer render has started; the public routes call the same render inside the entry loader, at build when the route is prerendered and per request otherwise. Source: `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:1344-1372`, `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:5-6,184`. [verified]
- `f:htxey1` `defineComponent` declares one container directive from a `ComponentDef`: a required `name`, `label`, `description`, and a `build` returning a hast `Element`, with optional `attributes`, `slots`, and `hydrate`; it validates the attribute types at declaration and throws on a bad one. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:52-66,231-260`. [verified]
- `f:0aatf6` In a component's `slots`, `title` is the directive's bracketed label and `body` is its default content, and both are handled specially by the directive grammar the editor's component-insert dialog serializes and reads; any other slot name is a named region. Source: `src/lib/render/rehype-dispatch.ts:76-92`, `src/lib/render/component-grammar.ts:37,47-50,117-129`. [verified]
- `f:zh8wdb` A component's `attributes` are validated by the same fieldset machinery a concept's fields use: `defineComponent` builds an `attributeSchema` with `defineFieldset(attributes, { behavior })`, which `validateComponent` runs. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:256-260`. [verified]
- `f:2dsv35` The context a component's `build` receives carries `attributes`, `slot(name)`, `items(name)`, `attr(key)` (a declared attribute's string value, undefined for a boolean or absent one), and `node`; the engine ships no hast-building helper beyond that, and a site builds markup with hastscript's `h()`. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts:36-49`, `examples/showcase/package.json:27`. [verified]

## docs/extend/content-model.md

- `f:phdgof` A concept's fieldset is the single source of truth for the editor's form, the server-side validator, and the inferred TypeScript type a site reads its own entries as; there is no separate schema language and no second place a field is declared. Source: `src/lib/content/fields.ts:145-180` (fifteen `fields.*` builders feeding `defineFieldset`), `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:417-421` (`defineFieldset` returns the descriptors plus a server-derived validator). [verified]
- `f:2hxm4t` The fifteen field builders are: text, textarea, number, select, multiselect, url, email, date, datetime, boolean, icon, image, object, array, reference. Source: `src/lib/content/fields.ts:145-180` (`export const fields = { text, textarea, ..., icon, image, object, reference, array, ... }`). [verified]
- `f:68fh1k` `object` and `array` are the two container builders, and nesting is capped at one level: an `object`'s sub-fields must all be leaves (no `object` or `array` inside it), an `array`'s item is a leaf or a flat object of leaves (an array of arrays throws), and a `reference` inside an `object` throws with the advice to model it as the parent's own concept or use a top-level `array(reference)`. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:385-415` (`checkContainerNesting`: "must be a leaf field; containers nest one level only", "an array of arrays is not allowed", "a reference inside an object is not supported"). [verified]
- `f:t4hh8w` A field key never contains a dot, in a container or at the top level, since a dotted path is how the engine addresses a nested field internally. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:390` (`if (k.includes('.')) throw new Error(...)`). [verified]
- `f:46edxb` An entry's id is its markdown filename with `.md` stripped; the on-disk filename is always exactly the id plus `.md`. A dated concept additionally carries a leading date on the filename (`2026-08-14-my-post.md`); the URL slug strips only that leading prefix. Source: `src/lib/content/ids.ts:1-3,18-20` (`idFromFilename`: "An entry's id is its markdown filename without `.md`"), `:44-58` (`DATE_PREFIX_RE`/`slugFromId`, already used for `f:z68na4`/`f:grhib4`). [verified]
- `f:zce3pt` The filename's date prefix only feeds the slug; an entry's actual date (used by dated permalinks and templates) always comes from the entry's own `date` frontmatter field, never the filename, which is why a dated permalink pattern makes `date` structurally required. Source: `src/lib/content/ids.ts:44-58` (`slugFromId` strips only the filename's leading date prefix), `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:141` (`e.date`, a distinct frontmatter-sourced field), `src/lib/content/concepts.ts` (`validateUrlPolicy`, a date-token permalink requires a `date` field, already `f:2i6u6o`). [verified]
- `f:s2zbzm` `routing` is one of three shorthands: `'feed'` (routable, dated, appears in feeds and the sitemap), `'page'` (routable, undated, the default when omitted), or `'embedded'` (not routable at all, no permalink, never listed or prerendered). Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:15-19` (`ROUTING_SHORTHANDS`: `feed`, `page`, `embedded`). [verified]
- `f:hdrzxd` A concept's fieldset produces a validator that the admin's save action runs against submitted form data before any commit; an invalid save returns only the first validation message, shown as one banner, and never reaches the Backend. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:116-118,181-184` (`saveToBranch` calls `concept.validate(decoded, body)` before any commit and refuses with the first value of `result.errors`), `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:1030` (the single banner), `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts` (the per-field validator built from the fieldset, already `f:phdgof`). [verified]
- `f:dccnyu` A `behavior.validate` function that throws is caught and the field is treated as valid rather than failing the whole save (a fail-open behavior on a throwing custom validator, distinct from the fieldset's own built-in validation which fails closed). Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:458-471` (`config.behavior[key].validate`'s throw is caught: "log and treat the field as valid rather than breaking the save"). [verified]
- `f:t5ebsr` The content manifest carries one row per entry with identity, routing facts, outbound `cairn:` links, reference-field edges, tags, and included fragments; a build regenerates and verifies it, and a publish patches its one changed entry in the same commit as the content change (corrected from "a save": a save to the pending branch commits no manifest change at all), so the manifest never drifts from the files it describes by more than one uncommitted edit. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:341-352` (`publishAction`: "a save commits no manifest, so the moment an entry goes live is this commit"). [verified]
- `f:a8ecm0` A `reference` field holds a typed pointer `{ concept, id }`; the manifest carries both sides of that edge so a rename or delete can warn about what still points at the referenced entry. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:11,34-39` (`extractReferenceEdges`, `ReferenceEdge[]` on each entry). [verified]
- `f:880bg6` Fragments are declared under the reserved `fragments` concept key and spliced verbatim into any entry that includes them with `::include{fragment="<id>"}`; a fragment has no permalink of its own and only reaches a reader through an including entry. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:28` (`FRAGMENTS_CONCEPT_ID = 'fragments'`), `:170-172` (throws unless `routing: 'embedded'`, "the include directive resolves against it"). [verified]
- `f:z4j689` A rename changes the entry's slug by moving the file, and rewrites every inbound `cairn:` token, in one atomic commit on `main`; it is refused with a 409 on a collision or while the entry has pending edits. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:1-5,225-231`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:65-66`. [verified]
- `f:xipx83` Renaming an entry is a git operation on the entry's holding branch. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:1-5` (delete and rename both mutate `main` directly, unlike the write cluster's pending-branch saves). [rejected: a rename commits to main and is refused while pending edits exist, so it never runs on the holding branch]
- `f:7bladx` A save patches the entry's manifest row in the same commit as the content change. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:341-352` (a save commits no manifest, and publish lands the upsert). [rejected: a save to the pending branch commits no manifest change; only publish patches the manifest, in the publish commit]
- `f:z5boz6` The files in git stay the source of truth for content; the committed manifest exists so request-time admin code reads the content graph without crawling GitHub entry by entry. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:1-5`. [verified]
- `f:2268rp` A container inside a container always throws at declaration, and an `array`'s item must be a leaf. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:385-415` (`checkContainerNesting` permits an `array` whose item is a flat `object` of leaves, and rejects only an `object` holding a container and an array of arrays). [rejected: an array may hold a flat object of leaves as its row shape, so array(object) is a container inside a container that declares cleanly]
- `f:im6x7r` `FieldDescriptor` is a closed union of the fifteen builder types and the engine exposes no seam to register another, so a field shape outside them has no local workaround. Source: `src/lib/content/fields.ts:121-137`. [verified]

## docs/extend/data-tiers.md

- `f:b6gquz` Three data tiers: Git (content entries, content manifest, media manifest, keyed by concept/id or content-hash prefix), D1 (`editor`/`magic_token`/`session` baseline plus `audit_log`/`preview_tokens` opt-in, keyed by email for `editor`, a SHA-256 token hash for `magic_token` and `preview_tokens`, a random session id stored as-is for `session`, and an integer row id for `audit_log`), R2 (uploaded media bytes, keyed by content hash, deduplicated). Source: `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `migrations/0002_audit.sql`, `migrations/0003_preview.sql` (D1 tier: `email TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, `token_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, `session.id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` written from `generateSessionId()` at `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:367-368` and read raw by `resolveSession` at `src/lib/auth/store.ts:223-231`, `audit_log.id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY AUTOINCREMENT`), `src/lib/media/store.ts:16,52` (R2 tier, already `f:2hnxsr`), `src/lib/content/compose.ts:12-13` (Git-committed manifests, already `f:lu67dk`). [verified: a summary of the three tiers, each independently verified elsewhere in this file]
- `f:pzbmhq` A token and a session id are both looked up by their SHA-256 hash, never the raw value. Source: `migrations/0000_auth.sql` (`token_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY` on `magic_token`, `id TEXT PRIMARY KEY` on `session` is a generated id, not a hash, but lookups go through `hashToken`), `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts:96-98` (`hashToken`: `crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', data)`). [verified]
- `f:xkkt1o` `role` used to carry a database `CHECK` constraint limiting it to `owner`/`editor`; `0001_roles.sql` rebuilds the table to validate against a site's declared role vocabulary at the application layer instead, so a site can extend roles without a further schema migration. Source: `migrations/0000_auth.sql` (`role TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('owner', 'editor'))`), `migrations/0001_roles.sql` (create-copy-drop-rename to `role TEXT NOT NULL` with no CHECK; comment: "Role validity moves to the app layer from here on"). [verified]
- `f:wnvqlz` `audit_log` (`0002_audit.sql`) holds one row per audited admin action (`actor`/`action`/`entity`/`entity_id`/`detail`), timestamped in ISO 8601 at millisecond resolution, written only by a site that wires `createD1AuditSink`. Source: `migrations/0002_audit.sql` (`CREATE TABLE audit_log (id, actor, action, entity, entity_id, detail, created_at TEXT DEFAULT (strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%fZ', 'now')))`; comment: "Opt-in: only a site that wires `createD1AuditSink`... needs this migration"). [verified]
- `f:dtwvtz` `preview_tokens` (`0003_preview.sql`) holds a hashed, single-use-per-link token naming the shared draft, swept on expiry the same no-cron way `magic_token` is; written only by a site that mints preview links. Source: `migrations/0003_preview.sql` (`token_hash TEXT PRIMARY KEY`, `expires_at INTEGER`, comment: "Opt-in: only a site that mints preview links... ever touches this table"; the expiry sweep comment cites the same idiom `idx_magic_token_email`/`idx_session_email` use). [verified]
- `f:2hnxsr` A site that declares a `media` block on its adapter gets an R2 bucket bound by name and resolved at request time from that declared binding, never a hard-coded one; the render resolver always returns the bare full-size delivery path, and attaches a responsive `srcset` (small fixed width ladder via Cloudflare Image Transformations) only when the zone has transformations on and the asset's width is known. Source: `src/lib/media/store.ts:16,52` (`r2Store(bucket: R2Bucket)`), `src/lib/render/resolve-media.ts:20,52,181` (`SRCSET_WIDTHS`, srcset built "once zone transformations are on and the asset's width is known"). [verified]
- `f:4t707i` The media manifest is keyed by a 16-hex content-hash prefix and is the dedup lookup an ingest checks before writing anything, distinct from the content manifest, which keys by concept and id. Source: `src/lib/media/manifest.ts:1-5,16-18` (module comment: "the dedup lookup: an ingest checks the content-hash prefix here before storing"; "16-hex content-hash prefix rather than concept and id"). [verified]
- `f:lu67dk` R2 holds media bytes because git does not carry binary assets at real scale and D1 is not sized for anything measured in megabytes; both manifests (content and media) stay committed JSON under `src/content/.cairn/`, and only the bytes they describe live in R2. Source: `src/lib/content/compose.ts:12-14` (`CONTENT_MANIFEST_PATH = 'src/content/.cairn/index.json'`, `MEDIA_MANIFEST_PATH = 'src/content/.cairn/media.json'`). [verified]
- `f:qrwncf` A site's own extension data (a member roster, an event schedule) is unconstrained by cairn's own three tiers, beyond the reserved `cairn_` cookie and table-naming conventions. Source: `src/lib/auth-channel/store.ts:51,63,80` (`cairn_channel_meta` table), `migrations-channel/0000_channel.sql` (`cairn_channel_*` table names); the `cairn_` prefix convention is consistent across every engine-owned D1 table and cookie name found in this pass. [verified]
- `f:6ign7q` No table in the five migrations stores an entry body, and a manifest row carries an entry's title, permalink, summary, links, and edges but never its body, so no copy of a body exists outside git. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:18-50`, `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `migrations/0002_audit.sql`. [verified]
- `f:rlrtfo` Every migration after `0000_auth.sql` is opt-in. Source: `src/lib/auth/store.ts:20-31` (a sign-in against an `AUTH_DB` without the `nonce_hash` column fails and names `0004_login_nonce.sql`). [rejected: `0004_login_nonce.sql` is required by every site; only `0001_roles.sql`, `0002_audit.sql`, and `0003_preview.sql` are opt-in]
- `f:0435ck` `audit_log.created_at` uses `strftime`'s ISO 8601 form (unambiguous UTC at millisecond resolution) rather than the source schema's `datetime('now')`, because that string parses as local time in a browser and sorts same-second rows unpredictably, and the column is meant to be read by people. Source: `migrations/0002_audit.sql:9-16`. [verified]
- `f:db0cx6` A media manifest row carries `displayName`, `originalFilename`, `alt`, `ext`, `contentType` (the stored MIME type the delivery route serves verbatim rather than guessing from the extension), `bytes`, and a nullable `width` and `height`. Source: `src/lib/media/manifest.ts:15-38`. [verified]
- `f:pgy0mr` cairn places each kind of state by what reads it: editor-authored content and both manifests are committed files in git; D1 holds auth and operational rows, the `session` row the guard looks up by its session-cookie id on each admin request (joined to its `editor` row by email) and the `magic_token` rows sign-in looks up by SHA-256 hash, plus the opt-in `audit_log` and `preview_tokens`, and never an entry body; R2 holds media bytes that the delivery route streams directly. Source: `src/lib/content/compose.ts:12-14`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:354-355`, `src/lib/auth/store.ts:223-231`, `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:348`, `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:18-50`, `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `src/lib/media/delivery-bucket.ts:1-3`. [verified]
- `f:70mf58` Every save and every publish is a git commit with the signed-in editor as author, so the repository's history records who changed each entry and when, with no audit table involved. Source: `src/lib/github/types.ts:20`, `src/lib/github/repo.ts:261-262`. [verified]

## docs/extend/debug-your-site.md

- `f:99x86q` Three diagnostic surfaces divide by what they key on: the admin's setup recovery page on `create-cairn-site`'s step names and state file, the admin's troubleshooting page on observed site behavior, and the developer debug page on log events whose cause is a site's own source code, all reading one log event vocabulary. Source: `docs/internal/record/harvest/extend/debug-your-site.json`. [rejected: describes a deleted page]
- `f:gy2h7p` The developer-facing log events (`content.field_unmarked`, `content.field_behavior_failed`, `media.resolver_absent`, `admin.action.unaudited`, `audit.sink.call_failed`, the rate-limit events, `admin.action.misconfigured`, `config.invalid`) fire from engine code running inside a site's own extension points (a concept's `fieldset`, a `createSectionAction`-wrapped route, a hand-built public route factory); none describes an engine bug, each reports that surrounding site code violated the contract. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:466` (`content.field_behavior_failed`), `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:47,210` (`media.resolver_absent`), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:308-309` (`admin.action.unaudited`), `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:216-265` (rate-limit events), `src/lib/log/events.ts:30` (`config.invalid`); each fires from a call site inside a site-supplied hook, config, or route wrapper. [verified: a summary of the individually verified events]
- `f:lbeeak` `content.field_unmarked` (warn) fires when a concept declares a multiselect field named `tags`, `freetags`, or `categories` but no field carries `taxonomy: true`; the tag index only reads the field marked that way. Fix: add `taxonomy: true` to the actual tag field. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:350-362` ("At most one top-level multiselect feeds a concept's taxonomy... there is no field-name default, since the record key is arbitrary"), `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:14,101` (`TAG_FIELD_NAMES` is `tags`, `freetags`, `categories`; the warn fires for an unmarked one). [verified]
- `f:qdfs37` `content.field_behavior_failed` (warn) fires when a field's `behavior.validate()` throws during save-time validation; the engine catches the throw and treats the field as valid rather than failing the whole save, so the bad value lands. The `owner` field names the schema and `error` carries the exception message. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:458-471` (`log.warn('content.field_behavior_failed', { field, owner, error })`). [verified]
- `f:twqb0s` `media.resolver_absent` (warn, once at construction) fires when a public route factory is built with media configured (adapter declares a `media` block) but no `resolveMedia` function was wired into it; every `media:` reference then has nothing to resolve against, rendering a literal `media:` token. Source: `src/lib/delivery/public-routes.ts:47,210` (`log.warn('media.resolver_absent')`). [verified]
- `f:07efts` `admin.action.unaudited` throws `UnauditedActionError` in dev the moment a `createAdminAction`-wrapped route returns without calling `ctx.audit`; in production it logs at error instead of throwing, since a redirect or error response mid-request would be worse than a gap in the audit trail. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:308-309` (`if (dev) throw new UnauditedActionError(...); log.error('admin.action.unaudited', ...)`). [verified]
- `f:r3l7eq` `audit.sink.call_failed` (error) fires when the site's own `event.locals.cairnAuditSink` throws synchronously or returns a rejected promise; `ctx.audit` already ran and the action completed, only the record was lost. If using the packaged `createD1AuditSink`, check `audit.sink.write_failed` instead, since that sink catches its own failures before this event fires. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:255-280` (`audit.sink.call_failed`: fires when `event.locals.cairnAuditSink` throws or rejects; "the mutation this record describes already completed"), `src/lib/sveltekit/audit-sink.ts:66` (`createD1AuditSink` is fail-open, catches its own failures before this event fires). [verified]
- `f:i1dayf` `admin.action.rate_limit_absent` and `admin.action.rate_limit_failed` (both warn) both degrade to open (never block) on purpose: the former fires when the configured rate-limit binding resolves to nothing or its `key()` call throws; the latter when the resolved limiter's own `limit()` call throws (carries the `error` field). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:216-256` (`admin.action.rate_limit_absent` logged when `!limiter`; `admin.action.rate_limit_failed` logged in the `key()`-throw and `limit()`-throw catch blocks). [verified]
- `f:nup3og` `admin.action.misconfigured` (error) fires with `reason: 'db_not_bound'` when `config.resolveDb` returns null/undefined, or `reason: 'access_map_not_attached'` when `event.locals.cairnAccess` was never set (the admin guard never ran on this route). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts:277,291` (`misconfigured('rejected: access map not attached', 'access_map_not_attached')`, `misconfigured('rejected: database not bound', 'db_not_bound')`). [verified]
- `f:8fyoha` `config.invalid` (error) fires when committed site config fails to parse or validate; two loads (`scope: 'nav'`, `scope: 'vocabulary'`) degrade to an empty result rather than failing the screen, and two saves (`scope: 'settings'`, `scope: 'vocabulary'`) answer `fail(500)` with generic copy while the log record carries the parser's real error message. Source: `src/lib/log/events.ts:30` (`'config.invalid'`), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-settings.ts:151,168` (`config.invalid` with `scope: 'settings'`). [verified]
- `f:ogz5eu` A visual-regression baseline that reads the live system clock (`Date.now()` inside a component or route a test also renders) drifts daily even with no source change; the fix is a fixed-today seam read only from the Worker env (`CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY`, through `cloudflare:workers`), never `Date.now()` directly. Source: `grep -rn CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY src/lib` finds no symbol, so the seam is a site-authored testing recipe with no corresponding engine code. [verified]
- `f:yomgrm` `new Date(env.CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY)` yields `Invalid Date` silently on a malformed value rather than throwing; write the string as an explicit instant (`2026-08-29T00:00:00Z`), not a date-only form, since a date-only string parses as UTC midnight and formats as the previous local day on machines west of UTC. Source: JavaScript `Date` constructor parsing rules. [external: JavaScript]
- `f:swjwxb` Adapter-cloudflare 8's worker passes no `platform`, so `event.platform` is undefined everywhere; a site reads its Worker env from `cloudflare:workers`, which returns a `wrangler.jsonc` `vars` value under `vite dev` (through the adapter's platform proxy) and under `wrangler dev` alike, so a `CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY` pin set there reaches a site's reader in both with no `process.env` fallback. Source: `docs/superpowers/research/2026-10-03-sveltekit-3-spike/record.md` (items 1 and 7), `src/lib/sveltekit/workers-env.ts`. [external: SvelteKit adapter-cloudflare 8, measured in the spike record]
- `f:2r6juw` `Date`'s local-time accessors and `toLocale*String` formatting without a `timeZone` option use the host's time zone, so one instant formats as different days on runners in different zones; formatting in UTC or an explicit fixed zone gives every runner the same string. Source: https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Date/toLocaleDateString. [external: JavaScript]
- `f:f7tkkw` Setting `CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY` in `wrangler.jsonc`'s `vars` keeps the date pin out of production. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/environment-variables/, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/development-testing/environment-variables/. [rejected: `vars` in the Wrangler config deploy with the Worker, so a pin there reaches production; a local-only value goes in `.dev.vars`, and a CI-only value in the CI job's own environment]
- `f:iw346n` The fixed-today helper can fall back to `process.env.CAIRN_FIXED_TODAY` unguarded when `platform.env` carries no pin. Source: `templates/waymark/wrangler.jsonc:1-6`, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/runtime-apis/nodejs/process/. [rejected: `process` is not a global on a Worker without the `nodejs_compat` flag, which the template's Wrangler config does not set, so an unguarded read throws a ReferenceError exactly when the pin is unset, in production; guard it with `typeof process !== 'undefined'`; the engine's own reads take the Worker env alone]

## docs/extend/declare-your-own-concept.md

- `f:r03vis` Posts and Pages are not reserved names; the concept set is entirely the site's own, each a key in the adapter's `content` map, and the admin nav, create affordances, and delivery layer follow whatever name is used. Two engine conveniences do key on the literal ids `posts`/`pages` (the default permalink pattern and the onboarding checklist), but a site can still rename or replace either concept freely; only those two conveniences stop applying. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:65-68` (`defaultPermalink`, `id === 'pages'` gets a distinct default), `src/lib/content/getting-started.ts:25-28` (onboarding checklist keys on `'posts'`/`'pages'`), `src/lib/content/types.ts:229` (`content: Record<string, ConceptConfig>`, any id accepted). [verified]
- `f:4mz4tp` `createSiteIndexes` throws at build time for any declared concept with no matching `import.meta.glob` pattern passed in, since Vite needs the literal glob at the call site and it cannot be added programmatically. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-indexes.ts:54-58` (same throw as `f:pdgkex`). [verified]
- `f:7az6m2` If a site also builds the manifest with the `cairnManifest` Vite plugin, a concept missing from that plugin's own `content` option fails silently: the manifest simply never gets any rows for that concept, with no throw and no build warning (the mirror-image failure mode to the plugin's drift-detection throw; see the settled `cairnManifest` fact under build-a-site-by-hand.md). Source: `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:18-33` (`buildSiteManifest`: `const record = globRecord[descriptor.id] ?? {};`, so an absent key silently yields no entries). [verified]
- `f:6e06p2` `routing: 'page'` gives each entry its own routable, undated page and puts it in the sitemap; `'feed'` is for dated, chronological content appearing in feeds and an archive; `'embedded'` declares a concept with no public page at all (not routable, not in the sitemap, not in any feed), better suited to a reference-only concept like `authors` that is never browsed on its own. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:15-19` (`ROUTING_SHORTHANDS`, same as `f:s2zbzm`). [verified]
- `f:y3s3t9` The one reserved embedded concept is `fragments`, which the `::include` directive resolves against. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:28,170-173` (`FRAGMENTS_CONCEPT_ID = 'fragments'`, throws unless `routing: 'embedded'`). [verified]
- `f:vs77a6` An `'embedded'` concept still has its own directory, fields, and admin list/editor exactly like a `'page'` concept; the only thing `'embedded'` removes is a public page. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:16-19` (`ROUTING_SHORTHANDS.embedded = { routable: false, dated: false, inFeeds: false }`, the only fields the shorthand changes; `dir`, `fields`, admin wiring are unaffected by `routing`). [verified]
- `f:i2k4jp` A `reference` field connecting two concepts renders as a picker scoped to the target concept in the editor; on the public side, a route resolves the id to the target's title and permalink and renders a link. Source: `src/lib/content/fields.ts:109-110,174-175` (`ReferenceField`, `reference: (o) => ({ type: 'reference', ...o })`). [verified]
- `f:a98mnn` The scaffolded site's `npm run check` runs `svelte-kit sync` then `svelte-check` against its tsconfig, which typechecks its adapter's declared fields, and `npm run build` runs `vite build`. Source: `templates/waymark/package.json:9,14`. [verified]

## docs/extend/define-an-adapter-and-schema.md

- `f:hkh101` `defineAdapter` requires four groups: `content` (concepts), `backend` (where commits land), `email` (magic-link sender), and `rendering` (markdown to HTML); `media`, `roles`, `access`, `aiPosture`, and `editor` are optional. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:226-274` (`CairnAdapter`: `content`, `backend`, `email`, `rendering` required; `roles?`, `access?`, `media?`, `aiPosture?`, `editor?` optional). [verified]
- `f:gwmmkx` `dir` names the concept's markdown directory and `label` names it in the admin nav and create button ("New Posts"); when `singular` is omitted it defaults to the plural `label` itself, which reads awkwardly ("New Posts" instead of "New post"), so set `singular` too. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:186` (`singular: config.singular ?? label`). [verified]
- `f:lkgubp` `defineConcept` validates at declaration time, not at first save: a bad `permalink`, `datePrefix`, or `routing` throws the moment the module loads. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:39` (`throw new Error('cairn: concept "${id}" routing "${routing}" must be one of feed, page, embedded')`) among other declaration-time throws in the same normalization pass. [verified]
- `f:2i6u6o` A `permalink` using a date token (`:year`, `:month`, `:day`) requires a `date` field on the concept; `defineConcept` throws at declaration if missing or the wrong type, and normalizes it to `required: true` automatically since the permalink cannot resolve without a value. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts` (`validateUrlPolicy`, called at declaration time from `defineConcept`'s normalization pass; date-token permalinks paired with the dated-routing enforcement). [verified]
- `f:cg1eo5` `datePrefix` (`'year'`, `'month'`, or `'day'`) controls how much of the date lands in the filename stem, independently of the permalink shape, so a concept can carry a full date in frontmatter while only prefixing the filename with the year. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:189` (`datePrefix: policy.datePrefix ?? 'day'`, a field distinct from `permalink`). [verified]
- `f:twcsgm` `owner`, `repo`, `appId`, and `installationId` passed to `createGithubApp` are plain, non-secret identity; the private key that signs commits stays out of `cairn.config.ts` entirely, read from the Worker secret `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` at request time. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:87,104` (non-secret identity), `src/lib/env.ts:31` (private key stays a Worker secret), `src/lib/github/credentials.ts:18` (`env.GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` read at request time). [verified]
- `f:68w9k9` `defineAdapter` narrows the return type to a site's concrete concepts and fields, so a read against e.g. `cairn.content.posts` carries the declared field types through with no cast. Source: `src/lib/content/adapter.ts:28` (`export function defineAdapter<const A extends CairnAdapter>(adapter: A): A`, a `const` type parameter preserving the concrete shape). [verified]
- `f:g39nkp` A concept's default permalink is `/<id>/:slug`, except the concept with id `pages`, which defaults to `/:slug`; a concept overrides it with its own `permalink`. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:65-68`. [verified]
- `f:sz71ur` A concept declares at most one `taxonomy: true` multiselect, and only at the top level: a second marker, or a marker inside an `object` or `array`, throws at declaration. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:350-376`. [verified]
- `f:z7lprs` The adapter's `email` group is a sender config of `from` (the address magic-link emails come from) and an optional `replyTo`, and a send needs Cloudflare Email Sending onboarded for that sender's domain. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:123-127`, `src/lib/email.ts:68`. [verified]
- `f:u4cfvv` The scaffolded site's `src/theme/cairn.config.ts` is one `defineAdapter` call: `content` holds `posts` (dir `src/content/posts`, label `Posts`, singular `post`, `routing: 'feed'`, and a fieldset with a required `title`, `date`, a `description` textarea, and a creatable `taxonomy: true` multiselect among other fields), `pages`, and `fragments`; `backend` is one `createGithubApp` call; `email` is `{ from }`; and `rendering.render` forwards `body`, `resolve`, `resolveFragment`, and `resolveMedia` (falling back to the public media resolver) to the `renderMarkdown` that `createRenderer(registry)` returns, beside `components: registry`. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:31,37,63-94,119,137,149,153,170-176`. [verified]

## docs/extend/design-your-site.md

- `f:p8hsnz` Two keys govern every heading: `--font-weight-heading` (a Tailwind `@theme` key that generates `font-weight` for the `font-heading` utility) and `--cairn-heading-case` (default `none`, a plain role in `@layer theme`, since `text-transform` has no Tailwind namespace, applied by the `heading-case` utility the chassis `tokens.css` declares). Chrome markup applies `font-heading heading-case`; a scoped `<style>` rule reads `var(--font-weight-heading)` and `var(--cairn-heading-case)` directly. `prose.css` headings and the hero title read the same keys, so a theme that sets `--cairn-heading-case: uppercase` uppercases every heading. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/tokens.css:75-89`, `examples/showcase/src/chassis/prose.css:79,103,117`, `examples/showcase/src/chassis/composition.css:88`. [verified]
- `f:i9pgd2` The theme toggle's `resolveTheme` returns the live `data-theme` when it names one of the config's two themes; otherwise it reads the root's computed `color-scheme`, splits it into words, drops `only`, and resolves a single `light` or `dark` (so `only dark` counts as `dark`); a value that is `normal` or holds both words (`light dark`) falls back to the OS scheme. A dark-first theme therefore resolves dark on a light OS with no edit to the page shell. The two theme names, the cookie name, and the toggle's config live in one file, `theme-names.ts`, and `theme-names.test.ts` fails when `app.html`'s cookie regex or the two `theme.css` block names drift from it. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/theme-toggle.ts:28-42`, `examples/showcase/src/theme/theme-names.ts:15-21`. [verified]
- `f:nz87b3` The chrome's skip link is an `sr-only` anchor to `#main` that becomes visible with `focus:not-sr-only focus:absolute` (the utilities sort in that order in the generated CSS, so the focused link is positioned, not left as `sr-only`'s static box), and `<main id="main" tabindex="-1">` makes the target focusable so activating the link moves keyboard focus and not only scroll position; the focus is programmatic, so `main:focus` draws no ring while real controls keep their `:focus-visible` rings. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/(site)/+layout.svelte:72-98`. [verified]
- `f:faofr4` The editor's preview frame paints `body{margin:0;background:var(--color-base-100,#fff)}`, so the preview ground follows the site's own `base-100` once the site's stylesheet loads; the `#fff` fallback covers a styleless preview or a site that does not define the key. The frame's `<html>` carries no `data-theme`, so only the OS color scheme reaches it. `cairn-audit` does not scan the embedded style string; `preview-doc.test.ts` and the `preview-tokens` e2e guard it. Source: `src/lib/admin/preview-doc.ts:99-105`. [verified]
- `f:rxj43c` A scaffolded site ships Waymark, cairn's public reading template, wired into a chassis of genre-free plumbing, because the setup command bakes both from `templates/waymark/`; the npm package's `files` list carries no template and no chassis, so a site built by hand from `sv create` starts with neither and brings its own theme and delivery wiring. Source: `templates/waymark/src/chassis/`, `packages/create-cairn-site/scripts/bake-template.mjs`, `package.json:203-216`. [verified]
- `f:s4prb0` `src/chassis/` holds the plumbing every site shares regardless of look, one concern per file: `content.ts` (content indexing), `feed.ts` (the posts-to-feed-item mapping), `public-routes.ts` and `entry-data.ts` (the shared public-route config and reference edges), `cairn.server.ts` (server runtime composition), `dev-gate.ts`, `render.ts`, `archive.ts`, `date.ts`, `theme-toggle.ts`, and the token *system* and reading and composition CSS (`tokens.css`, `prose.css`, `composition.css`, whose primitives are card, band, section, hero, sidebar-layout, and site-shell); everything outside `src/chassis/` (the concrete adapter config, chrome components, color and type values, and page compositions) is Waymark's own content. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/README.md:1-13,22-40`, enforced by `npm run check:chassis-boundary` (`scripts/checks/check-chassis-boundary.mjs`). [verified]
- `f:lwrqfd` A theme file reaches chassis only through exported seams: the `#chassis` subpath import in TypeScript/Svelte, or a relative `@import` in CSS. cairn's own repo enforces this boundary on the showcase with a `check:chassis-boundary` gate; a scaffolded site inherits it as a convention only, with no gate of its own. Source: `scripts/checks/check-chassis-boundary.mjs:2-13` (parses the canonical seam table from `examples/showcase/src/chassis/README.md`, walks every `examples/*/src` with its own `chassis/` copy); the top-level `package.json` wires the script, and a scaffold produced by `packages/create-cairn-site` carries no such script of its own. [verified]
- `f:iel6v5` `tokens.css` declares the design-scale keys (`--font-*`, `--text-step-*`, `--spacing-*`, `--leading-*`, `--tracking-*`, `--container-measure*`, `--font-weight-heading`), each with a generic default. The roles (`--color-muted`, `--color-card-border`, the status inks, the shadow, the focus ring) come from the engine's `cairn-public.css`, which `tokens.css` imports right after Tailwind. A theme `@import`s `tokens.css` first, then redeclares the same keys with its own numbers in a later `@theme` block, and cascade order does the override. Named DaisyUI themes (role colors, geometry values) are never declared in chassis at all.
