Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/tokens.css:51-52` (`@import "tailwindcss"`, then `@import "@glw907/cairn-cms/cairn-public.css"`), `:91-98,122-131` (the `@theme` block declaring `--font-weight-heading`, `--leading-*`, `--tracking-*`, `--container-measure*`), `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:59-60` (`--color-muted` and `--color-card-border`). [verified]
- `f:kt0epf` The committed re-skin recipe is about fourteen values, light and dark: rotate `--color-primary`'s hue while holding lightness and chroma, edit the `base-100/200/300` ladder plus `base-content`; optionally swap the two `--font-*` tokens or retune one type ratio or space-scale step. `prose.css` comes along for free at zero extra edits since it reads the same role tokens. Source: `examples/showcase/src/theme/theme.css` (the site's own `@plugin "daisyui/theme"` block carrying `--color-primary`/`base-100/200/300`/`base-content`), `examples/showcase/src/chassis/prose.css:1-6` (module comment: "every element bound to the DaisyUI roles and the cairn tokens in theme.css"). [verified]
- `f:dh5f33` Waymark's two re-skin face tokens are `--font-display` and `--font-body` in the `@theme` block of `src/theme/theme.css`; the file self-hosts each face from a Fontsource variable package (`@fontsource-variable/figtree`, `@fontsource-variable/source-sans-3`, `@fontsource-variable/source-code-pro`, each a `devDependencies` entry in the scaffold's `package.json`) through an `@import` of the package's `index.css` (`@import "@fontsource-variable/figtree/index.css"` and its two siblings) placed directly under the chassis import, since those `@import` rules must precede every other rule, and each token names the family Fontsource registers ("<Name> Variable") ahead of a `system-ui` stack, so a token naming a face whose package is not installed and imported renders in the next family of its stack with no error. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/theme.css:32,90-100,212-215`, `templates/waymark/package.json:29-31`, https://www.w3.org/TR/css-fonts-4/#font-family-prop (a family the user agent cannot match falls through to the next in the list). [verified]
- `f:3v9jzj` A status fill is one of four daisyUI theme keys, `--color-info`, `--color-success`, `--color-warning`, and `--color-error`, which Waymark sets in both its light and dark daisyUI blocks and from which the engine's sheet derives the matching `--cairn-<status>-ink`. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/theme.css:46-49,129-136,181-187`, `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:42-45`. [verified]
- `f:ylmc9c` A full status-color rebrand costs one fill per status, light and dark: the engine derives each on-surface ink (`--cairn-{info,success,warning,error}-ink`) from its fill at 50 percent, so retuning a fill retunes its ink. Only a theme that overrides an ink in its daisyUI block has to retune the fill and the ink together, or the directive text and code-highlight colors desync from the fills. Source: `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:42-45` (`--cairn-success-ink: color-mix(in oklab, var(--color-success) 50%, var(--color-base-content))`, and the three sibling inks). [verified]
- `f:xv2ien` Two CI gates hold the re-skin recipe honest on cairn's own showcase: `check:public-tokens` (the audit's three public-scope rules, `public-literals`, `theme-conformance`, and `theme-contrast`, run from the packaged audit over the showcase, again with the `examples/cairn-theme` overlay layered after `theme.css`, and a third time over a copy of the showcase carrying the fixture theme, failing on any unsuppressed finding at any tier) and `test:reskin` (the same `theme-contrast` measurement over a hue-rotated theme and over Waymark with its four hand-tuned inks stripped, plus a check that prose reads from one token source). Those two scripts do not ship in a scaffolded site's own `package.json`, but the three public rules do ship in `cairn-audit`, and the scaffold's `check:cairn` script runs it, at advisory tier on a consumer. Source: `package.json:62,72` (`"check:public-tokens": "npm run package && node scripts/checks/check-public-scope.mjs"`, `"test:reskin": "npm run package && node scripts/lab/reskin-fixture.mjs"`, both top-level engine scripts absent from `packages/create-cairn-site`'s template), `scripts/checks/check-public-scope.mjs:44-48` (`VARIANTS`: the showcase, the showcase with the cairn-theme overlay, and the showcase copy with the fixture theme), `templates/waymark/package.json:16` (`"check:cairn": "npm run build:admin-css && cairn-audit"`), `docs/reference/cairn-audit.md:99-101` (the three rules at advisory tier). [verified]
- `f:bdnzcy` `/styleguide` is the live demo surface: it renders every registered directive from the registry (a sample for each entry that declares a `preview`, serialized as directive markdown, and the entries without one listed by name), plus the type scale and component recipes, against the current `theme.css`. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/(site)/styleguide/+page.server.ts:97-109` (the loop over `cairn.rendering.components?.defs` with `previewMarkdown(def)`, `withoutPreview`). [verified]
- `f:7pv6se` `cairn-media-seed --from <url>` seeds wrangler's local R2 state from a deployed site's media library; it is idempotent (a re-run overwrites the same keys with the same bytes), and a site with nothing deployed yet has nothing to seed. Source: `src/lib/media-seed/assemble.ts:14,33,39,44` (`--from` flag; comment: "overwrites an earlier one"). [verified]
- `f:ctognq` `composition.css` exposes primitives `.cairn-card`, `.cairn-band`, `.cairn-section`, `.cairn-hero`, `.cairn-sidebar-layout`, each exposing its own `--cairn-<primitive>-*` custom properties for a per-instance override on top of shared tokens. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/composition.css:18-28` (`.cairn-card { --cairn-card-padding: ...; --cairn-card-radius: ...; }`). [verified]
- `f:kj37zz` `feed.ts` has exactly two dependents (the two feed routes) and can be deleted along with those route bodies with nothing else changing; `content.ts` is load-bearing for six delivery routes at once and is not a bare deletion, requiring all six routes' imports to be replaced in the same change. `src/chassis/README.md` documents the dependents table for eleven of its files. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/README.md:251-252` (dependents table: `content.ts` -> "Every delivery route... a theme dropping it must replace all six route imports"; `feed.ts` -> "`feed.xml/+server.ts`, `feed.json/+server.ts`... Delete the file and the two feed route files"). [verified]
- `f:kq6ud3` Waymark's `theme.css` names three token tiers by how far a re-skin reaches: Tier 1 is the two daisyUI theme blocks, Tier 2 (the owned floor) is the on-surface inks, the elevation pair (`--color-card-border` and `--cairn-shadow`), the CTA pair (`--cairn-cta-*`), and the code-highlight binding, and Tier 3 is the design-scale keys that override the chassis defaults; the status inks are hand-tuned per scheme in the two daisyUI blocks, and the CTA pair never reads `--color-neutral` directly because it inverts to a light value. Source: `examples/showcase/src/theme/theme.css:74-88,335-341`. [verified]
- `f:krq13h` The chassis holds the feed and sitemap builders. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/README.md:22-40`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/sitemap.xml/+server.ts:2-14`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/feed.xml/+server.ts:2`. [rejected: the chassis holds only `feed.ts`, the mapping from the posts index to the feed-item shape, while the builders are the engine's `rssResponse`, `jsonFeedResponse`, and `sitemapResponse` on `/delivery`, which the route files call; the chassis has no sitemap file]
- `f:kt11l7` Every file in the chassis names its real dependents in the README's dependents table, so a removal is a bounded, checkable edit. Source: `examples/showcase/src/chassis/README.md:22-40,239-262`. [rejected: the dependents table has rows for eleven of the chassis's thirteen documented files, and `public-routes.ts` and `entry-data.ts` have no row, so their removal notes are not recorded there]
- `f:l2mbcj` In a scaffolded site the directive registry (`src/theme/markdown-components.ts`, listed by `cairn.config.ts`), the chrome (`SiteHeader.svelte`, `SiteFooter.svelte`), and the page compositions (`ArticleView.svelte`, `EntryRow.svelte`, and the `(site)` route pages) are files in the site's own tree that the setup command copies in and the npm package does not ship, so no engine version governs how they look; the daisyUI plugin is limited to the four components the template renders (button, badge, alert, card), and the showcase the template is baked from builds its own home and article views the same way. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/`, `examples/showcase/src/chassis/tokens.css:54-75`, `package.json:203-216`. [verified]
- `f:l41gju` After seeding, the scaffold's `npm run dev` renders every page with real content and real images. Source: `templates/waymark/scripts/dev.mjs:1-10,26-30`, `templates/waymark/src/hooks.server.ts:17-23`, `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/handle.ts:131-187`. [rejected: `npm run dev` sets `CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND=1`, which turns the dev backend handle on, and that handle replaces `MEDIA_BUCKET` in the Worker env (through `withEnv`) with its in-memory fake bucket for every `/admin`, `/media`, and `/preview` request, so the media route never reads the wrangler local R2 state that `cairn-media-seed` fills; seeded objects serve under `vite dev` or `wrangler dev` run without the opt-in]
- `f:l9mf1w` Media uploaded through the local `/admin` lands directly in the local R2 bucket, so a site with nothing deployed needs no seeding. Source: `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/handle.ts:131-187`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-media-shared.ts:105-116`. [rejected: under the dev backend the upload action writes into the handle's in-memory fake bucket, which lives only for the server process, so an upload through the local admin is lost on restart and never reaches wrangler's local R2 state]
- `f:mvkyea` Vite hot module replacement applies a changed stylesheet or component to the running page without a manual reload. Source: https://vite.dev/guide/features#hot-module-replacement. [external: Vite]
- `f:mzmvt8` `theme-contrast` (advisory tier) measures every text-bearing pair a public theme paints against WCAG AA at 4.5:1, in sRGB and display-p3 and in every scheme the theme defines, plus the focus-ring color against 3:1, and reports a value it cannot place as unmeasured instead of passing it; it reads the site's real import chain and needs `daisyui`. Source: `src/lib/audit/rules/static/theme-contrast.ts:1-20,32-38`. [verified]
- `f:blhd7f` `PreviewConfig`, the adapter's `editor.preview` member, has four members: `stylesheets` (required, the absolute or root-relative URLs linked inside the preview document), `bodyClass` (a class list on the preview document's body), `containerClass` (a class list on one wrapper element around the rendered content; omitted, the content renders bare), and `byConcept` (a map keyed by concept id whose entries carry an optional `bodyClass` and an optional `containerClass`). `resolvePreview` applies an entry's concept override key by key with nullish coalescing, so an absent or undefined override key keeps the top-level value; the stylesheets are always the shared top-level list, and the `byConcept` map never reaches the client. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts#PreviewConfig`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-read.ts#resolvePreview`. [verified]
- `f:spn4hj` The scaffold's adapter sets `preview: { stylesheets: [themeCss, siteCss], containerClass: 'site-main prose' }`, with `themeCss` and `siteCss` imported from `./theme.css?url` and `./site.css?url`. The `(site)` layout's `<main>` carries `site-main` and `ArticleView.svelte` wraps the entry in `<article class="prose">`, while the preview frame renders one wrapper element, so both classes go on that one element. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:28-29,220-227`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/+layout.svelte:87`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/components/ArticleView.svelte:108`. [verified]
- `f:f28x0x` A site imports `@glw907/cairn-cms/cairn-public.css` once, after `@import "tailwindcss"` and before its own reading-surface sheet, so a later declaration in the site's own `@theme` block wins over the sheet's two colors. The sheet declares no daisyUI theme variable (its only custom properties are `--flow-space`, the `--cairn-*` roles, `--color-muted`, and `--color-card-border`) and activates no daisyUI plugin, yet its roles read daisyUI roles such as `--color-base-content` and `--color-primary`, so a site without the chassis installs daisyUI and supplies its own `@plugin "daisyui/theme"` block. Source: `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:1-5,21-62`. [verified]
- `f:hva8r5` The engine sheet declares its roles in `@layer theme` on `:root, [data-theme]`, the lowest layer Tailwind declares, so the same key written anywhere else, unlayered in `:root` or in a daisyUI theme block, overrides the engine's default, and the `[data-theme]` selector recomputes a role inside a nested theme region. Source: `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:7-10,21-23`. [verified]
- `f:18qj2u` Waymark's styleguide call-to-action panel reads the five `--cairn-cta-*` keys (`--cairn-cta-bg`, `--cairn-cta-content`, `--cairn-cta-border`, `--cairn-cta-btn-bg`, `--cairn-cta-btn-content`) and its header navigation links read `--cairn-caption-tracking`, and neither the engine sheet nor the chassis `tokens.css` defaults any of the six, so the theme declares each. Waymark declares them unlayered in `:root` and repeats the dark CTA values, beside the comma-bearing shadow and dark card border that the daisyUI plugin's option parser cannot carry, in a media-guarded `:root:not([data-theme])` block and a `:root[data-theme="cairn-dark"]` block kept identical by hand. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/styleguide/+page.svelte:743-745,767-768`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/components/SiteHeader.svelte:162`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/theme.css:314-393`. [verified]
- `f:h5e8d4` The chassis file `src/chassis/tokens.css`, not a theme file, activates the daisyUI plugin (`@plugin "daisyui" { themes: false; exclude: ... }`) and excludes every daisyUI component key except four (`button`, `badge`, `alert`, `card`), where `alert` serves the markdown directive's classes and `card` serves a showcase fixture route the template excludes; the component set is chassis-level and shared by every theme, so a theme that adopts an excluded component, such as a `.tooltip` or a `.modal`, drops that component's key from the `exclude` list in `tokens.css`. Source: `templates/waymark/src/chassis/tokens.css:18-35,57-72`. [verified]
- `f:u893cs` Waymark hand-tunes all four status inks (`--cairn-success-ink`, `--cairn-warning-ink`, `--cairn-error-ink`, `--cairn-info-ink`) in both its light and dark daisyUI blocks, overriding the engine's derived defaults, so a Waymark status rebrand retunes each ink with its fill, or deletes the override and lets the ink follow the fill. Source: `templates/waymark/src/theme/theme.css:143-146,192-195`. [verified]
- `f:4xptbu` `cairn-media-seed --from <url>` downloads each manifest row from under `<url>/media/`, the fixed path `<url>/media/<slug>.<hash>.<ext>`, and never reads the adapter's `assets.publicBase`, so a site whose media route is mounted at another path cannot seed with it, and the command has no flag to change the path. Source: `src/lib/media-seed/assemble.ts#downloadUrl` (`/media/`), `src/lib/media-seed/assemble.ts:9-10`. [verified]

## docs/extend/enable-tidy.md

- `f:glluwk` Tidy is opt-in, off by default, and never runs without an editor explicitly triggering it on a draft. Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:124-125` `TidyConfig.enabled` doc comment: "Master switch. Default false; tidy is opt-in (spec 2.8, decision 1)." The "never runs without an editor explicitly triggering it" half is consistent with `tidyAction` (`src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts`) being a server action invoked only from the editor's own Tidy button, with no scheduled or automatic call site found under `src/lib`. [verified]
- `f:vcthwj` Turning tidy on requires `npm install @anthropic-ai/sdk`, an optional peer dependency. Source: `package.json:206,212-214` (`peerDependencies["@anthropic-ai/sdk"]`, `peerDependenciesMeta["@anthropic-ai/sdk"].optional: true`). [verified]
- `f:avtt69` `tidy.enabled: true` is set in `site.config.yaml`, and `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is bound as a Worker secret via `npx wrangler secret put ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`. Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts#TidyConfig` (the `tidy` block on the site config, `enabled` the master switch), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-settings.ts:123` (`DEFAULT_SITE_CONFIG_PATH`, the `site.config.yaml` file), `src/lib/sveltekit/platform-bindings.ts:47` (`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY?: string`). [verified: the config key, the file, and the binding name trace to source; the `wrangler secret put` command is Cloudflare's own secret-write command]
- `f:59aion` `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` is the one optional member of `CairnPlatformBindings`; every other binding in that type is required. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/platform-bindings.ts`. [verified]
- `f:fmv6m8` The default tidy model is `claude-sonnet-5`. Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:133` `export const DEFAULT_TIDY_MODEL = 'claude-sonnet-5';`. [verified]
- `f:4c92fh` `claude-haiku-4-5` is the documented cheaper/faster alternative model. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-settings.ts:132` (`'claude-haiku-4-5': 'Claude Haiku'`) and `src/lib/admin/CairnTidySettings.svelte:401`. [verified]
- `f:ssdn1f` Tidy runs at the "low" effort tier by default, since a proofread does not need extended reasoning. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts` (`output_config: { effort: 'low' }` per doc comment on `supportsEffort`). [verified]
- `f:wlohtk` Only models in `claude-sonnet-5`, `claude-opus-5`, `claude-sonnet-4-6` (by prefix) or `claude-opus-4-<n>` with n >= 6 accept the `output_config.effort` parameter; other models (including `claude-haiku-4-5`) reject it with a 400. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts:57-68` `supportsEffort()`. [verified]
- `f:3yte99` `tidy.conventions` builds a set of style rules beyond the always-on objective fixes (spelling, grammar, doubled words, stray whitespace, capitals, terminal punctuation); only `fixes` defaults on, every other convention defaults off. Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:174-176` `defaultTidyConventions()` returns `{ fixes: true, enDashRanges: false, smartQuotes: false, brandCaps: false }`; every other field (`oxfordComma`, `numberStyle`, `measurements`, `percent`, `emDash`, `ellipsis`, `timeFormat`) stays `undefined` (off) unless set. [verified]
- `f:sdz8sm` Convention fields: `fixes` (boolean, default true), `oxfordComma` (`always`/`complex-only`/`never`), `numberStyle` (`under-ten`/`under-hundred`/`always-numerals`), `measurements` (`abbreviate`/`spell-out`), `percent` (`sign`/`word`), `emDash` (`spaced`/`closed`), `enDashRanges` (boolean, default false), `ellipsis` (`single-char`/`three-dots`), `timeFormat` (three literal formats), `smartQuotes` (boolean, default false), `brandCaps` (boolean, default false). Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:142-176` `TidyConventions` interface and `defaultTidyConventions()`. [verified]
- `f:xijdh6` An editor's personal dictionary lives in a separate committed file, `src/content/.cairn/dictionary.txt`, not a config choice. Source: `src/lib/content/compose.ts:14` `const DICTIONARY_PATH = 'src/content/.cairn/dictionary.txt';` and `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-context.ts:402` (same literal default). [verified]
- `f:q412re` Before a proposed tidy change reaches an editor, cairn checks the model output against the original: directive structure, heading count/levels, fenced-code-block count, frontmatter byte-for-byte, the `media:` token multiset, and every code span must match, plus a wording-divergence bound; a failing result never reaches the review screen. Source: `src/lib/admin/tidy-validate.ts` `validateTidy()` (checks `directiveSignature`, `headingSignature`, frontmatter span equality, `mediaHashes` multiset, `codeContents` multiset, then the `divergence` floor/fraction bound). [verified]
- `f:f5548q` The tidy input cap is roughly 24,000 characters (about 8,000 input tokens under Sonnet 5's tokenizer); a draft over that is refused before the model is ever called. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts:44` `const MAX_TIDY_CHARS = 24_000;`. [verified]
- `f:6zqexn` The settings screen's key-health check caches its result for ten minutes. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/tidy-key-health.ts:19` `const TTL_MS = 10 * 60 * 1000;` (governs both the unhealthy-key mark and the settings-load probe cache). [verified]
- `f:38qxp3` In local development the Anthropic SDK is never actually called; the dev backend injects a stubbed Anthropic client. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-context.ts:135,371` (`lazyAnthropicClient` dynamic-imports the real `@anthropic-ai/sdk` and is the only default; `config.tidy?.client` is a caller-supplied override), `templates/waymark/src/chassis/cairn.server.ts:22-28` (the scaffold passes a canned client only under the dev-build flag plus the dev-backend opt-in), `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/handle.ts:185` (the dev handle supplies only a dummy key). [rejected: no engine or dev-backend code swaps the client on its own; a plain `npm run dev` without both the dev-build flag and the dev-backend opt-in calls the real SDK given a real key, exactly as production does]
- `f:noqkmx` The tidy review shows the proposed copy-edit as a diff, one hunk per change: objective hunks (spelling, doubled word, whitespace, punctuation) come pre-kept, judgment hunks default to undecided and are never swept by "Accept fixes" until the author confirms each; the author's original stays in the editor buffer and nothing is written until Apply, which lands the kept hunks as one undoable step, while Cancel and Reject all leave the document byte-identical. Source: `src/lib/admin/TidyReview.svelte:2-12` `@component` block. [verified]
- `f:ymsfzv` The optional `@anthropic-ai/sdk` peer is imported lazily at call time, so a site that never installs it builds cleanly, and a tidy call without it refuses with the install command (`Run npm install @anthropic-ai/sdk.`) and logs `tidy.failed` with `reason: 'sdk_missing'`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-context.ts:125-144` `lazyAnthropicClient`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts:195-197`. [verified]
- `f:z2vl1z` No current CLI or doctor check reads or probes `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`: the `config.tidy-key-missing` condition is still registered, but no check under `src/lib` or `tool/` emits it. Source: `src/lib/diagnostics/conditions.ts:126-132` (registry entry only); `grep -rn tidy-key-missing src/lib tool` finds only the registry and `tool/internal/spine/condition.go:31`, with no emitter. [verified]
- `f:rcoz6t` The `/admin/settings` tidy screen actively probes the key with a zero-token `models.list` call and renders one of three states: the editor-tier conventions when tidy is on and the key is present and not confirmed invalid; a "Tidy is not set up yet" gate when tidy is off or the key is missing; or a distinct "Tidy's key isn't working" state when Anthropic rejects a present key. An unverifiable probe (`unknown`) fails soft and keeps the editor tier open. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/tidy-key-probe.ts:11-27` `probeTidyKey`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-settings.ts:241-253`, `src/lib/admin/CairnTidySettings.svelte:6-17`. [verified]
- `f:mg0n21` When Anthropic rejects the key (401 or 403) during a tidy call, the action returns a non-retryable refusal telling the editor to let their site developer know, logs `tidy.failed` with `reason: 'auth'`, and marks the key unhealthy so the next edit load hides the Tidy button for the cache window. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts:201-210`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-read.ts:496` (`enabled: (runtime.tidy?.enabled ?? false) && !keyKnownUnhealthy()`). [verified]
- `f:rdenuk` A tidy call refuses before any model call when tidy is off ("Tidy is not enabled for this site.") or the key is missing ("Tidy is not configured: the Anthropic API key is missing."), and an over-long draft refuses with "This is too long to tidy at once. Select a passage and tidy that instead." Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-tidy.ts:130,135,151`. [verified]
- `f:0j54bd` The tidy model is a developer-tier setting shown read-only on the settings screen with a plain-language label (for example "Claude Sonnet 5"); the editor-tier settings save writes only the `tidy.conventions` block back to the committed site config YAML, leaving `tidy.enabled` and `tidy.model` untouched. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-settings.ts:30-31,65-71,265-271`. [verified]
- `f:625qj8` Convention semantics as the prompt applies them: `oxfordComma: complex-only` follows the AP complex-series rule; every `numberStyle` threshold keeps numerals for ages, dates, measurements, and percentages; `measurements` changes only the notation, never the measurement system or the number; `smartQuotes` applies the full apostrophe rule set; `brandCaps` corrects only names on a curated list. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/tidy-prompt.ts:86,100,106,142,148`, `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:150-171`. [verified]
- `f:s6av65` The structural validator is a rewrite and injection backstop, never a voice safeguard; the config-driven prompt is what protects voice. A rejected result shows "Tidy returned a result that changed more than the wording, so it was discarded. Your text is unchanged." Source: `src/lib/admin/tidy-validate.ts:7-11,39-40`. [verified]
- `f:jay6aq` Adding a word to the personal dictionary is an action in the spellcheck suggestion popover ("Add to dictionary"), not in the tidy review, and spellcheck is configured separately under `spellcheck` in the site config (regional spelling is `spellcheck.dialect`, not a tidy toggle). Source: `src/lib/admin/spellcheck.ts:264`, `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:88-92,140`. [verified]
- `f:x4lexa` Setting `tidy.conventions.fixes: false` turns off the objective fixes, so Tidy skips spelling, grammar, and the rest of that group. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/tidy-prompt.ts:76-79` (the objective fixes live in the always-on core; the group toggle is a screen control that does not strip the core), `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts#TidyConventions` (the `fixes` doc comment: the always-on core governs the group). [rejected: `fixes` is read only by the settings screen's on/off chip and never reaches `conventionLines`, so the objective fixes run whatever the value]
- `f:tna81y` The tidy prompt maps the remaining conventions to one rule line each: `percent` `sign` or `word` picks the `%` sign or the word `percent`; `emDash` `spaced` or `closed` sets the spacing and turns a double hyphen into one em dash; `enDashRanges: true` turns a hyphen between two numbers into an en dash; `ellipsis` `single-char` or `three-dots` picks the ellipsis form; `timeFormat` is one of the literals `'5 PM'`, `'5pm'`, `'5 p.m.'` and renders times as the chosen literal. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/tidy-prompt.ts:114-138`, `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts#TidyConventions`. [verified]
- `f:66alqp` An editor adds a word to their personal dictionary from inside the tidy review, so a name Tidy would flag stops flagging in later passes. Source: `src/lib/admin/spellcheck.ts:264` (the `Add to dictionary` action is defined in the spellcheck suggestion popover), `src/lib/admin/TidyReview.svelte` (the tidy review carries no dictionary action). [rejected: the action belongs to the spellcheck suggestion popover and the tidy review has none, see `f:jay6aq`]
- `f:mbw7mk` A scaffolded site's `cairn.server.ts` builds the tidy client from `createFakeAnthropic` (`@glw907/cairn-cms-dev`) only when `__CAIRN_DEV_BUILD__` and the dev-backend opt-in are both true, so tidy calls a canned stub instead of the Anthropic SDK; a default production build drops the block. The dev backend's own handle sets a dummy `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` presence flag so the tidy action does not refuse before it builds a client. Source: `templates/waymark/src/chassis/cairn.server.ts:22-28`, `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/handle.ts:185`, `packages/cairn-cms-dev/src/fake-anthropic.ts:25`. [verified]

## docs/extend/link-content-with-references.md

- `f:563h9u` A `reference` field stores another entry's permanent id in frontmatter, typed to one concept, declared via `fields.reference({ label, concept, required })`. Source: `src/lib/content/references.ts` (`ReferenceEdge`, `extractReferenceEdges`). [verified]
- `f:3zswxq` `array(reference)` renders in the editor as a removable chip list. Source: `src/lib/admin/ReferenceField.svelte:6` ("`array(reference)` renders a removable chip list, each chip showing the target's resolved title"). [verified]
- `f:9i99mg` The engine records each reference as a `ReferenceEdge` (`{ field, concept, id }`), with `concept` taken from the field's own declaration rather than the stored value. Source: `src/lib/content/references.ts:22` (`export interface ReferenceEdge`) and `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:93` (`extractReferenceEdges(frontmatter, descriptor.fields)`). [verified]
- `f:gk88bv` The reference rewriter is byte-preserving: it splices the changed id into frontmatter by source offset rather than a YAML round-trip, so renaming a target updates every referencing entry's stored id without touching other bytes. Source: `src/lib/content/references.ts:2-3` ("this module surgically rewrites that id on rename. The rewriter is byte-preserving: it splices the value substring by source offset rather than round-tripping through gray-matter"). [verified]
- `f:nibxrd` `resolveReferences(site, descriptor, frontmatter)` resolves a `reference` field to one `ResolvedReference`, and an `array(reference)` field to an array of them in edge order. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts:159` `export function resolveReferences(...)` and `:137` `projectReference`. [verified]
- `f:mpdshq` `resolveReferences` drops an id with no live target rather than throwing, since a build already guarantees every committed reference resolves. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts:155` (doc comment: "build's `verifyReferences` gate already fails a true dangling edge, so an unresolved id at request..."). [verified]
- `f:6ggh4v` `verifyReferences` walks the manifest and throws, naming the source entry, field, and missing target, the moment a build runs it; a dangling reference fails the build. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:388` `export function verifyReferences(manifest: Manifest): void`. [verified]
- `f:l32sb1` Unlike a `cairn:` link, a reference has no prerender-time resolver fallback, so the build gate is its only integrity check. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts:155` doc comment (`resolveReferences` drops an unresolved id rather than throwing, deferring entirely to `verifyReferences` at build) contrasted with `src/lib/render/resolve-links.ts`'s `cairn:` resolver, which runs a live resolve at render/prerender time. [verified]
- `f:2zl9zw` The delete guard checks two things before deleting an entry: inbound `cairn:` body links from the published manifest, and inbound reference edges from a cross-branch index built over the manifest plus every open `cairn/*` edit branch. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:102-138` (`inboundLinks(manifest, ...)` against the published manifest, then `buildReferenceIndex(backend, runtime.concepts, manifest, { strict: true })` unioning main plus every open branch). [verified]
- `f:7s52oz` The cross-branch reference index build runs with `strict: true`, and a read error during the index build refuses the delete rather than counting as an absent reference (fails closed). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:134-138` (`buildReferenceIndex(..., { strict: true })`; catch returns `fail(503, { error: 'Could not verify where this entry is referenced. Try again.' })`). [verified]
- `f:8on7h4` An unpublished draft's reference edge still counts: deleting or renaming its target is refused even though nothing about the edge is live yet. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:127-138,272-286` (the strict index unions "main's published edges" with every open `cairn/*` branch before the refusal check). [verified]
- `f:re5qlm` Renaming refuses outright, naming the blocking entries, when a different open branch than the entry's own holds an inbound edge. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:282-286` ("Refuse when a THIRD-PARTY open branch holds an inbound reference (symmetric with the pending-edits guard)"). [verified]
- `f:bz5j8f` Saving an entry whose `reference` field names a missing or still-draft target only warns; a body `cairn:` link to a missing target refuses the save outright. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:246-273` ("a link to a draft target commits with a warning... A reference NEVER blocks the save: unlike a body link, an absent or draft target only warns") with `fail(400, ...)` at line 261 for the body-link case. [verified]
- `f:5fs5o7` Each manifest entry records its outbound `cairn:` link edges in `links` and its typed frontmatter reference edges in `references` (`{ field, concept, id }` each); `references` is omitted for an entry with no reference fields, and a manifest committed before the field still parses. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts#ManifestEntry` (`links: CairnRef[]`, `references?: ReferenceEdge[]`). [verified]
- `f:jxgm4i` A `ResolvedReference` carries the target's `id`, `concept`, `title`, and `permalink`, plus an optional `summary`, so a public route renders a reference as a link from `permalink` and `title`. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts#ResolvedReference`. [verified]
- `f:2g0v8u` The delete action checks in this order: inbound `cairn:` body links from the published manifest (409, `Cannot delete <id>: N pages link to it.`); for a fragment, inbound `::include` edges (409); then a strict cross-branch reference index, refusing with 503 when that index cannot be built; then inbound reference edges (409, `Cannot delete <id>: N entries reference it.`), each blocker carried as the referencing entry's concept, id, title, and permalink. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:104-158`. [verified]
- `f:et9w8d` The delete refusal for an inbound reference reports each referencing entry together with the name of the referencing field. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:146-158` (each blocker is mapped to `concept`, `id`, `title`, and `permalink` only). [rejected: the reference index rows carry `field`, but the refusal drops it, so the blockers list names entries and never fields]
- `f:bbovmp` A single `reference` field renders a combobox-style button showing the current target's resolved title, and opening it shows an entry picker scoped to the field's declared concept; picking sets the stored id. Source: `src/lib/admin/ReferenceField.svelte:2-9` (the `@component` comment). [verified]
- `f:2dilwu` A `reference` or `array(reference)` field must name a concept declared under the adapter's `content`: `normalizeConcepts` throws, naming the concept, the field, and the undeclared target, so a typo fails at composition rather than at the build's `verifyReferences` gate. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts#normalizeConcepts` (`which is not declared under content`). [verified]
- `f:z7n6u8` A concept declaration's `dir`, `label`, and `singular` are all required fields. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts#ConceptConfig` (`label?: string`), `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:182,186` (`config.label ?? defaultLabel(id)`, `config.singular ?? label`). [rejected: only `dir` and `fields` are required; `label` defaults from the concept id and `singular` defaults to `label`]
- `f:yw7rs6` A `cairn:` link whose target is missing never degrades silently: when the render's link resolver returns nothing, as the editor preview's does, the link renders marked broken (class `cairn-broken-link`, href neutralized, text kept), and when the resolver throws, as the build's does, the render fails; the save guard refuses a body link to a target absent from `main` because publishing the entry first would fail the deploy build, while a missing `reference` target is dropped from the resolved list with no mark. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-links.ts#remarkResolveCairnLinks` (`cairn-broken-link`), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:243-247` (the save guard's comment), `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts#resolveReferences`. [verified]

## docs/extend/migrate-existing-content.md

- `f:qd7iat` cairn ships no importer; migration is done with a short script using `parseMarkdown`, the same parser cairn's own save path uses, to split frontmatter from body. Source: `src/lib/index.ts:53` `export { parseMarkdown } from './content/frontmatter.js';`. [verified]
- `f:3r6rqn` `serializeMarkdown` is an internal save-path detail, not a public export; a migration script must write the target `---`-delimited frontmatter block directly instead. Source: `src/lib/index.ts` (grep for `serializeMarkdown` finds no such export; only `parseMarkdown`, `formatManifest`, `parseManifest`, `verifyManifest`, `verifyReferences` are exported from `content/`). [verified]
- `f:z68na4` An entry's id is its filename stem, verbatim, the only identity cairn gives it. Source: `src/lib/content/ids.ts:1-3` ("An entry's id is its markdown filename without `.md`, so there is no slug codec") and `:18-20` `idFromFilename`. [verified]
- `f:grhib4` cairn strips only a date prefix at the concept's declared `datePrefix` granularity: a file named `2024-03-15-my-trip.md` under `datePrefix: 'month'` strips only `2024-03-`, leaving day digits (`15-my-trip`) in the slug; this never throws or fails a build. Source: `src/lib/content/ids.ts:44-58` `DATE_PREFIX_RE` (`month: /^\d{4}-\d{2}-/`) and `slugFromId()` (a non-matching prefix is simply not stripped, no throw). [verified]
- `f:nhjraz` A filename outside the lowercase-letters/digits/single-internal-hyphens shape still serves its own page but is invisible to a `cairn:` link and to the rename/delete guards, since each treats that id as unparseable. Source: `src/lib/content/ids.ts:6-10` (`ID_RE`/`isValidId`), `src/lib/content/links.ts:28` (`!isValidId(token.id)` returns null, so a `cairn:` token to such an id is no target), `src/lib/content/cross-branch-index.ts:94`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:222,244` (delete and rename refuse an id failing `isValidId`), `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:105` (`entryId(file.path)` takes the served id from the filename with no shape check). [verified: the shape gate and each consumer trace to source, and serving follows from the index taking the id from the path unchecked]
- `f:xl7d9s` cairn has no bulk media importer; an old image URL left as-is in a migrated body renders unchanged, since the render pipeline passes through anything that is not a `media:` token or recognized image source. Source: no media-importer module exists anywhere under `src/lib` (grep for "media importer"/"bulk import" finds nothing); `src/lib/render/resolve-media.ts:1-5` only visits and rewrites nodes carrying a parsed `media:` token (`parseMediaToken`), leaving every other image `src` untouched as it flows through the rest of the pipeline. [verified]
- `f:my9dgx` Validate migrated entries by starting the dev server against the local double and opening each migrated entry in the admin; a field that fails validation shows its error inline. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:116-118,181-185` (a failed save returns only the first field's message as one refusal line), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-read.ts` (the edit load calls no validator). [rejected: opening an entry runs no validation, and a failed save reports only the first field's message as a single refusal line, not an inline error per field]
- `f:w77gj8` `parseMarkdown` returns `gray-matter`'s `data` as `frontmatter` and its `content` as `body`, so the body is everything after the closing `---` (its leading newline kept) and YAML scalars arrive typed: an unquoted timestamp such as `2024-01-15T00:00:00-07:00` becomes a `Date` object, and a bare `2024-01-15` becomes a `Date` at UTC midnight. Source: `src/lib/content/frontmatter.ts#parseMarkdown`, run 2026-09-29 against `gray-matter` with both date shapes. [verified]
- `f:ettk66` `parseMarkdown` hands back the old file's YAML values exactly as written, so an unquoted `date:` stays the same string the file held and `String(frontmatter.date).slice(0, 10)` yields `YYYY-MM-DD`. Source: `src/lib/content/frontmatter.ts#parseMarkdown` (returns `parsed.data` from `gray-matter`). [rejected: `gray-matter` parses YAML, so an unquoted timestamp arrives as a `Date` object and `String(date)` begins with a weekday and month name, not the ISO date]
- `f:ivhuyc` A `date` field validates as a calendar date in exactly `YYYY-MM-DD` form (a `Date` from `parseMarkdown` is coerced to its UTC date first, a string with a time part fails with `must be a valid date (YYYY-MM-DD)`), while a `datetime` field accepts the trimmed string as written with no format check. Source: `src/lib/content/fieldset.ts:113-119,282-286,306-308` (`coerceToText`, the `date` and `datetime` arms of `validateField`), `src/lib/content/frontmatter.ts#isCalendarDate`. [verified]
- `f:2p519o` A configured tag vocabulary holds lowercase hyphenated slugs as values (`SAFE_TAG_VALUE`), a save may add only a vocabulary value to an entry's taxonomy field, and an entry's existing tags outside the vocabulary are kept as orphans rather than refused. Source: `src/lib/nav/site-config.ts:28,405` (`SAFE_TAG_VALUE`, the vocabulary value check), `src/lib/content/taxonomy-enforce.ts:11-25,28-30` (`resolveAllowed` unions the vocabulary with the entry's prior tags, `unlistedTags` names the orphans). [verified]
- `f:0nw43j` An entry's draft state is the frontmatter key `draft: true`: the manifest and the content index read `frontmatter.draft === true`, and any other value or an absent key reads as published. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:110` (`draft: frontmatter.draft === true`), `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:106` (`const draft = raw.draft === true;`). [verified]
- `f:y8qw8f` A migrated `draft: true` has no cairn counterpart as a frontmatter key, because cairn's draft state is controlled only through the admin and is never written by hand. Source: `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:110`, `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:106`. [rejected: the draft state is the frontmatter key `draft: true` itself, so a Hugo or Jekyll `draft: true` carries over unchanged]
- `f:4ikssa` The content index validates each entry's frontmatter and body with its concept's validator; an entry that fails is recorded as a content problem (with its field errors) and left out of the typed read, so one bad file degrades to a skip, not a crash. Source: `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:104-114`. [verified]
- `f:xjdhau` The media library uploads one image file per action: the upload dialog's file input has no `multiple`, and a chosen or dropped file is stored and committed in one step; the asset is then referenced by a `media:` token resolved through its content hash, and the library shows its where-used list and edits its default alt. Source: `src/lib/admin/MediaUploadDialog.svelte:2-19,217-223`, `src/lib/render/resolve-media.ts:82-99` (`findByHash` resolves a `media:` ref through the manifest), `src/lib/admin/CairnMediaLibrary.svelte:1-24`. [verified]
- `f:tg9e0z` A site's own reusable body block is a component declared with `defineComponent`, collected by `defineRegistry`, and invoked in an entry body only as a container directive (`:::name ... :::`): the pipeline stamps a registered container directive for dispatch and restores a leaf (`::name`) or text (`:name`) directive to its literal source text. Source: `src/lib/render/registry.ts#defineComponent`, `src/lib/render/registry.ts#defineRegistry`, `src/lib/render/remark-directives.ts:39-52` (`restoreLiteral`), `src/lib/render/remark-directives.ts#remarkDirectiveStamp`. [verified]
