## docs/extend/organize-your-admin-nav.md

- `f:ys1iq1` `navLayout` is declared on the adapter's `editor` group in `cairn.config.ts`; a site declaring no `navLayout` gets a flat, unsectioned list of concepts and engine screens, resolved through the same function a declared layout uses. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts:300,418` (`navLayout?: NavLayout` under the adapter's `editor` group), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:574-578` ("Synthesize the flat zero-config default arrangement for a site that declares no navLayout"). [verified]
- `f:nvpcmk` `navLayout` is an ordered array mixing three node kinds: an engine reference (`{ screen: '<id>' }`), a site entry (`{ label, icon, href }`), and a section (`{ label, children }`, one level deep, no nesting). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:144` `export type NavLayout = (NavLayoutEntry | NavLayoutEngineRef | NavLayoutSection)[];` (`NavLayoutSection.children` at `:132` typed `(NavLayoutEntry | NavLayoutEngineRef)[]`, i.e. one level deep). [verified]
- `f:jsig0m` An engine reference's `screen` can name a declared concept or one of the fixed utility screens: `media`, `vocabulary`, `nav`, `settings`, `editors`, `help`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:100` `const ENGINE_SCREEN_IDS = ['media', 'vocabulary', 'nav', 'settings', 'editors', 'help'] as const;` (the full `navLayout` engine-reference universe, validated by `validateNavLayout`). [verified]
- `f:hsk70g` The navLayout screen-id universe resolved: `navLayout`'s six engine-reference ids (`media`, `vocabulary`, `nav`, `settings`, `editors`, `help`) and the access map's four enforced ids (`media`, `vocabulary`, `nav`, `settings`) are deliberately different sets, not a contradiction between the two pages. `ACCESS_FIXED_SCREENS = ['media', 'vocabulary', 'nav', 'settings']` (`src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:280`) is the set `validateAccessComposition` accepts as an access-map key alongside real concept ids; `editors` and `help` are excluded by design, per the doc comment at `admin-nav.ts:276-278`: "`editors` stays owner-only regardless of the map (canReach's own floor) and `help` stays open to any editor capability; neither route reads the map, so the map cannot name them." So a `navLayout` reference to `editors`/`help` is valid nav wiring that the access map can never gate directly (their fixed capability floor is the only enforcement). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:100,276-280,301-322` `validateAccessComposition`. [verified]
- `f:s4kynf` `icon` on a site entry must be a name from the bundled allowlist documented on `NavIcon`. Source: `src/lib/admin/admin-nav-icons.ts` (grep hit for `navLayout`, contains the icon set). [verified: file exists and is the icon-set module referenced]
- `f:ld6xam` The whole `navLayout` tree validates once at runtime composition; a bad icon name, a colliding `href`, an unknown screen id, or an out-of-vocabulary role name fails the build with an actionable, `navLayout`-prefixed message. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:179` `export function validateNavLayout(...)`, `:79-93` `validateEntry` (throws `navLayout: icon "..."` and `navLayout: href "..." collides...`), `:203-211` `checkRoles` (throws on an out-of-vocabulary role name). [verified]
- `f:yf1rrr` A screen the tree never references still renders, in a trailing fallback group after a divider, in engine order; reference a screen with `hidden: true` to remove it from the sidebar without removing the route. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:563-568` (the `fallback` array collects every unreferenced, visible engine screen in `engineScreenOrder(opts)`) and `:118-121,530` `NavLayoutEngineRef.hidden` (`if (node.hidden || !engineVisible(...)) return null;`, so the route itself is untouched, only the sidebar entry drops). [verified]
- `f:bvfs3e` Hiding a screen from the nav is never authorization; the route itself must still be denied via the access map for a real deny. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:118-121,530` (`hidden` removes only the sidebar entry, the route itself is untouched, already `f:yf1rrr`), `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:457-486` (`requireAccess`, the actual enforcement). [verified]
- `f:542r4m` A section's `collapsed: true` sets its starting state only for a visitor with no persisted nav-collapse cookie; once toggled, the cookie carries the whole collapsed set and wins over the declared default in both directions. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:135-140` (`NavLayoutSection.collapsed` doc comment: "Whether this group starts collapsed for a visitor with no persisted nav-collapse cookie... the `cairn-admin-nav-collapsed` cookie carries the full collapsed set and wins entirely"). [verified]
- `f:qzbfrd` `roles` on a section gates every child at once, on top of each child's own gate, and never widens what a child would otherwise show, only narrows; a site entry can carry its own independent `roles`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:519,534,542,604` (a section gates all children via `roleMatches(node.roles, ...)` before rendering any child, each child then re-checked against its own `roles` at `:534`; `NavLayoutEntry.roles` at `:61` is independent per-entry). [verified]
- `f:zyjzeg` `ownerOnly` on a nav site entry is cosmetic only: it hides the link from non-owner sessions but gates nothing server-side; the route itself must still gate. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:59,69,465-467,533` (`ownerOnlyVisible` gates only the resolved nav tree's visibility). [verified]
- `f:rwefuj` A `navLayout` engine reference takes `label` (relabels the door without touching its engine-owned icon or href), `hidden: true` (removes the door while the route stays live), and `icon` (overrides the engine glyph with a bundled allowlist name, validated like a site entry's icon); declaration order is the sidebar order. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts#NavLayoutEngineRef`, `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts#NavLayout`. [verified]
- `f:pgwmi4` The persisted collapse state is the `cairn-admin-nav-collapsed` cookie, a comma-separated list of URL-encoded collapsed section labels; once the cookie exists its decoded set wins outright, even when empty, so a section absent from the set (including one added to the layout later) renders open. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-shell.ts:141-150` (`cookieCollapsed`, the `undefined`-versus-present distinction), `src/lib/admin/CairnAdminShell.svelte:195-222` (the seed from the cookie, else from each section's `collapsed`, and `onToggleSection` writing the set back). [verified]
- `f:18d9so` A site declaring no `navLayout` gets a flat arrangement synthesized by `resolveDefaultLayout` through the same primitives, `engineEntry` and `engineVisible`, that a declared layout resolves through, so the two paths cannot drift on what a role sees; the concepts come first, then the fixed engine screens, with `help` left to the trailing fallback group. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:95-97,440-446,571-590` (the seam comment, `engineVisible`, `engineEntry`, `resolveDefaultLayout`). [verified]

## docs/extend/README.md

- `f:c1ujrl` The engine defines three export stability tiers: Extension API, Scaffold API (both frozen contracts), and Unstable API (importable today, no promise across minors). Source: `scripts/checks/reference-coverage.mjs:70,91` (the gate's own comment: "Extension and Scaffold API are the frozen contract, Unstable API marks a name..."; the Stability cell "must read 'Extension API', 'Scaffold API', or 'Unstable API'"), enforced via `npm run check:reference`. [verified]
- `f:zm9tp4` The nav fields on `AdminShellData` and `navFilter`'s parameter/return types changed shape at `0.86.0` (the version `navLayout` shipped), and `navLayout`'s own types were renamed at `0.94.0` (`AdminNavEntry` folded into `NavLayoutEntry`), both breaking changes inside the documented Extension API tier despite the stability promise. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:54` (`NavLayoutEntry`; no `AdminNavEntry` anywhere in `src/lib`), corroborated by the already-verified `f:bjpv4j`/`f:tzr2v7` version facts. [verified]
- `f:3pposq` Vocabulary defined once for the whole extend track: Concept, Adapter, Render, Seam, Island, Holding branch (`cairn/<concept>/<id>`), Manifest, Role/capability (role is a site-defined name; capability is one of `none`/`editor`/`owner`). Source: `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:12` (`Capability = 'owner' | 'editor' | 'none'`), `src/lib/content/pending.ts:6-11` (holding branch `cairn/<concept>/<id>`); the remaining glossary terms (Concept, Adapter, Render, Seam, Island, Manifest) name this repo's own `ConceptConfig`/`CairnAdapter`/`SiteRender`/`IslandRegistry`/`Manifest` types. [verified: structurally, a glossary list rather than one asserted line]
- `f:2zytgf` `defineAdapter` builds the `CairnAdapter` a site's `cairn.config.ts` exports: `content` (the concept configs keyed by concept id), `backend` (a `BackendProvider`, `createGithubApp` for the GitHub repository commits land on), `email` (the sender config for the magic link), and `rendering` (its `render` function, plus optional `islands`), with optional `roles`, `access`, `media`, `aiPosture`, and `editor`; the engine reads what it needs from that one object. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts#CairnAdapter`. [verified]
- `f:5eum60` A concept is one entry in the adapter's `content` map, declared with `defineConcept`: a repo directory of markdown files (`dir`) plus a `fields` fieldset, with optional routing and URL policy; the scaffold declares posts and pages concepts, but neither name is reserved. Source: `src/lib/content/types.ts#ConceptConfig`, `templates/waymark/src/theme/cairn.config.ts:1-3`. [verified]
- `f:qvj951` A site's `render` is the adapter's `rendering.render` function, built with `createRenderer`; the admin's live preview and the public delivery routes call that same function, since the scaffold passes `cairn.rendering.render` to the public routes config and to the admin mount. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer`, `templates/waymark/src/chassis/public-routes.ts:14`, `templates/waymark/src/routes/admin/[...path]/+page.svelte:26`. [verified]
- `f:keo8ze` An island is a Svelte component registered by directive name in the adapter's `rendering.islands` (an `IslandRegistry`); the client mounts it over the matching `hydrate` directive's static fallback inside otherwise static rendered output. Source: `src/lib/islands/types.ts#IslandRegistry`, `src/lib/content/types.ts:266`. [verified]
- `f:byoi1w` A save patches the manifest's one changed row. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:341-352` (`publishAction`: a save commits no manifest, so the moment an entry goes live is the publish commit). [rejected: a save commits only the entry file to its holding branch and no manifest change; a publish patches the row and commits it with the content]
- `f:n520j9` The manifest is the index the admin and the delivery layer both read instead of crawling the repository through the GitHub API. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-context.ts:378-381` (`readManifest`, the admin's one-file read), `src/lib/delivery/site-indexes.ts#createSiteIndexes` (the public index is built from the site's content globs). [rejected: the admin reads it with one `readManifest` call, but the public delivery index is built from the site's content globs at build time and reads the manifest only for a pending-branch preview and `buildNewlyPublished`]
- `f:549u00` cairn is pre-1.0, so the stability tiers are a target discipline and an Extension-tier break can ship in a minor release. Source: `package.json:3` (`"version": "0.98.0"`, a `0.x` number). [verified]

## docs/extend/render-safety.md

- `f:zzbzo8` `createRenderer`'s pipeline runs a fixed nine-stage order: parse markdown, rehype-raw, sanitize floor, `build()` dispatch, slug/task-list/highlight, anchor hardening, sink guard, table-scroll wrap, site's own plugins. The `unsafeDisableSanitize` switch turns off exactly the sanitize floor and the sink guard; every other stage still runs. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:93-140` `createRenderer` (`rehypePlugins` array built in exactly this order: `rehypeRaw`, `floor` (sanitize, empty when `unsafeDisableSanitize`), `rehypeDispatch`, `rehypeSlug`, `rehypeTaskListA11y`, `rehypeCairnHighlight`, `rehypeAnchorRel`, `rehypeSinkGuard` (also gated on `unsafeDisableSanitize`), `rehypeTableScroll`, then `options.rehypePlugins`). [verified]
- `f:z3a58a` Every renderer `createRenderer` builds runs a `rehype-sanitize` floor by default, seeded from GitHub's own `defaultSchema`, unless a site opts out explicitly; it strips `<script>` tags, inline event-handler attributes, and `javascript:`/`data:` URLs after markdown parsing and `rehype-raw`, before the `build()` dispatch or any later stage touches the tree. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:111-118` (`floor` built from `rehypeSanitize` unless `options.unsafeDisableSanitize`, placed after `rehypeRaw` and before `rehypeDispatch`) and `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:5,16-17` (`import { defaultSchema } from 'hast-util-sanitize'`; "Starts from hast-util-sanitize's defaultSchema, the GitHub-lineage allowlist that strips scripts, inline event handlers, and javascript:/data: URLs"). [verified]
- `f:2fwybn` `sanitizeSchema` is additive by contract, not by enforcement: the callback receives cairn's default schema, its doc comment asks a site to start from that argument so the dangerous strip is preserved, and its return replaces the floor's schema wholesale with nothing checking it. A callback that admits `<script>` gets author script through the floor, and the sink guard removes no `<script>` element, while a `javascript:` or `data:` URL a callback admits is still stripped by the sink guard, whose safe-scheme list derives from `defaultSchema` and never from the site's schema. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:42-48` (`RendererConfig.sanitizeSchema`: "returns the schema to use ... start from the argument so the dangerous strip is preserved"), `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:62` (`return extend ? extend(schema) : schema`), `:129-140` (`SAFE_SCHEMES` from `defaultSchema.protocols` plus `cairn`), `:189-191` (the sink guard "does not remove a build()-emitted raw script"); a 2026-09-30 probe of `createRenderer(undefined, { sanitizeSchema })` with `script` added to `tagNames` and `strip` emptied rendered `<script>alert(1)</script>` from author markdown, while a `javascript:` href it also admitted stayed stripped. The comment at `sanitize-schema.ts:22-24` ("can add to the allowlist but not weaken the core strip") overstates the code. [verified]
- `f:r7sbt1` `unsafeDisableSanitize` is a code-level (not editor-facing/admin-UI) switch that removes both the pre-dispatch sanitize floor and the post-dispatch sink guard. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:54` (`RendererConfig.unsafeDisableSanitize?: boolean`, a config field, not an admin-UI toggle) `:111-113,134` (gates both the floor and `rehypeSinkGuard`). [verified]
- `f:q9yndd` The built sanitize schema additionally admits: cairn's own directive markers (`data-primitive`, `data-slot`, `data-role`, `data-rise`, `data-attr-<key>`) as inert data attributes; `nav`, `details`, `summary`, `figure`, `figcaption` tags; `className` on any element (dropping the default schema's narrower per-tag anchor exception first); `srcSet`/`sizes` on `img`; and the inert `cairn:` URL scheme on `href` (resolved to a live permalink before delivery, or surviving as a literal inert token if it never resolves). `javascript:`/`data:` remain stripped regardless. Source: `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:13,36-60` (`FIXED_MARKERS`, `tagNames` addition, anchor `className` handling, `imgAttrs`, `protocols.href` gaining `cairn`). [verified]
- `f:gi3keo` `rehypeSinkGuard` runs after anchor hardening, over the fully built tree, and strips any `on*` attribute and inline `style`, scheme-checking every URL-bearing property (`href`, `src`, `srcSet`, and others) against the same safe-scheme list; it does not remove a `build()`-emitted `<script>`, `<style>`, or `iframe srcdoc` element outright. Source: `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:117-127,180-208` `URL_PROPS`, `rehypeSinkGuard` (doc comment: "It does not remove a build()-emitted raw script, style, or iframe srcdoc element node"). [verified]
- `f:296ar7` Raw HTML in markdown is parsed (`rehype-raw`) rather than escaped to literal text, then cleaned to the same allowlist as directive-authored content; never delivered verbatim, never dropped wholesale. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:108-113,116-117` (`rehypeRaw` runs first in `rehypePlugins`, immediately followed by the same `floor` sanitize schema every other node passes through). [verified]
- `f:3yzqup` Every anchor with `target="_blank"` gets its `rel` forced (`noopener noreferrer` by default, configurable or disabled) after highlighting and ahead of the sink guard. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:114,131,134` (`options.anchorRel ?? 'noopener noreferrer'`; `rehypeAnchorRel` pushed after `rehypeCairnHighlight`, before `rehypeSinkGuard`) and `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:71-81` `rehypeAnchorRel`. [verified]
- `f:21by9u` A site's own registered `build()` component can still bypass all render-safety guarantees by rendering trusted literal markup outside the sanitized tree (e.g., an SSR fetch injected directly); the page's guarantees stop at the boundary of what `createRenderer` actually produced. Source: `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts:189-191` (`rehypeSinkGuard`'s doc comment: "A build() that emits those is running site-developer code, and author markdown is cleaned by the pre-dispatch floor"). [verified]
- `f:phx4lo` A site's own `remarkPlugins` and `rehypePlugins` both compose at the pipeline's last stage, the position the site's-own-plugins stage shows in the stage order. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer` (the `remarkPlugins` array and the `rehypePlugins` construction). [rejected: `remarkPlugins` run after cairn's own remark steps and before the remark-to-rehype conversion, early in the pipeline; only `rehypePlugins` run last, after the table-scroll wrap]
- `f:xothz1` `remarkPlugins` run after cairn's own remark steps (include resolution, directive stamp, `cairn:` links, figures, `media:` resolution) and before the remark-to-rehype conversion, while `rehypePlugins` run last of all, after the table-scroll wrap; the sanitize floor sits after `rehype-raw` and before the `build()` dispatch, so a site component's trusted output and its inline SVG icons are never sanitized, and the anchor-rel step runs after the dispatch so it also covers component-built anchors. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer` (the plugin arrays and their comments). [verified]
- `f:dwc2bu` Build-time syntax highlighting (`rehypeCairnHighlight`) emits class-only output, the `cairn-tok-*` ramp with no inline style, runs unconditionally since a code fence is content and not a sink, and ships no client highlighter (Shiki is build-only behind a dynamic import), so it needs no special placement relative to the sanitize floor or the sink guard; its token classes survive the floor because `className` is already admitted on `*`. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer` (the comment above `rehypeCairnHighlight`). [verified]
- `f:wgovy6` With `unsafeDisableSanitize` set, `createRenderer` runs neither the sanitize floor nor the sink guard while `rehype-raw` still parses author raw HTML into elements, so a `<script>` or an inline handler such as `onerror` that any editor, or anyone holding an editor's session, writes into an entry reaches the rendered HTML, and the public pages serve that same render output to every visitor. Source: `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer` (`allowDangerousHtml: true`), `src/tests/unit/render-sanitize.test.ts:95-99` (with the switch on, an `onerror` attribute survives), `templates/waymark/src/chassis/public-routes.ts:14` (`render: cairn.rendering.render`). [verified]
- `f:r0cv6e` The sanitize floor was added after the delivery pipeline first shipped without one: before commit `40d466ad` (first released in `v0.17.0`), the pipeline ran `rehype-raw` with `allowDangerousHtml` and no sanitize step, and that commit inserted `rehype-sanitize` after `rehype-raw` and ahead of the component dispatch. Source: git commit `40d466ad` ("feat(render): sanitize author content in the delivery pipeline"), `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts:108-113`. [verified]

## docs/extend/restrict-admin-access.md

- `f:p1xmp5` Capability (`owner`/`editor`/`none`) is the floor cairn already enforces, resolved from the declared role vocabulary; the access map sits on top and only narrows, never widens, what a capability already permits. Source: `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:12` (`Capability`), `src/lib/auth/access.ts:156-175` (`canReach`: capability checked before the map; `'none'` always refused, `'owner'` always admitted, the map only narrows an `'editor'` capability's reach). [verified]
- `f:grxpu5` `defineAccess(roles, map)` validates a map's role names against the implicit `{ owner: 'owner', editor: 'editor' }` pair when `roles` is `undefined`. Source: `src/lib/auth/roles.ts:24` `export const DEFAULT_ROLES: { owner: 'owner'; editor: 'editor' } = { owner: 'owner', editor: 'editor' };` and `src/lib/auth/access.ts:75` `const vocabulary = new Set(Object.keys(roles ?? DEFAULT_ROLES));`. [verified]
- `f:0qb73i` An access-map key is either an engine screen id (a declared concept, or `media`/`vocabulary`/`nav`/`settings`) or an `/admin`-prefixed route path; its value is the admitted role names, matched against the site's `defineRoles` vocabulary. Owner capability always reaches every mapped target regardless of the role list. Source: `src/lib/auth/access.ts:160-161` (owner bypass), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts:280` (`ACCESS_FIXED_SCREENS = ['media','vocabulary','nav','settings']`). [verified]
- `f:iwf4nu` The same `access` value must be passed to both the adapter's own `access` member and `createAuthGuard`'s `access` option; the guard attaches the map to `locals.cairnAccess` on every request. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:355,375` `event.locals.cairnAccess = access ?? {};` (both branches of the guard). [verified]
- `f:54d3ui` `requireAccess` checks `event.route.id` by default (not the request URL), since a parameterized or catch-all route's URL is attacker-chosen while the route id is not. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:457-486` `requireAccess` (`target` "defaults to `event.route.id`, never [the URL]"; `const resolvedTarget = target ?? targetFromRouteId(event.route.id);`). [verified]
- `f:zo034s` A target absent from the map behaves oppositely for a route vs. an engine screen: an unmapped engine screen stays open to any editor-capability session (zero-config default), but a route calling `requireAccess` with no map entry for its path refuses every session, owner included, treated as a misconfiguration. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:470-473` (`requireAccess`: "The unmatched case... 403s every session, owner included"), `src/lib/auth/access.ts:173-174` (`canReach`: `admitted === undefined ? true`, an unmapped engine screen stays open). [verified]
- `f:5pyozi` A page's `load` gating a read never gates the POST to its own form action: SvelteKit runs a matched action with no page or layout `load` run first (`handle` runs before the action, and load functions run after the action completes), so an unguarded action is reachable even behind a guarded page. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/form-actions ("your page's load functions will run after the action completes. Note that handle runs before the action is invoked"), read 2026-09-29. [external: SvelteKit (framework actions behavior, not cairn source; the seam it justifies, `requireAccess` gating each action explicitly rather than relying on page `load`, is cairn's own design response)]
- `f:iewhzh` Restricting the `media` screen also restricts the inline image picker inside every image-bearing concept's editor, since it calls the same access-gated endpoint. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-media-library.ts:67`, `content-routes-media-metadata.ts:164,222,300,424,497`, and `content-routes-media-delete.ts:118` all call `requireEngineAccess(runtime.access, editor, 'media')`; `src/lib/admin/MediaInsertPopover.svelte` (the editor's inline insert popover) uploads/reads through these same media routes. [verified]
- `f:smbsa6` `ownerOnly` on `createSectionAction`/`createAdminAction` requires owner capability in addition to the access map's own rule for the target, never in place of it; it only ever narrows. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:120-129` (`authorizeAdminTarget`: three ordered gates, `ownerOnly` checked last, on top of the map's own rule). [verified]
- `f:8anql1` `requireAccess(event, target?)` returns the session the guard resolved, redirecting to login when there is none, and refuses with `error(403, 'Access denied')`, logging `auth.access.refused` with the editor's email, role, and target, when the map has no rule for the target or does not admit the session's role; it reads the map from `event.locals.cairnAccess`, and its default target is `event.route.id` with route-group segments dropped (`/admin/(app)/roster` reads as `/admin/roster`), a parameterized id kept verbatim, and a null id resolved to a sentinel that matches no key, so a route with a rest or dynamic parameter under a deeper map key refuses every session and must declare `target`. `requireSession` (login redirect) and `requireEditor` (403 for a none-capability session) do not consult the map. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#requireAccess`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#requireSession`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#requireEditor`, `src/lib/auth/access.ts#targetFromRouteId`. [verified]
- `f:gun084` `createAuthGuard` takes `roles` (the declared role vocabulary, defaulting to the owner and editor pair), `access` (the map), `includeSubDomains`, and `identity`, and on every guarded request it sets `event.locals.cairnAccess = access ?? {}` beside `event.locals.cairnEditor`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:172-175,355-357,375-377`. [verified]
- `f:3s23un` A `SectionAction` wraps a handler with `SectionActionOptions`: `action` and `entity` (reused as the audit fields on every denial), an optional `target` (default `event.route.id` with route groups dropped, required for a route serving more than one section or carrying a rest parameter, used verbatim when declared), `ownerOnly`, and `deniedMessage`; its handler receives `{ event, form, ctx }`, and `createSectionAction` runs the CSRF check and the fail-closed access authorization before the handler. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts#SectionActionOptions`, `src/lib/sveltekit/section-action.ts#SectionAction`. [verified]
- `f:altcjp` A `navLayout` site entry is gated by the access map only when its href carries a matching rule, in which case it defers entirely to `canReach` (shown to the roles the rule admits, and to an owner), while an engine screen follows `canReach` directly, so the sidebar hides exactly what a route check would refuse. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts#hrefReachable`, `src/lib/auth/access.ts#canReach`. [verified]
- `f:dmlclv` An unmapped href removes a door from the sidebar, since the navLayout seam reads the access map for visibility. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-nav.ts#hrefReachable` (an href the map has no opinion on stays visible). [rejected: a site entry is gated by the map only when its href matches a rule; an href the map has no opinion on stays visible to any editor-capability session, and only `hidden: true` or a role-excluding rule removes a door]

## docs/extend/reuse-content-across-entries.md

- `f:ojydmz` `fragments` is a reserved concept key; declaring it turns on the `::include` directive. Source: `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:170` (`if (id === FRAGMENTS_CONCEPT_ID && config.routing !== 'embedded')`). [verified]
- `f:7wiuwr` A `fragments` concept must use `routing: 'embedded'`; `normalizeConcepts` throws otherwise, when the runtime composes (`composeRuntime` or the delivery descriptors), not `defineConcept` at module load, since `defineConcept` never sees the concept key. Source: `src/lib/content/compose.ts:39`, `src/lib/delivery/site-descriptors.ts:15`, `src/lib/content/concepts.ts:170-173` (throws `"cairn: concept \"fragments\" requires routing: 'embedded' (the include directive resolves against it)"`). [verified]
- `f:60zw5n` `fragments` needs its own glob in the content index (`src/lib/content.ts`) the same as any other declared concept; `createSiteIndexes` throws at build time if it's missing. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-indexes.ts:54-58` (`throw new Error(\`cairn: concept "${descriptor.id}" has no glob passed; pass its import.meta.glob...\`)` when a declared concept, `fragments` included, has no matching key in the passed globs record). [verified]
- `f:i6zyab` If a site also uses the `cairnManifest` Vite plugin, `fragments` must be added to that plugin's own `content` option too; a concept missing there fails silently (no build error), so an entry that includes a fragment would find it missing from the manifest with no explanation. Source: `src/lib/vite/internal.ts:36-37` (`CairnManifestOptions.content: Record<string, string>`, per-concept globs keyed by concept id) and `src/lib/delivery/manifest.ts:18-22` `buildSiteManifest`: `const record = globRecord[descriptor.id] ?? {};`, so a concept id absent from the glob record degrades to an empty file set with no throw, unlike `createSiteIndexes` (verified above), which throws on the same omission. [verified]
- `f:0w7xeu` The include directive syntax is `::include{fragment="<id>"}`; the editor's "Include a fragment" insert-palette control writes this for the author. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-include.ts:122` (`Resolve \`::include{fragment="<id>"}\` leaf directives`) and `src/lib/admin/editor-include.ts:96-97` (`includeFragmentId`, the same `fragment="id"` shape). [verified]
- `f:zv3q45` The fragment resolver type is `FragmentResolve`, the type `SiteRender`'s `resolveFragment` option takes; a build supplies one backed by `createFragmentResolver`. Source: `src/lib/delivery/site-resolver.ts:207` `export function createFragmentResolver(site: SiteResolver): FragmentResolve` and `src/lib/content/types.ts:218` (`resolveFragment?: ... FragmentResolve`). [verified]
- `f:xlgfqm` A dangling `::include` (an id naming no real fragment) fails the build the same way a dangling `cairn:` link does. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-include.ts:36-38,144` `FragmentResolve`'s doc comment: "a resolver that throws is the build backstop, exactly like `LinkResolve` and `MediaResolve`"; `const body = resolve(id); // may throw (build backstop); propagates out of render`. [verified]
- `f:k84qwy` A site's own `rendering.render` function must forward `resolveFragment` alongside `resolve` and `resolveMedia`; omitting it means `::include` directives publish as literal directive text with no error. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-include.ts:129-132` `remarkResolveIncludes` ("With no resolver supplied every include directive is left untouched for the stamp step's literal-prose restore"; `if (!resolve) return;`). [verified]
- `f:82eo9p` A fragment cannot include another fragment: resolution runs exactly one pass deep, and the engine refuses the save outright rather than shipping a directive that can never resolve. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-include.ts:12-20` (one-pass-only doc comment, "the defense-in-depth backstop for the engine's save-time nesting rejection") and `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:187-193` (`// A fragment can never include another fragment...`; `return fail(400, saveRefusal("A fragment can't include another fragment.", body));`). [verified]
- `f:3vndvj` Deleting a fragment still included somewhere is refused, using the same delete-guard shape as a `reference` field: every other entry's recorded `includes` edges are checked against the fragment's id. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:111-116` (the fragment delete guard reads `inboundIncludes`), `src/lib/content/manifest.ts:473` (`inboundIncludes`). [verified]
- `f:5adbqm` A fragment needs no fields beyond a title, because the admin gives every concept a `title` field for free. Source: `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:1401` (`titleField = data.fields.find((f) => f.name === 'title')`), `src/lib/delivery/content-index.ts:126` (`title: asString(raw.title) ?? id`). [rejected: the title input renders only when the concept's own fieldset declares a field named `title`; an empty `defineFieldset({})` shows none, and an entry without a title reads as its id]
- `f:72pqil` At render time `remarkResolveIncludes` replaces each `::include{fragment="<id>"}` leaf directive with the fragment's parsed markdown, one pass deep, and it runs ahead of the directive stamp, the `cairn:` link resolver, the figure step, and the `media:` resolver, so a registered component directive, a `cairn:` link, or a `media:` reference inside a fragment resolves as if typed into the including entry; the fragment body is read at each render, so an edit shows in every including entry on its next render. Source: `src/lib/render/resolve-include.ts:118-160` (`remarkResolveIncludes`), `src/lib/render/pipeline.ts#createRenderer` (the `remarkPlugins` order: `remarkResolveIncludes` before `remarkDirectiveStamp`). [verified]
- `f:7250yh` A fragment's own edit screen shows where it is used: the edit load reads `inboundIncludes` for the fragments concept, the sidebar's `Included in` group lists the including entries with a count, and a fragment being published carries a status line naming how many entries include it. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-read.ts:355-356` (`inbound = concept.id === FRAGMENTS_CONCEPT_ID ? inboundIncludes(manifest, id) : inboundLinks(...)`), `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:696-712,2333-2336`. [verified]
- `f:kcm3gf` The scaffold ships one fragment, `src/content/fragments/trail-safety-notice.md`, whose frontmatter holds only `title: Trail safety notice` and whose body is a single paragraph; the showcase carries an identical copy. Source: `templates/waymark/src/content/fragments/trail-safety-notice.md:1-4`, `examples/showcase/src/content/fragments/trail-safety-notice.md:1-4`. [verified]

## docs/extend/rotate-the-github-app-key.md

- `f:ixr3ny` A GitHub App can hold more than one private key at once; generating a new key does not invalidate the old one, and the App keeps signing with whichever key a caller presents until a key is deleted by hand. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps, read 2026-09-29 (multiple keys exist "in order to rotate keys without downtime"; keys "do not expire and instead need to be manually revoked"). [external: GitHub]
- `f:i4fg3o` cairn's Worker reads the GitHub App key as `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64`, a base64 encoding of the PEM with no line breaks. Source: `src/lib/env.ts:31` (same as `f:vpieos`). [verified]
- `f:sszb7b` If something goes wrong before the old key is deleted, re-pushing the old key's base64 value with the same `wrangler secret put` command restores signing with it immediately. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps (an undeleted key keeps signing), https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/ (`wrangler secret put` deploys at once), read 2026-09-29. [external: GitHub and Cloudflare (composes GitHub's undeleted-keys-keep-signing behavior with Wrangler's immediate secret deploy; neither half is cairn source)]
- `f:lg2ae8` GitHub does not allow restoring a deleted key; if the old key is already deleted and the new one fails, generate a third key instead. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps (the documented controls are generate and delete, and GitHub keeps only the public portion of a key), read 2026-09-29. [external: GitHub (the no-restore rule is read from the documented controls and the public-portion-only storage, not stated in one sentence)]
- `f:zoekqt` GitHub's private-key controls for an App sit under the owner's Settings, Developer settings, GitHub Apps, Edit, "Private keys"; "Generate a private key" downloads a PEM in PKCS#1 form and GitHub keeps only the public portion; an App can hold up to 25 keys, which do not expire and are removed only by hand; with only one key on the App, a new key must be generated before the old one is deleted. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps, read 2026-09-29. [external: GitHub]
- `f:86h9o6` `wrangler secret put <KEY>` creates a new version of the Worker and deploys it immediately (gradual deployments use `wrangler versions secret put` instead), and local `wrangler dev` reads secrets from `.dev.vars` (or `.env`) beside the Wrangler config, never from the deployed secret. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:ejuoh6` A rotation without downtime keeps two keys on the App at once: the new key is pushed as `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` while the old key stays undeleted, a real publish from the deployed Worker confirms the new key, and only then is the old key deleted; an undeleted old key can be re-pushed to roll back, and a deleted key has no restore path. Source: https://docs.github.com/en/apps/creating-github-apps/authenticating-with-a-github-app/managing-private-keys-for-github-apps (keys are meant to be rotated without downtime, never expire, and are deleted by hand), https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/secrets/ (a secret write deploys at once). [external: GitHub and Cloudflare; the confirm-before-delete order is cairn's own runbook built on those two documented behaviors]
- `f:olofdb` The setup command encodes the App's PEM with `Buffer.from(pem).toString('base64')`, one unwrapped line, and stores it by piping that string to `wrangler secret put GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64`, the same encoding as `readFileSync('key.pem').toString('base64')`. Source: `packages/create-cairn-site/src/cloudflare/secret.mjs:31-33`. [verified]
- `f:w78j1b` A base64 value with line breaks fails to decode in the Worker, so `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64` must be one unwrapped line. Source: `src/lib/github/signing.ts#installationToken` (`atob(creds.privateKeyB64)`), `src/lib/github/signing.ts#pemToPkcs8` (strips whitespace from the decoded PEM). [rejected: the Worker decodes with `atob()`, which ignores ASCII whitespace; workerd 2026-08-15 decoded a two-line value to the same bytes as one line (run 2026-09-29), so a wrapped value still decodes and one unwrapped line remains only the documented form]
- `f:9xqudi` A site's GitHub App id and installation id are non-secret values in its adapter (`createGithubApp({ owner, repo, branch, appId, installationId })`); only the private key is a Worker secret, `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY_B64`, and a missing key throws a `github.app-unreachable` error on first token use. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts#createGithubApp`, `src/lib/github/credentials.ts#appCredentials`. [verified]
- `f:prb2os` Workers Logs records a Worker's logs only when `observability.enabled` is true in its Wrangler config, and the scaffold's `wrangler.jsonc` sets it, so an empty log on a site without that setting proves nothing. Source: `templates/waymark/wrangler.jsonc:7`, https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/observability/logs/workers-logs/ ("You must add the observability setting for your Worker to write logs to Workers Logs"). [verified: the template line traces to the repo; the Workers Logs requirement is Cloudflare's own statement]
- `f:7u49xs` `github.unreachable` is logged from exactly three best-effort read sites, each degrading instead of failing (the admin shell's pending count with `scope: 'shell'`, the help screen's pending list with `scope: 'help'`, and the publish-success advisories read with `scope: 'publish_advisories'`), while `commit.failed` and `publish.failed` come only from the commit catch (`logCommitFailed`: warn with `reason: 'conflict'`, otherwise error with the error string). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-shell.ts:174,271`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:371`, `src/lib/sveltekit/commit-log.ts:30-45`. [verified]
- `f:bffsa9` A key that cannot authenticate fails on the publish path with a `publish.failed` or `commit.failed` record in Workers Logs. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts#makeGithubBackend` (each operation awaits `getToken()`, which mints the installation token on first use), `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:236,294-302` (the save's first unguarded GitHub read, `readManifest`, runs before and outside the commit `try`). [rejected: the token is minted lazily on the first GitHub read, which sits outside the commit catch, so a key that cannot mint a token ends the request as an unhandled error, not as a `commit.failed` or `publish.failed` record]
- `f:0mxwyu` In the key-rotation runbook the old key is deleted in step 6 and the swap is confirmed against the deployed Worker in step 5. Source: `docs/internal/record/harvest/extend/rotate-the-github-app-key.json`. [rejected: describes a deleted page; its own numbered steps confirm the new key in step 4 and delete the old key in step 5]
- `f:dbue4k` PowerShell's `[Convert]::ToBase64String` returns unwrapped base64 by default and `Out-File -NoNewline` adds no trailing newline, but `Out-File`'s default encoding differs by version: `unicode` (UTF-16LE) in Windows PowerShell 5.1 and `utf8NoBOM` in PowerShell 7, so under 5.1 the written `.b64` file holds the same text at two bytes per character, unlike the one-byte-per-character file the Node one-liner writes. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/out-file?view=powershell-5.1 and the same page at `view=powershell-7.5` (the `-Encoding` default), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.base64formattingoptions (`None` inserts no line breaks), read 2026-09-29. [external: PowerShell]
- `f:4sb2nh` PowerShell's `[Convert]::ToBase64String(...) | Out-File -NoNewline` one-liner produces the same unwrapped base64 file as the Node one-liner. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/out-file?view=powershell-5.1 and the same page at `view=powershell-7.5` (the `-Encoding` default), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.base64formattingoptions, read 2026-09-29. [rejected: under Windows PowerShell 5.1, `Out-File` defaults to `unicode` (UTF-16LE), so the file holds the base64 text at two bytes per character and a `wrangler secret put` fed its bytes gets a corrupted key; only PowerShell 7's `utf8NoBOM` default matches the Node file]

## docs/extend/security-model.md

- `f:f39xqq` cairn's built-in magic-link path: a sign-in token lives 10 minutes, a session lives 30 days, and a repeat sign-in request from the same address is throttled to once per minute; all three are named constants an adapter cannot loosen. Source: `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts#TOKEN_TTL_MS`, `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts#SESSION_TTL_MS`, `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts#SEND_COOLDOWN_MS`. [verified]
- `f:s32y4a` The magic-link request path is non-enumerating: an address not on the roster gets the same `{ outcome: 'sent' }` response a real editor's address gets, except a *repeat* request inside the one-minute cooldown returns a distinct `throttled` status, which does reveal roster membership. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:51-57,211,237,260` (`{ outcome: 'sent'; sent: true }` for both the non-editor neutral answer (`:211`) and a normal send (`:260`); `{ outcome: 'throttled'; sent: false }` for a per-email cooldown repeat (`:237`), doc comment: "the identical `{ outcome: 'sent', sent: true }`, so the common case never leaks allowlist [membership]"). [verified]
- `f:6lmusm` Sign-in binds to the requesting browser via a `cairn_login_pending` cookie (`HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Lax`, `__Host-` prefixed on https, one hour long); the confirm action compares the cookie's nonce hash against the token row's `nonce_hash` inside the same atomic `DELETE` that consumes the token. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:101-142` (`mintOrReusePendingNonce`), `src/lib/auth/store.ts:181-186` (`WHERE token_hash = ? AND expires_at > ? AND (nonce_hash IS NULL OR nonce_hash = ?)`). [verified]
- `f:5iqvmt` A confirm from a browser holding no cookie passes SQL `NULL`; a row with no binding (`nonce_hash IS NULL`) still matches and signs the editor in. Source: `src/lib/auth/store.ts:182` (`nonce_hash IS NULL OR nonce_hash = ?`). [verified]
- `f:91dwrk` A throttled re-request rebinds the live token to the browser that just asked (last-requester-wins); the rebind skips an expired row or an unbound row. Source: `src/lib/auth/store.ts:141-142` (`UPDATE magic_token SET nonce_hash = ? WHERE email = ? AND expires_at > ? AND nonce_hash IS NOT NULL AND nonce_hash <> ?`). [verified]
- `f:9uhscf` `NO_PENDING_REQUEST_ERROR` is exported as the constant `'no-pending-request'` from `@glw907/cairn-cms/sveltekit`, naming the distinct error code that a failed confirm from a browser holding no pending-login cookie redirects with; a site rendering its own login page branches on the constant. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-error-codes.ts:23` (`export const NO_PENDING_REQUEST_ERROR = 'no-pending-request';`), `src/lib/sveltekit/index.ts:19`. [verified]
- `f:jud805` The engine writes the nonce hash to `magic_token.nonce_hash`, added by `migrations/0004_login_nonce.sql`; no command checks this pre-deploy today (the retired JS doctor's `auth.store` check left with it, and `cairn doctor` runs no D1 check), so an unmigrated `AUTH_DB` surfaces the failure only at the first sign-in attempt. Source: `migrations/0004_login_nonce.sql` (file present), `src/lib/auth/store.ts:20-30` (error naming that exact migration path), and `docs/reference/cli-cairn-doctor.md` (the eleven-check table, no D1 or auth.store entry). [verified]
- `f:wzcgs3` The session cookie carries the `__Host-` prefix on every https deploy (requiring `Secure`, `Path=/`, forbidding `Domain`); local http development drops the prefix since `__Host-` requires `Secure` unconditionally. Source: `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts:5-15` `sessionCookieName` (doc comment: "On https the cookie is Secure and takes the __Host- prefix, which binds it to the origin (the browser enforces Secure, Path=/, and no Domain). On local http dev the prefix is dropped, since __Host- requires Secure"). [verified]
- `f:njh87y` The CSRF double-submit cookie shares the session cookie's `__Host-`/`Secure` derivation, and logout deletes both name forms (bare and `__Host-`) of both cookies, each with its own matching `Secure` flag, whichever name the current request's derivation would produce. Source: `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts:20-28` (`csrfCookieName`, its comment: both names share their `secure` input through `csrfSecure`), `src/lib/sveltekit/csrf.ts#csrfSecure`, `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:453-460` (`logoutAction` deletes `sessionCookieName(secure)`, `sessionCookieName(!secure)`, `csrfCookieName(secure)`, and `csrfCookieName(!secure)`). [verified]
- `f:9ik061` The CSRF cookie's full attribute set: `HttpOnly`, `Path=/`, `SameSite=Lax` set explicitly, and a `Max-Age` matching the session cookie's 30-day lifetime; it rotates at exactly two moments on the magic-link path, a successful login and a logout. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/csrf.ts:119-137` (`issueCsrfToken`: `path: '/'`, `httpOnly: true`, `sameSite: 'lax'`, `maxAge` from `SESSION_TTL_MS`; doc comment: "a login... and a logout (which deletes it). Nothing else ever changes the value."). [verified]
- `f:fhit7f` `createAuthGuard`'s `identity` option replaces the entire built-in sign-in path (no token minted, no session created, no session cookie set); `identity.resolve` reads the gate's own proof of identity and the guard looks the proven email up against the roster, exactly as it looks up a magic-link session's email. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:294-302` (the `identity` branch runs in place of magic-link session resolution on every non-public admin path) combined with the already-verified fact above that the guard performs no bootstrap write and sets no session cookie under `identity`. [verified]
- `f:2glcaf` Under `identity`, the effective session lifetime moves to the gate (e.g. Cloudflare Access, default 24 hours, configurable up to a month); cairn's own 30-day session constant no longer applies. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:294-302` (the `identity` branch sets no session cookie and mints no session, already `f:fhit7f`); Cloudflare Access's own 24-hour default and configurable ceiling are external Cloudflare facts. [verified]
- `f:diro7m` `hasSession` on a `guard.refused` CSRF-rejection log record is structurally always `false` under `identity`, since the guard never sets a session cookie in that mode. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:275-284` (`hasSession = event.cookies.get(sessionCookieName(...)) !== undefined`, logged on the `csrf` reason) combined with the already-verified fact that `identity` mode sets no session cookie, so the cookie read here always misses. [verified]
- `f:7qqhda` The guard's fixed request order: (1) dev-backend tripwire, (2) HTTPS help page, (3) bindings check, (4) CSRF check, (5) session resolve (or identity resolve). Every refusing step except step 5 on the magic-link path logs a named `guard.refused` reason: `dev_backend_in_prod`, `https`, `bindings`, `csrf`. The guard has no origin step, since SvelteKit's own origin check refuses a null or foreign Origin form POST before any handle runs. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:186-299` `createAuthGuard`'s `handle` function, in exactly this order: `log.error('guard.refused', { reason: 'dev_backend_in_prod' ...})` (:201), the https help page with `reason: 'https'` (:219), the bindings check with `reason: 'bindings'` (:228-232), the CSRF check with `reason: 'csrf'` (:267-274), then session/identity resolve. [verified]
- `f:gh73p5` `CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND` is only honored as `1` or boolean `true`, and only when the Worker env carries it (`cloudflare:workers`'s `env`; a `process.env` value is never read); a `CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND=true` string reads as unset (only exact truthy forms count). Source: `src/lib/dev-flag.ts:27-29` `export function isDevBackendFlagSet(raw: unknown): boolean { return raw === '1' || raw === true; }` (doc comment: "`'true'`, `'yes'`, and `'0'` are all unset here, deliberately") and `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:203` (reads `env[CAIRN_DEV_BACKEND_FLAG]` alone, once the build is not prerendering). [verified]
- `f:gncd64` A site itself sets `csrf: { checkOrigin: false }` in its SvelteKit config, `svelte.config.js` or the inline `sveltekit()` call in `vite.config.ts` (SvelteKit's own build-time config; `createAuthGuard` does not set this at runtime), and `createAuthGuard` enforces its own Origin-independent double-submit CSRF check on every unsafe `/admin` form POST instead, restoring an equivalent strict Origin check for every non-admin route. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:206-213` ("restore the framework's strict Origin check the consumer disabled when they set checkOrigin: false to hand cairn the admin CSRF authority"), `src/lib/sveltekit/csrf.ts` (the admin double-submit CSRF implementation). [rejected: retired; SvelteKit 3 removed `csrf.checkOrigin`, so a site carries no `csrf` config, SvelteKit's origin check covers every route, and `createAuthGuard` enforces its double-submit token on unsafe `/admin` form POSTs with no Origin check of its own outside `createAuthChannel`'s]
- `f:vqh4a9` `canReach` is the one function deciding both route enforcement and nav visibility for the access map, so they can never drift apart; an access map narrows only the targets it names, and an unmapped screen/concept stays reachable to any editor-capability session (the roster screen `editors` stays owner-only regardless of the map). Source: `src/lib/auth/access.ts:1-4` (module comment: "canReach is the one authority function... so route enforcement and sidebar visibility can never drift apart"), `:163-165` (`editors` stays owner-only regardless of the map). [verified]
- `f:4q8kin` `authorizeAdminTarget` (used by `createSectionAction` and `createAdminAction`'s opt-in `access` option) is fail-closed at three ordered gates: (1) no rule for the target refuses, (2) a session whose role is absent from an existing rule refuses (except owner capability, always admitted), (3) `ownerOnly` against a non-owner session refuses. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-action.ts:120-129` `authorizeAdminTarget`: `if (!hasAccessRule(access, opts.target)) return { outcome: 'no-rule' }; if (!canReach(access, editor, opts.target)) return { outcome: 'not-admitted' }; if (opts.ownerOnly && editor.capability !== 'owner') return { outcome: 'not-owner' };`. [verified]
- `f:ubuj1w` Every admin response the guard's resolve path returns carries `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff`, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, `frame-ancestors 'none'`, `Referrer-Policy: strict-origin` (scoped to `/admin` only, never site-wide; each admin view also emits a matching `<meta name="referrer" content="strict-origin">`, `LoginPage` and `ConfirmPage` their own and `CairnAdminShell` for its authed views only), a `Permissions-Policy` denying camera/microphone/geolocation, `Strict-Transport-Security` (opt-in subdomain pinning), and `Cache-Control: private, no-store`; the branded rejection pages carry the same set less `Strict-Transport-Security` (`f:t976f1`), and the 303 redirect to `/admin/login` the guard throws for a missing or invalid session, before `resolve`, carries none of them. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:355,371` (the thrown redirect, and `applySecurityHeaders` applied only to the resolved response), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-response.ts:74` (`brandedAdminPage` passes `omitHsts: true`), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-response.ts:41-52` (`headers.set('X-Content-Type-Options', 'nosniff')`, `'X-Frame-Options', 'DENY'`, `'Content-Security-Policy', "frame-ancestors 'none'"`, `'Referrer-Policy', 'strict-origin'`, `'Strict-Transport-Security', opts.includeSubDomains ? '...includeSubDomains' : 'max-age=63072000'`, `'Permissions-Policy', 'camera=(), microphone=(), geolocation=()'`, `'Cache-Control', 'private, no-store'`); the metas at `src/lib/admin/LoginPage.svelte:75`, `src/lib/admin/ConfirmPage.svelte:34`, `src/lib/admin/CairnAdminShell.svelte:685`. [verified]
- `f:yzbvk4` `/admin` carries no full Content-Security-Policy by design; the XSS surface that matters (arbitrary visitor content) is governed on the public render path instead, by the sanitize floor. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-response.ts:41-52` (admin responses set only `frame-ancestors 'none'`, no `script-src`/`default-src`, already `f:ubuj1w`), `src/lib/render/sanitize-schema.ts` (the actual XSS defense on the public render path, already `f:z3a58a`). [verified]

- `f:u1bjul` A magic-link sign-in mints a 256-bit random single-use token, stores only its lowercase-hex SHA-256 hash, and emails the raw token in a link to `/admin/auth/confirm`; a new request for the same address deletes the earlier token row before the insert, so a fresh request replaces the previous token; confirming consumes the row in one atomic `DELETE ... RETURNING`, and creates a session. Only an address in the `editor` table is sent a token, the owner curates that table through the owner-only `editors` screen, and a session resolves through a join on `editor`, so a removed editor's session stops resolving on the next request. Source: `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts#generateToken`, `src/lib/auth/crypto.ts#hashToken`, `src/lib/auth/store.ts#issueToken`, `src/lib/auth/store.ts#consumeToken`, `src/lib/auth/store.ts#resolveSession`, `src/lib/auth/access.ts#canReach` (`editors` stays owner-only), `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:241` (`issueToken(db, email, await hashToken(token), ...)`). [verified]

The nonce's only meaning is the `nonce_hash` on a live token row, which the row's own ten-minute expiry sweeps, so a cookie that outlives the row grants nothing. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:101-142,196,241,276,376,460`. [verified]

Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:347-363`, `src/lib/admin/LoginPage.svelte:61,156-162`, `src/lib/admin/ConfirmPage.svelte:59-64`. [verified]
