Source: `docs/internal/admin-smoke-test.md:8-18` (the recorded `event.url` observation), `@sveltejs/kit` 3.0.0 `runtime/server/csrf.js` (the compare). [candidate: the `event.url` half is the smoke doc's recorded observation; the 403 consequence follows from the compare and was not probed under `wrangler dev`]
- `f:f3zp4n` SvelteKit 3's origin check refuses a POST, PUT, PATCH, or DELETE that carries no `Content-Type` header, or a form one, when its `Origin` is missing or differs from the site's own origin (`paths.origin` when set, else `url.origin`), so a server-to-server POST with no body and no content type, such as a webhook, gets the plain 403 `Cross-site POST form submissions are forbidden`; SvelteKit 2.70.3 let an absent content type pass. A missing `Origin` is refused before `csrf.trustedOrigins` is consulted, so the only config that admits it is `trustedOrigins: ['*']`, which also turns the check off for `/admin`; the fix is for the sender to set a content type such as `application/json`. Source: `@sveltejs/kit` 3.0.0 `runtime/server/csrf.js` (`is_csrf_forbidden`), read 2026-10-06; `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-03-sveltekit-3-upgrade-design.md` (the CSRF evidence). [external: SvelteKit]

## docs/extend/share-a-draft-preview.md

- `f:6gmzt1` Draft preview is opt-in per site: the share affordance ships on every upgraded edit screen, since `EditPage`'s `previewMint` prop defaults on and the single-mount admin wires both actions (a site can pass `previewMint={false}` to hold it back), but a mint answers the missing-table failure until `migrations/0003_preview.sql` is applied, and the minted `/preview/<token>` link resolves only where the site mounts its own never-prerendered preview route. Source: `src/lib/admin/EditPage.svelte:94-103,114`, `src/lib/sveltekit/cairn-admin.ts:302-304`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-preview.ts:28-33,93-96`, `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-history-revert-preview-design.md:137-140`. [verified]
- `f:049lo2` Draft preview requires `migrations/0003_preview.sql`, which adds one table, `preview_tokens`, to the existing `AUTH_DB`: `token_hash` (the lowercase-hex SHA-256 of the token, never the plaintext), `concept` and `entry_id` (the shared entry), `editor` (the minting editor's email), `expires_at` and `created_at` (epoch milliseconds), with an index on `expires_at` for the sweep on mint and one on `(concept, entry_id)` for the revoke and lifecycle cleanup. The package ships `migrations/`. Source: `migrations/0003_preview.sql:1-24`, `package.json:203-216`. [verified]
- `f:rr00qr` Until the preview migration runs, both the mint and revoke actions answer with an actionable failure naming the migration, rather than a raw database error. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-preview.ts:28-33,71,122` `missingPreviewTableFailure()` ("The preview_tokens table is missing. Apply migrations/0003_preview.sql to AUTH_DB, then try again."), returned by both the mint (`:71`) and revoke (`:122`) actions on `isMissingTableError(err)`. [verified]
- `f:gvim4v` `loadPreview` renders a minted link through the same composition an entry's public page already uses, and must be mounted inside the same route group as the entry pages so it inherits the site's layout/stylesheets/chrome; the route must set `export const prerender = false` since a preview link is a bearer credential. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:385` (renders "through the site's own public composition"), `:414-415,442-443` (throws unless `export const prerender = false`: "A preview link is a bearer credential; prerendering it would ship a token in a static asset"). [verified]
- `f:xssf06` `PreviewBanner` exposes five CSS custom properties for palette override (`--cairn-preview-bg`, `--cairn-preview-fg`, `--cairn-preview-border`, `--cairn-preview-link`, `--cairn-preview-radius`), read via `var(--x, <token fallback>)` with no scoped-element declaration, so a site's own `:root`/`[data-theme]` declaration always wins with no specificity fight. The fallbacks are daisyUI 5 role tokens (draft: `--color-base-200` ground, `--color-warning` border, no link; ended: `--color-base-100`, `--color-info`, `--cairn-info-ink` link with a `cairn-focus-ring`-language focus outline; text `--color-base-content`), with no color literal and no `prefers-color-scheme` block, so the banner follows a daisyUI site's own theme in every scheme; a site without daisyUI sets the five properties itself. Source: `src/lib/public/PreviewBanner.svelte:22-40,85-127` (five custom properties, `var(--x, <fallback>)`, no scoped-element declaration). [verified]
- `f:afkvsb` `loadPreview` reads no cookie and touches neither `locals.cairnEditor` nor `locals.cairnAccess`; the token alone is the credential, so anyone holding the link can view the draft with no sign-in. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts` `loadPreview`'s doc comment: "No cookie is read or set, and `locals.cairnEditor`/`locals.cairnAccess` are never read: the token itself is the sole credential, an unauthenticated read on one entry". [verified]
- `f:zke3iw` The preview response sets `cache-control: private, no-store`, `x-robots-tag: noindex, nofollow`, and locked-down `referrer-policy`/`x-frame-options`, on every response including refusals. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:248-257` `PREVIEW_HEADERS` (`'x-robots-tag': 'noindex, nofollow'`, `'cache-control': 'private, no-store'`, `'referrer-policy': 'no-referrer'`, `'x-content-type-options': 'nosniff'`, `'x-frame-options': 'DENY'`), set via `event.setHeaders(PREVIEW_HEADERS)` at the top of `loadPreview` before any refusal branch runs. [verified]
- `f:l4waby` While a pending branch exists, a preview renders with `preview.state: 'draft'`; once the branch is gone (publish and discard both delete it) and a live file exists on the default branch, `loadPreview` returns `state: 'published'` with the live permalink, and `PreviewBanner` renders that state as "This preview has ended." with a link to the published page; the banner never claims the draft went live, since a discarded edit of a live entry reaches the same state. Every other failure (malformed token, expired row, concept or id mismatch, gone branch with nothing published) answers an identical uninformative 404. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:262,460-462,480-511` (`state: 'draft'` while the branch resolves, `state: 'published'` with `published: { permalink }` once the branch read misses but the default-branch entry exists; `rejectPreview` throws the identical `error(404, NOT_FOUND_MESSAGE)` for every refusal reason), `src/lib/public/PreviewBanner.svelte:8-13,74-80`. [verified]
- `f:pimnjy` `previewMintAction` refuses with `fail(400)` when the entry carries no pending draft to share; `previewRevokeAction` deletes every outstanding link for the entry in one call and is idempotent (revoking nothing still succeeds with a zero count). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-preview.ts:13-27,55-83` (`previewMintAction`'s `no-draft` case answers `fail(400, ...)`), `:111-135` (`previewRevokeAction` returns `{ count: result.count }` with no special case for zero). [verified]
- `f:a7yrhq` Deleting an entry, renaming it, and discarding a never-published entry each clear its outstanding preview rows in their own cascade, closing an id-reuse gap where a stale link could resolve to a different entry later; publishing, and discarding an edit of an already-live entry, deliberately leave the rows in place so `loadPreview` can answer a now-stale link with the ended page instead of a bare 404. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-destructive.ts:165-167,203-205,404-407`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-entry-write.ts:407-410,531-543`. [verified]
- `f:cnz554` A parent layout load runs on every route beneath it, the preview route included, and SvelteKit makes data returned from a layout load available to the child layouts and pages it wraps, so a privileged value a group layout returns reaches an anonymous preview holder exactly as it reaches a public visitor, since the preview route reads no session; the load event carries `route.id` to branch on, and a route group's layout applies only to the routes inside its own group, so a preview route mounted in a sibling group never runs that layout. Source: https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/load#Layout-data, https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/load#Using-URL-data-route, https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/advanced-routing#Advanced-layouts-(group), `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:396-399`. [external: SvelteKit (layout load data, route.id, and route groups are framework behavior; the mitigations are site-authoring practices, not an engine mechanism)]
- `f:btx359` `PreviewBanner` is exported on the `./public` subpath because it renders on a site's public preview route; the removed `./components` subpath no longer carries it. Source: `package.json:113-117`, `src/lib/public/index.ts:1-6`. [verified]
- `f:6c3amx` The scaffold's preview mount is `src/routes/(site)/preview/[token]/+page.server.ts`, inside the `(site)` layout group that carries the theme stylesheets and chrome: it calls `loadPreview(runtime, publicRoutesConfig, event)` with the same `publicRoutesConfig` object the entry catch-all builds `createPublicRoutes` from, so the two cannot drift, and it exports `prerender = false`, which `loadPreview` also enforces by throwing a build-time error when it runs during a build. `loadPreview` takes the composed runtime, the site's `PublicRoutesConfig`, and the request event. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/preview/[token]/+page.server.ts:1-21`, `templates/waymark/src/chassis/public-routes.ts:12`, `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:383-387,431-443`. [verified]
- `f:6hmgmv` The scaffold's preview page renders `PreviewBanner` from `@glw907/cairn-cms/public` with `preview={data.preview}` above the same `ArticleView` the public route renders, passing `preview` so that view suppresses the canonical link, `og:url`, and the raw-markdown twin link. Source: `templates/waymark/src/routes/(site)/preview/[token]/+page.svelte:1-15`. [verified]
- `f:6rao75` The design record rejected a neutral-shell preview handler because component-heavy drafts render as a broken-looking page without the site's stylesheet, and chose a site-mounted preview page inside the same layout group as the entry pages, since the stylesheets and chrome live on that layout chain and mounting outside the group reproduces the unstyled page. Source: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-history-revert-preview-design.md:126-147`. [verified]
- `f:6vg3gb` A site without daisyUI must set all five `--cairn-preview-*` properties itself because no literal fallback stands in for a missing token. Source: `src/lib/public/PreviewBanner.svelte:85-100`. [rejected: the four color properties fall back to daisyUI role tokens with no literal, so a site without daisyUI sets those, but `--cairn-preview-radius` falls back to `var(--radius-box, 0.5rem)` and the banner's font is a literal system stack, so the radius needs no setting]
- `f:74ls5e` Each `--cairn-preview-*` property replaces the default for the whole banner: `--cairn-preview-bg` and `--cairn-preview-border` override both the draft state's and the ended state's own fallbacks, `--cairn-preview-fg` sets the text, and `--cairn-preview-link` reaches only the ended state's link, since the draft state renders none. A site sets them on `:root` or any ancestor, pointing them at its own tokens so the banner keeps following the site's theme. Source: `src/lib/public/PreviewBanner.svelte:37-44,92-127`. [verified]
- `f:76m2vf` A mint returns the absolute link `<configured origin>/preview/<token>` and its expiry in epoch milliseconds, built from the configured origin and never the request's host header; the link's lifetime defaults to seven days and a site-set `ttlMs` must fall between one minute and thirty days. The link's loader reads no session (`f:afkvsb`) and renders the pending draft through the site's composition (`f:gvim4v`). Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes-preview.ts:93-96`, `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts:61-82,156-165`. [verified]
- `f:7e0i53` `previewMintAction` and `previewRevokeAction` are the entry-scoped actions that `createCairnAdmin`'s single-mount actions record wires and that the edit screen's Share preview panel posts to; `createContentRoutes` also returns both, for a site that mounts content routes by hand. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/cairn-admin.ts:302-304`, `src/lib/sveltekit/content-routes.ts:98-99`, `src/lib/admin/ShareLinkPanel.svelte:54-58,81,151`. [verified]

## docs/extend/sign-in-through-your-organization.md

- `f:v5ndhs` Configuring `createAuthGuard`'s `identity` option replaces the whole magic-link path; there is no partial adoption where some editors use magic links and others go through the gate. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:294-302` (same `identity` branch as `f:fhit7f`, runs in place of magic-link resolution on every non-public admin path, with no partial mode). [verified]
- `f:gs23fb` Every Access application carries its own AUD tag, a property separate from the application's id (the API's application object lists both `id` and `aud`); the token's `aud` claim names the application the token is valid for, so a verifier validates against the AUD tag, never the id; the tag is copied from the application's Additional settings and never changes unless the application is deleted or recreated. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/validating-json/, https://developers.cloudflare.com/api/resources/zero_trust/subresources/access/subresources/applications/methods/get/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:g0206o` Before enabling `identity`, every roster row's email must match the primary address the IdP will assert (not an alias), or that editor is refused as unknown the moment the gate goes live; a mismatch is fixed from the roster screen or with `wrangler d1 execute` against `AUTH_DB`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:329-345` (a resolved email is trimmed, lowercased, and looked up in the roster with no alias step; no row renders the unknown-identity page), `src/lib/auth/store.ts#normalizeEmail`. [verified: the exact-match lookup is code, which is why an alias would not match; the `wrangler d1 execute` route is operational advice]
- `f:emrebl` Under `identity`, the only join between the gate and the roster is the email string the resolver returns: the guard checks that `resolved.email` is a non-empty string, trims and lowercases it, and looks it up in the roster, with no cross-check of its own, so cairn cannot tell a directory-asserted address from a self-asserted one. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:308-347` (the identity branch and its `findEditor` lookup), `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#IdentityResolver`. [verified]
- `f:k40l86` The Access application and the cairn roster are two independent admission lists that never reconcile automatically: the application decides who reaches `/admin` at all, the roster decides who among those may edit. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:294-302` (the guard's roster lookup against the identity gate's proven email, already `f:fhit7f`); the Access application's own admission decision is external Cloudflare configuration outside cairn's code. [verified: the roster-independent half traces to code; the Access-application half is external Cloudflare configuration]
- `f:4olp4u` The free Cloudflare Zero Trust plan caps the number of users, counted as editors who authenticate through Access rather than by the size of cairn's own roster, so a larger editorial team needs a paid plan; the cap itself is Cloudflare's figure on its plans page. Source: https://www.cloudflare.com/plans/zero-trust-services/. [vendor: link, not a repo fact]
- `f:qhmydf` The first owner must be seeded before enabling `identity`, since nothing in identity mode can bootstrap an owner (the guard never reaches the routes' bootstrap config); seed via `create-cairn-site`'s own method or `wrangler d1 execute` against `AUTH_DB`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:296-302` (the `identity` branch never reaches `auth-routes.ts`), `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:45,202-204` (`bootstrapOwner` lives only in the magic-link routes). [verified]
- `f:zt0oag` No Cloudflare cache rule may match `/admin` under an identity gate: a cache rule can tell Cloudflare to ignore the origin's `Cache-Control` and cache the response for a set TTL, which would override the engine's own `private, no-store` on every admin response and let one editor's cached page be served to the next matching request. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cache/how-to/cache-rules/settings/ (Edge TTL, "Ignore cache-control header and use this TTL"), `src/lib/sveltekit/admin-response.ts#applySecurityHeaders` (the engine's `Cache-Control: private, no-store`), read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare (the override is Cloudflare's; that a cached copy reaches the next matching request follows from cache-key semantics)]
- `f:vnm1p5` The Access application must cover `/admin`, every path beneath it, and `/admin/__data.json` (SvelteKit's data-only fetch); it must not cover `/preview/<token>`, since a preview link's reviewer has no reason to be admitted to `/admin`; Access resolves overlapping application paths from the most specific path outward. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/policies/app-paths/ (wildcards and policy inheritance: the more specific path takes precedence), `src/lib/sveltekit/preview.ts` (the preview route reads a preview token, not a session), read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare (path coverage and precedence are Cloudflare's; the `/preview/<token>` exclusion is a cairn seam-design recommendation resting on the preview loader's no-session behavior)]
- `f:0gltjq` Access application CORS settings must stay off, since enabling them can make the `X-Cairn-CSRF` header settable cross-origin, collapsing the CSRF witness the admin guard relies on. Source: header name confirmed at `src/lib/admin/client-ingest.ts:237` and other action call sites (`'X-Cairn-CSRF': opts.csrf`); Cloudflare Access's CORS settings, which answer preflights and set allowed headers, are documented at https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/cors/. [verified: the `X-Cairn-CSRF` header this warning protects is real cairn wiring, though the Access CORS mechanic itself is an external Cloudflare platform behavior]
- `f:51uyqr` Setting `workers_dev: false` alone does not close a preview-URL exposure: Wrangler leaves an existing Version URL setting alone when `preview_urls` is omitted, and when none exists it follows `workers_dev`'s value, so a site that set `preview_urls: true` or toggled Version URLs on in the dashboard still serves `/admin` on an ungated `<alias>-<name>.<subdomain>.workers.dev` hostname unless `preview_urls: false` is also set; an Access application can also be configured to gate those hostnames directly. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/configuration/previews/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare (the older-Wrangler dashboard case was not re-checked)]
- `f:iaqcq6` The npm-era doctor's `--probe` flag and its two arms (a login probe under `identity`, and a `workers.dev` exposure check) retired with the rest of that bin; no tool checks either arm until a later 1.x release of the `cairn` CLI. A site checks both by hand: an unauthenticated `GET` of the primary hostname's `/admin/login`, confirming a redirect lands on the gate's own hostname, and the same `GET` against `<worker-name>.<subdomain>.workers.dev/admin` and the preview alias, watching for any Worker-served response there. Source: absence of any `workers_dev`, `preview_urls`, or login-probe check across every file under `tool/internal/doctor/` and `tool/cmd/cairn/doctor.go` (confirmed by grep); `tool/internal/spine/condition.go:43` declares `admin.login-probe-failed`, but no check emits it. [verified: an absence proof, confirmed by grep across the current Go doctor's check set; "a later 1.x release" is roadmap intent, not code]
- `f:lyaf6p` The verifier code is application code the site writes and owns; cairn ships no OIDC client and does not depend on `jose`, so nothing in cairn typechecks or verifies the recipe's logic. Source: `package.json` (grep for `jose` in dependencies/devDependencies/peerDependencies finds no entry). [verified]
- `f:42fdqq` An Access-token verifier should read the `Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion` header, not the `CF_Authorization` cookie: Cloudflare recommends validating the header because the cookie is not guaranteed to be passed, and the cookie is a browser-attached value that can be set by hand. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/validating-json/ ("We recommend validating the Cf-Access-Jwt-Assertion header instead of the CF_Authorization cookie, since the cookie is not guaranteed to be passed"), read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare (the by-hand risk is reasoning on top of that recommendation; the verifier is site code cairn does not ship)]
- `f:zas1dk` The verifier must check `payload.type === 'app'` (an application token, not the `org` global session token) and refuse a token with no `email` claim: Cloudflare documents `type` as `app` or `org`, and a service-token request's token carries `common_name` with an empty `sub` and no email. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/application-token/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:q0icwk` On Cloudflare Access, a user-initiated logout clears the browser's authorization cookie immediately and previously issued tokens stop being accepted in 20 to 30 seconds; an administrator's manual token revocation locks the user out for up to a minute. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/access-settings/session-management/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:3p8yu8` `identity` takes any `IdentityResolver` (a `resolve` function plus a `logoutUrl`); the seam is not Cloudflare-specific, so a different reverse proxy can implement the same interface. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:71-87` `export interface IdentityResolver { resolve(event: CairnEvent): Promise<ResolvedIdentity | IdentityRefusal>; logoutUrl: string; label?: string; }`, a plain interface with no Cloudflare-specific type in its signature. [verified]
- `f:n1om0r` `BackendProvider` (documented in the core reference) is the seam for a content backend other than GitHub; `createGithubApp(...)` is the one provider cairn ships today, and none ships besides it. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts:78,88,162` (`BackendProvider`, `createGithubApp` the sole factory), `src/lib/index.ts:127-128` (the only `BackendProvider`-producing export). [verified]
- `f:agif8l` Cloudflare Access is a self-hosted application layer in front of a Worker: only users who match the application's policies reach it, an application connects to an identity provider, Google Workspace and Microsoft Entra ID each have a dedicated connector, and Cloudflare's own one-time PIN is a separate login method. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/self-hosted-public-app/, https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/google-workspace/, https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/entra-id/, https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/one-time-pin/, read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:waqteu` The AUD tag is found on the Access application's Overview tab. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/validating-json/, read 2026-09-29. [rejected: Cloudflare's docs copy the tag from the application's Additional settings, and dashboard tab names drift]
- `f:0t56uj` The Access token's `type` claim is observed on real tokens but is not in Cloudflare's published field list. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/application-token/, read 2026-09-29. [rejected: Cloudflare's Application token page documents `type`, `app` for an application token and `org` for a global session token]
- `f:hilyos` `ResolvedIdentity` carries an email and an optional advisory `displayName`, never a role: the guard takes the role from the roster row and uses the resolver's display name (capped at 120 characters) only when the roster's is empty; a proven email with no roster row is refused with the unknown-identity page and logged as `auth.identity.unknown` with the normalized email, and adding the row lets the very next request succeed. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#ResolvedIdentity`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:329-359`. [verified]
- `f:fu4uis` The guard logs an identity refusal at error when its reason is `audience`, `issuer`, `keys`, or `error` (a misconfigured gate that would refuse the whole roster) and at warn for any other reason (`missing`, `invalid`, `expired`, `no_email`, or a site's own word); a resolver that throws is logged at error, and `auth.identity.unknown` is a separate warn event for a proven identity the roster does not recognize. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:112-115,294-347`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#IdentityRefusal`. [verified]
- `f:ig5pn3` The shell's logout posts to the bare `/admin`, a guarded path, so under `identity` the guard must resolve an identity before `logoutAction` runs: a stale tab whose gate session already ended is refused with the identity-unresolved page. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:429-437` (the comment that the action posts to the bare `/admin`), `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:296`. [verified]
- `f:eiaqkh` The showcase's `hooks.server.ts` swaps its dev backend in behind the build-time `__CAIRN_DEV_BUILD__` define and an opt-in, importing `devBackendHandle` dynamically, so a default build never carries the alternate handle. Source: `examples/showcase/src/hooks.server.ts:12-21`. [verified]
- `f:lbetsq` The magic-link path sends through Cloudflare Email Sending (`cloudflareSend`) unless `CairnAdminConfig.auth.send` supplies a `SendMagicLink` of the site's own, which receives the same built magic-link message the built-in sender does; `SendMagicLink` is exported from `/sveltekit`. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:160-165,247`, `src/lib/sveltekit/cairn-admin.ts:110`, `src/lib/sveltekit/index.ts:111`. [verified]
- `f:gcd8h7` A `BackendProvider` carries a `kind` tag and the default `branch` and connects with `connect(env)`, which returns a live `Backend` for a route that needs one; `createGithubApp(...)` is the one provider cairn ships, returning a provider whose `connect` mints and caches the installation token lazily. Source: `src/lib/github/backend.ts#BackendProvider`, `src/lib/github/backend.ts#createGithubApp`. [verified]
- `f:fe0dyh` Cloudflare's documented origin-side validation of an Access application token is stateless: it checks the signature against the team's `/cdn-cgi/access/certs` keys plus the `iss` and `aud` claims, and the token carries its own `exp`; the documented procedure has no revocation step, so a revocation (a user's logout, a per-user revoke, or an application's revoke of existing tokens) takes effect only where Access sits in the request path, and a token issued before it keeps verifying at an origin that checks it this way until its `exp`. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/validating-json/ (the signing keys and the `jwtVerify` sample with `issuer` and `audience`), https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/access-settings/session-management/ (the revocation paths, after which tokens stop being accepted by Access), https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/application-token/ (the `exp` claim), read 2026-09-29. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:dwc4kp` `createAuthGuard` returns a plain SvelteKit `Handle` whether or not `identity` is set, so an identity-mode guard composes with a site's other handles through SvelteKit's `sequence` exactly as the zero-config guard does. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:169` (`export function createAuthGuard(config: AuthGuardConfig = {}): Handle`), https://svelte.dev/docs/kit/@sveltejs-kit-hooks (`sequence` chains multiple `handle` calls), read 2026-09-29. [verified]
- `f:4uqi5r` Under `identity` the guard awaits `identity.resolve(event)` on every non-public `/admin` request and keeps no session that caches a verified identity, so whatever verification a resolver performs (a JWT signature check, in the Access recipe) runs once per request, including a request that reaches the Worker without passing the gate; `resolveRateLimit`, exported from `@glw907/cairn-cms/cloudflare`, checks one or several keys against a Workers `RateLimit` binding as best-effort back pressure a site can put in front of that work. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:296,316` (the identity branch and its `resolve` call), `src/lib/cloudflare/rate-limit.ts:30-33` (doc comment: "best-effort back pressure, never an authoritative security control"), `src/lib/cloudflare/index.ts:7`. [verified]
- `f:33upyd` A gate login method whose email the signing-in user controls lets that user sign in to cairn as any roster address they choose to assert: Access takes a user's email from the claim the identity provider returns (`email` by default, or a configured Email claim), and the guard admits whatever email the resolver hands back once it matches a roster row, with no cross-check of how the address was proven. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts:316,335-336` (the resolved email is normalized and looked up with `findEditor`, nothing else), https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/integrations/identity-providers/generic-oidc/ (the Email claim setting), read 2026-09-29. [verified]
- `f:s9s8mw` Cloudflare's Workers sample for validating an Access token sets `TEAM_DOMAIN` to `https://<your-team-name>.cloudflareaccess.com`, builds a key set with `jose.createRemoteJWKSet` over the URL `${TEAM_DOMAIN}/cdn-cgi/access/certs`, reads the token from the `cf-access-jwt-assertion` request header, and calls `jose.jwtVerify(token, JWKS, { issuer: TEAM_DOMAIN, audience: AUD })`, where `AUD` is the application's AUD tag. Source: https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/access-controls/applications/http-apps/authorization-cookie/validating-json/, read 2026-09-30. [external: Cloudflare]
- `f:ojr3qm` `IdentityResolver.label` is optional and defaults to "your organization's sign-in"; the guard snapshots it at construction and renders it (HTML-escaped) as the gate's name on the identity-unresolved page, the unknown-identity page, and the login route's hand-off shape, which under `identity` returns only `{ identity: { label } }` in place of the magic-link form. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#IdentityResolver`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#createAuthGuard` (`label: identity.label ?? "your organization's sign-in"`), `src/lib/sveltekit/condition-response.ts:67-70`, `src/lib/sveltekit/identity-unresolved-page.ts:12`, `src/lib/sveltekit/identity-unknown-page.ts:14`, `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:275`. [verified]
- `f:pwmybh` `createAuthGuard` validates `identity.logoutUrl` once, at construction, and throws `cairn: identity.logoutUrl is not a safe redirect target` unless it is a root-relative path with no backslash, control character, or whitespace (checked again after percent-decoding, so `/%2f%2fevil.example` is refused) or an absolute URL whose protocol is exactly `https:`; the shell's logout then redirects to that value. Source: `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#validateLogoutUrl`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#isSafeLogoutUrl`, `src/lib/sveltekit/guard.ts#createAuthGuard` (`if (identity) validateLogoutUrl(identity.logoutUrl)`), `src/lib/sveltekit/auth-routes.ts:476-484`. [verified]

## docs/extend/what-the-scaffold-wrote.md

- `f:mrv24k` The scaffold's `check:cairn` script (`npm run build:admin-css && cairn-audit`) runs the whole static registry, so it now also runs the three public-scope rules; their findings print under the report as advisories and never change the exit code (`exitCodeFor` returns 1 only for an unsuppressed error-tier finding), and a missing `daisyui` or `tailwindcss` peer fails the run with a message that names the peer and `npm install --save-dev <peer>`. Source: `templates/waymark/package.json:16`, `src/lib/audit/report.ts#exitCodeFor`, `src/lib/audit/peers.ts:47`. [verified]
- `f:jcux9z` The scaffold's `.github/workflows/check.yml` pins `setup-node` to `node-version: 24` (the scaffold ships neither `.nvmrc` nor an `engines` field), matching the Node the setup command requires and the showcase's own workflow. Source: `templates/waymark/.github/workflows/check.yml:16`, `examples/showcase/.github/workflows/check.yml:16`. [verified]
- `f:n2skkz` The scaffold is Waymark, cairn's own reference theme, sourced from the same tree the engine tests itself against (`examples/showcase`), with the Playwright suite, the design-review tooling (the `theme-kit` and `probe-craft` routes), the test routes, and a members-login fixture pruned out by the template's exclude list. Source: `examples/showcase/.cairn-template.json:1-19`, comparing `templates/waymark/` against `examples/showcase/`: showcase has `e2e/` (including `members.spec.ts`), `migrations-members/`, and `src/members/`, none of which exist under `templates/waymark/`. [verified]
- `f:s23sk0` The scaffold's `theme.css` and `site.css` are the theme's own tokens and page styling, layered over two lower sources: the engine's `cairn-public.css` roles (the status inks, the shadow, the focus ring, `--color-muted`, `--color-card-border`), which the chassis `tokens.css` imports right after Tailwind, and the chassis's generic design-scale defaults. Source: `examples/showcase/src/theme/theme.css:90` (`@import "../chassis/tokens.css"`), `examples/showcase/src/chassis/tokens.css:51-52` (the `cairn-public.css` import), `src/lib/public/cairn-public.css:20-60`. - `f:7ozknm` The scaffold writes `.claude/skills/` with four skills: `cairn-admin-screens`, `cairn-consult`, `cairn-extend`, and `cairn-public`. `cairn-public` is the skill for restyling the public side. Source: `templates/waymark/.claude/skills/` (four directories, each with a `SKILL.md`), `templates/waymark/.claude/skills/cairn-public/SKILL.md:1-4` (name and description). [verified]
- `f:6jxd81` The scaffold's `/styleguide` route renders every registered markdown component from the registry (a sample per entry that declares a `preview`, the rest listed by name), plus the type scale and the component recipes. Source: `examples/showcase/src/routes/(site)/styleguide/+page.server.ts:97-109`. [verified]
- `f:gepykz` A `create-cairn-site` tree's `migrations/` carries `0000_auth.sql`, `0003_preview.sql`, and `0004_login_nonce.sql` (no `0001`/`0002` shown in the scaffold tree, unlike this engine repo's own `migrations/` which also has `0001_roles.sql` and `0002_audit.sql`), and this is correct, not a gap: `ls templates/waymark/migrations/` confirms the same three files ship in the actual scaffold template, and this file's own already-verified facts above (`extend.md:59-60`) explain why `0001`/`0002` are absent: `0001_roles.sql` is opt-in, applied only if a site declares a role vocabulary beyond the default `owner`/`editor` pair (the scaffold's own `0000_auth.sql` still carries the `role TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('owner', 'editor'))` constraint `0001_roles.sql` exists to lift, verified identical in `migrations/0000_auth.sql:5` and `templates/waymark/migrations/0000_auth.sql:5`), and `0002_audit.sql` is opt-in, applied only by a site that wires `createD1AuditSink` (its own file header: "Opt-in: only a site that wires `createD1AuditSink`... needs this migration"). Source: `templates/waymark/migrations/` (file listing), `migrations/0000_auth.sql`, `migrations/0001_roles.sql`, `migrations/0002_audit.sql`. [verified]
- `f:n7t4bn` `wrangler.jsonc` binds `AUTH_DB` (D1, auth store), `APP_DB` (D1, second database for the `signups/` example screen), `EMAIL` (Email Sending), `MEDIA_BUCKET` (R2), and `PUBLIC_ORIGIN`.
